# Cele 697 de capabilități Hermes — status cod

[Statusul sprintului](../HERMES_STATUS.md) · [Reguli și livrări](HERMES_SPRINT.md)

ID-urile H001–H697 sunt poziții în inventarul înghețat, validate prin hash. Folosește căutarea paginii pentru nume/ID sau comanda `show` pentru cerința originală completă. «Audit 07.09» înseamnă moștenit, nu verificat din nou. Dovezile legate sunt fișiere inspectate; simpla lor existență nu dovedește întregul flux și nu afirmă că testele au fost rulate în această livrare.

| <a id="h004"></a>H004 | Get shell tab-completion for the command tree | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Bash, zsh și fish primesc completări de comenzi/subcomenzi generate din arborele argparse instalat. Fish parcurge recursiv subparser-ele și tratează literal metadatele, fără apeluri către hub sau executarea CLI-ului la completare. Teste cu fish real acoperă fiecare cale curentă, extensii imbricate, prefixe parțiale, comenzi terminale și substituții ostile; Bash/Zsh rămân identice cu baza. Contractul H004 privește arborele de comenzi, nu completarea opțiunilor ori valorilor argumentelor. Scripturile salvate se regenerează după upgrade. Workdir integration rechecked previously-current evidence against base 837d6d08: the parser adds options without changing the command tree or completion implementation. Existing CLI/completion regression coverage is retained. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Image-turn confirmation review (2026-10-02): the agents/cli/nerva.py diff adds selection flags only to chat image turns, rejects them on text chat, validates optional bounded requirements and forwards explicit flags. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. Nous account collateral review (2026-10-02): compared the complete diff from 7a73bf03. Existing CLI command implementations are unchanged; only auth parser/dispatch registration is additive. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. Existing OAuth handler bodies are unchanged; four separate owner-only Nous routes and documentation are added. All pre-edit evidence hashes matched the prior checkpoint. This preserves the existing verdict without claiming new capability completion; the 266-pass/3-skip integration suite includes existing OAuth, secrets and route gates. | <a id="h042"></a>H042 | Start, check and stop the web UI / headless backend | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Hermes's row is start, check \*\*and stop\*\*, and the stop half is entirely absent: no \`stop\` verb, no pidfile, no ready file. The row cites \`hermes dashboard --status/--stop\` plus backend ready sentinels and a ready file (06-cli-f.md, 16 entries); Nerva has \`nerva status\`, which reads the HTTP surface and so cannot report a hub that is starting or wedged before the port opens, and Ctrl-C. Hermes also signals the conflict as a machine-readable \`BACKEND_PORT_IN_USE\`, where this slice signals it as an exit code and an English sentence — enough for a supervisor, not enough for a caller parsing a status stream. Not delivered either: \`--isolated\`, \`--skip-build\`, \`--no-open\`, the SSH session-token file and owner-nonce, or the interactive auth-setup prompt. \*\*One known limit, in the safe direction:\*\* the probe picks a single address family from the host string while uvicorn binds every address \`getaddrinfo\` returns, so a dual-stack name like \`localhost\` is probed on v4 only and can miss a conflict held on the v6 address — it misses silently, and uvicorn then reports it exactly as it does today. The Windows half of the \`SO_REUSEADDR\` choice is read off asyncio's \`reuse_address = os.name == "posix"\` line and has never been executed here; that test skips off POSIX and the docstring says so. [d1](../serve.py) [d2](../tests/test_h2311_operability.py) [d3](../docs/test-manual/01-environment-and-boot.md) |

| <a id="h117"></a>H117 | Message batching and debounce (treat a burst as one turn) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A burst of messages is one turn. agents/core/channels/batching.py holds the rules. Coalescer (agents/core/channels/batching.py:70) holds turn pieces per (conversation, sender) and merges them in arrival order, joined by newlines, into one turn. Each piece extends the batch window of WINDOW_SECONDS 0.35 (Hermes' _busy_text_debounce_seconds), never past HARD_CAP_SECONDS 1.0 from the first piece (Hermes' hard cap; deadline at agents/core/channels/batching.py:97). A batch of MAX_PARTS 32 pieces flushes at once. JARVIS_INBOUND_BATCH_MS and JARVIS_INBOUND_BATCH_MAX_MS set the window and cap for every channel (configured(), agents/core/channels/batching.py:127); a window of 0 turns batching off. Telegram's poll loop (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:530) hands each update to _handle_update (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:577). It queues what the update reads as: a text fragment, or what an attachment was read as (a photo's description, a voice note's transcript, its caption). So a client-split long message, an album (one update per photo) and a photo followed by its question each become one turn (_queue_turn, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:800). While pieces are held, the loop polls getUpdates with timeout 0 instead of the 25 s long poll (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1157), so the batch flushes when its window closes. A button tap or an observed group message first flushes what was said before it, and stop() waits for the cancelled poll task, then hands what is held to the chat lanes, which get LANE_DRAIN_BUDGET (2 s) to finish (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:346); a failed getUpdates also hands over what is held before its 3 s back-off. A slash command is never merged: what the sender said before it goes first, then the command alone, at once (is_command, agents/core/channels/batching.py:52, CommandRegistry.parse; Telegram's _queue_turn and AsyncBatcher.submit), so /stop after a message still engages the e-stop. The voice mark is keyed by (chat, sender), so another group member's older batch cannot take it, and only the running turn takes it: a reply sent in that turn's context while it runs (_RUNNING_TURN, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:91; _after_reply, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:1053). A send from outside that context never takes it (a digest or an outbound notice sent from outside it, another chat's turn, a task an earlier turn left running). One exception is stated at _RUNNING_TURN: once a turn has added an APScheduler job, a job the scheduler fires in that turn's context before the turn ends, and that sends to the turn's chat (the daily digest, in the owner chat), can take the mark. A service line (an unreadable-attachment notice, a transcript echo, a pairing reply, a reason acknowledgement) is sent with voice=False: it is never spoken and never takes the mark. A reply the decision inbox claims as a rejection's reason (H487) is not batched. The poll loop decides with the side-effect-free decision_reason_pending (AutonomyCoordinator.would_consume_reason_reply); a claimed reply, or any fresh text reply in a chat a reason prompt is still being posted to, first hands over what that chat held and is then handled in the chat's lane, where a reply the inbox turns down, or one whose hook fails, is handled as an ordinary message (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:611-639). Its received_at stamp comes from decision_reason_clock, read once per getUpdates page as the page comes back: never later than when that page came back, moved back by Telegram's date for the reply but never before the previous page came back, and after a page cut short by an error the next page keeps the earlier bound (_page_received and _reason_arrival, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:682, :690). The inbox judges the reason window by that stamp, not by when the lane reaches the reply; with no clock, or a failing one, the reply is judged when it runs. Discord and Slack, whose messages arrive as events, use AsyncBatcher (agents/core/channels/batching.py:145). It flushes each batch from its own timer and delivers batches for one key one at a time, under a per-key lock, in order, with at most MAX_PENDING (16) batches on their way and the caller held past that. Slack uses 1, so its one dispatcher waits on a running turn as before batching and its 128-slot ingress queue stays the only buffer. A key's lock is dropped once nothing holds or waits on it. Discord keys by channel and author (agents/core/channels/discord.py:88); Slack keys by channel, member and thread, and with batching off still returns the handler's answer (agents/core/channels/slack.py:365). A stopping Discord or Slack channel discards held pieces and cancels every batch on its way (a timer, a batch waiting behind its key's running turn, and that running turn); a piece or command still waiting for room or for its key is dropped when it wakes, as Slack's shutdown already discards its undelivered event queue. The merged text is what the gateway receives, so its prompt-injection scan runs on the whole message. The gateway scans it with quarantine.detect_injection_normalized (agents/core/channels/gateway.py:176): the raw text plus copies with whitespace collapsed and format characters stripped, hits unioned, so a payload split across pieces, lines or by a zero-width space is caught. Per-chat order is kept: a flush hands the merged turn over before anything later from that chat is processed. tests/test_h117_message_batching.py (60 cases) covers the coalescer (defaults, order, empty pieces, window, cap, wait on the earliest batch, full batch, off) and the Telegram loop: one poll, across polls within the window, after the window, the hard cap, senders and chats kept apart, a photo and its question, an album, a button tap, an observed message, stop, a full batch, batching off, the voice mark, the environment knobs, and the getUpdates timeout. It also covers AsyncBatcher (window, cap, one at a time, off with its answer, full batch with its timer cancelled, an empty batch, drain and discard, a failing delivery logged), Discord and Slack bursts, their stop, and a split injection payload caught in the merged turn. The per-message tests of the Telegram, Discord and Slack channels run with batching off, since they test each message on its own. tests/test_h117_reason_replies.py (79 cases) covers the reason replies with batching on: an ordinary reply still batched, a claimed one behind what its chat said before it and behind a tap read before it, a declined or failing hook handling it as a message, the page stamp and its bounds (Telegram's date, the previous page, a page cut short, a hostile date, a failing or missing clock) and service lines never spoken; tests/test_h487_telegram_reasons.py (34) and tests/test_telegram_voice_reply.py (32) cover the inbox's side and the voice mark left to the running turn. The review round added stop through stop(), a failing poll, command barriers (Telegram and AsyncBatcher), the group voice mark, a zero-width split, lock pruning, the discard of waiting and running batches, a command waiting at discard, the pending bound, and Slack's shutdown and backpressure. There are 61 mutants: 59 killed and 2 equivalent (returning or breaking on a batch that is already gone; a delay of exactly zero); the review round's 18 mutants: 17 killed and 1 equivalent (collapsing whitespace before detect_injection_normalized, which already scans a collapsed copy). [d1](../agents/core/channels/batching.py) [d2](../agents/core/channels/telegram.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/discord.py) |

| <a id="h130"></a>H130 | Serve the UI under a reverse-proxy sub-path | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Același bundle SPA funcționează la rădăcină sau sub un JARVIS_ROOT_PATH validat, cu reverse proxy care elimină prefixul. Bootstrap-ul serverului, navigarea și istoricul, API/SSE/streaming, chunk-urile lazy, fonturile, manifestul și service worker-ul folosesc prefixul; cache-urile PWA sunt separate per prefix. Paginile /map și /mission-control legate din SPA sunt adaptate. Teste prin aplicația reală și proxy HTTP verifică reload, shortcut-uri, cache offline, auth pe POST și lipsa cererilor la rădăcina greșită; coliziunile /v2 și /api au regresii. Sub prefix, /metrics păstrează eticheta de rută (middleware-ul de prefix întoarce scope\["route"\] către golden-signals, la fiecare mesaj trimis și la eroare), iar căile nerutate rămân \<unmatched\>. Contractul este pentru browser cu proxy prefix-stripping: wrapper-ul nativ cere prefix gol, /v1 și proxy-urile care păstrează prefixul nu sunt incluse. Fixture-ul dezactivează lifespan/provider startup; nu se pretinde deployment sau probă cu provider live. [d1](../agents/core/web_base_path.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/swarm.py) |

| <a id="h135"></a>H135 | Appearance preference follows the owner across devices | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Browser HUD, ambient and responsive phone layouts synchronize fixed appearance preferences through a narrow user-guarded Settings DB document. Boot reads never write defaults; stale known values coerce to supported choices. Prefix-scoped immutable edit journals preserve offline changes across tabs, server revision CAS prevents stale writes without Web Locks, and acknowledgements remove exact edits even after unmount. Motion defaults to the OS System preference. Two independent real browser contexts recover saved choices after cache loss. Five fixed local UI font choices now share that normalization, revision and offline journal contract. The HUD applies the body/display override at its root; code and explicitly monospace elements retain their independent font stack. Real root/prefix browser tests verify computed families, persistence in a fresh browser, look independence, offline replay and code font invariance. The separate native application now reads the same owner Settings DB document at boot and foreground entry. Its connection-scoped finite cache, explicit-save hydration epochs and serialized current-record writes prevent stale credentials or appearance from returning. All 19 native consumers react without resetting local component state; code fonts remain independent. The accepted cross-client preference contract is implemented; native authoring and a full curated font catalog are not required by this row. Scope remains one configured owner instance. Unsupported native fonts have explicit platform fallbacks; unavailable storage cannot preserve offline choices across process restarts. Native rendering is covered with real React source and host mocks, not device GUI proof. [d1](../agents/core/appearance.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/preferences.py) [d3](../agents/core/settings_db.py) |

| <a id="h145"></a>H145 | Read the system logs from the UI | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub's own log is readable from the UI. GET /api/admin/logs (agents/core/routers/admin.py:657) is admin-only and read-only; it reads off the event loop and answers a refused filter with 400, its reason and no-store. Behind it, agents/core/log_tail.py reads the file the hub's own setup_logging actually opened, or reports the error it met (FILE_LOG_STATE, agents/core/log.py:19; _writer_state, agents/core/log_tail.py:95), falling back to the configuration for a process that never set logging up ($JARVIS_LOG_FILE, else data_path('logs','jarvis.log') with system.log_to_file: configured_log, agents/core/log_tail.py:86). It lists only that file and its rotations named with ASCII digits, and only regular files by lstat, so a link, a pipe or a directory is never listed (list_files, agents/core/log_tail.py:114; _listed, agents/core/log_tail.py:105); a file is chosen by one of those names, and it is opened without following a link or blocking and checked to be a regular file (_read_window, agents/core/log_tail.py:197). The read is bounded: at most the last 4 MiB, read in one go and closed before parsing, at most 500 records, each at most 8,000 characters (read_path, agents/core/log_tail.py:279). Lines are grouped into records by the log format's header, so a traceback stays with its error: a record over the limit keeps its header and its newest lines behind a "\[… N lines not shown\]" marker, so the error line is always there; lines above the first header read (a record whose header rotated away, a file not in the log format) are records of their own, beyond the window too. Filters: File, a minimum Level (WARNING shows WARNING, ERROR and CRITICAL; a line with no level is not shown under a floor), a Component (a logger and its children) and Lines (1-500), decided on the record as written, so only returned records are redacted. Redaction runs on every returned field, each line whole before anything is cut, so a secret is never split into a visible half: the H495 secret scanner, an assignment rule for names ending like a credential (\`SMTP_PASS=\`, \`?token=\`, \`password:\`), and the known values of the process's secret-named environment variables and encrypted settings, so it masks at least what /api/admin/env masks (_Redactor, agents/core/log_tail.py:159; _known_secret_values, agents/core/log_tail.py:137); when the scanner cannot be loaded nothing is shown, with a note (_redactor, agents/core/log_tail.py:181; _record, agents/core/log_tail.py:251). Terminal escape sequences, C0 and C1 controls and bidirectional overrides are removed (_clean, agents/core/log_tail.py:190). The answer carries the components seen (redacted), the bytes scanned and whether only part of the file was read, and an honest note: file logging off and how to turn it on, a file the hub could not open at start, no file yet, or a file that may no longer be written (read_log, agents/core/log_tail.py:327). The HUD's Logs panel (frontend/src/panels/logs.tsx:54, registered in the Console's Observe group) has the File / Level / Component / Lines filters, colours records by level (ERROR/CRITICAL red, WARNING amber, DEBUG dim), shows them newest first as text, never markup, auto-refreshes every 5 s while switched on and the page is visible, skipping a tick while a read is in flight and stopping on a refused credential, shows LIVE only while the hub writes its file, shows no old records after a refused filter (and goes back to the current log when a rotation is gone), and names the admin token on a 401 or 403. \`nerva logs -n N\` (cmd_logs, agents/cli/nerva.py:1333) reads the same bounded, redacted tail (N counts records), says when only part was read, and refuses cleanly on an unreadable file or a missing redactor. tests/test_log_tail.py (33 cases) covers the resolution and the off state, rotations and refused names, records and tracebacks, the caps, the level floor and component children, the byte budget, a partial last line, bytes that are not UTF-8, control characters, redaction (current file, rotation, traceback line, filtered fields), the route and the CLI; tests/test_h145b_log_review.py (32) covers the first review: a secret across the cut (four offsets, and in a continuation line), a long traceback keeping its error line, links and a link swapped in after listing, a pipe, secret-named environment values and credential assignments (and ordinary numbers left alone), a redactor that cannot load (route and CLI), C1 and bidi controls, filtering before redaction, the writer's error and its path, setup_logging's record, no file yet, plain lines beyond the window, the 4 MiB budget, level-less lines under a floor, component redaction, the CLI's records, window note and -n 0, rotation names, a directory and the 400's no-store; frontend/src/test/logs-panel.test.tsx (14) covers the registration, the query, the admin read, order and colours, each filter, the 5 s auto-refresh, a tick skipped in flight, a hidden page, stale records after a refusal, LIVE only while writing, the off note, no match and a partial read, 401 and 403, a kept component and text-only rendering. The first review (two majors: a secret split by the cut came back in part, and a long traceback lost its error line; ten minors; nine nits) is answered; the feature's 29 mutants were caught but one equivalent, and this round's 26, all but one equivalent (the regular-file check after open, which lseek or read also enforces), two after their cases were added (a pipe, the partial first line). Test manual: PNB-169, PNB-170; the API sweep lists the route. Adapted from Hermes: one log file and its rotations instead of six named files, since the hub writes one log; the level is a floor rather than an exact match. Known limits: a record forged by a multi-line message that contains a header-shaped line is shown as its own record (a property of the log format). Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Image-turn confirmation review (2026-10-02): the agents/cli/nerva.py diff adds selection flags only to chat image turns, rejects them on text chat, validates optional bounded requirements and forwards explicit flags. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. Nous account collateral review (2026-10-02): compared the complete diff from 7a73bf03. Existing CLI command implementations are unchanged; only auth parser/dispatch registration is additive. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. Existing OAuth handler bodies are unchanged; four separate owner-only Nous routes and documentation are added. All pre-edit evidence hashes matched the prior checkpoint. This preserves the existing verdict without claiming new capability completion; the 266-pass/3-skip integration suite includes existing OAuth, secrets and route gates. [d1](../agents/core/log_tail.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/admin.py) [d3](../agents/cli/nerva.py) |

| <a id="h153"></a>H153 | Webhook subscriptions (create, enable, HMAC secret, delete) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 in five rounds. The first round shipped with H200 (critic note 7). After an adversarial review reopened it, the second round added the receiver switch, an event list and a prompt template. After a second review, the third added where a delivery goes (Hermes' Deliver to and deliver only), a description and a receiver switch that fails closed. A third review (review-H153c) found that as shipped, Deliver to reached none of the owner's channels, and the fourth round made a real send reach each offered channel, tested through the real path down to the channel adapter. A fourth review (review-H153d) found that the fourth round's plain-text rendering let one delivery freeze the hub, and rewrote the sender's file names and addresses; the fifth round sends the text as written, bounds every renderer, and fixes the review's minors. A fifth review (review-H153e) found no major; its three minors (inline code spans still quadratic in two renderers, first-read waiters holding worker threads, eight unpinned behaviours) and six nits are fixed. Backend CRUD is admin-guarded, and the store serves every change without a restart: - GET, POST, PATCH and DELETE /api/webhooks (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:175, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:181, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:194, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:221) list, create, update and delete hooks. - A create that carries a field it does not know (Hermes' skills, deliver_chat_id) is refused with 422, never silently dropped (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:87). - WebhookStore.create (agents/core/webhooks.py:486) mints a token and, when signed, an HMAC signing secret. Both are returned once, and every list masks them (agents/core/webhooks.py:635). A save that fails undoes the change in memory. (1) The receiver card (Hermes' platform-level toggle). The setting webhooks.receiver_enabled is a declared toggle, on by default. It is written through the audited PUT /api/admin/settings/webhooks, and the audit row ends with the value (receiver_enabled=false). A reseed is audited too (agents/core/routers/admin.py:455). When the switch is off, the trigger refuses every delivery with 503. It checks before the body is read and again after authentication, so a switch that lands while a sender streams its body stops that delivery (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:239). - It fails closed (agents/core/webhooks.py:251): - only a literal true is on, and a missing row is the declared default; - when the settings store cannot be read, settings_db.read_setting raises instead of answering the default (agents/core/settings_db.py:675), so the last state read or written stays in force; - a receiver never read is refused with "the webhook receiver's state cannot be read". - It is read off the event loop, and by one caller at a time: while a read runs, every other caller gets the last state at once. A caller with no state known yet (the first read after a start) waits for that read, at most two seconds, so a burst that arrives while it runs is answered, and a read slower than that is still refused, closed. The router waits on the event loop (agents/core/webhooks.py:377): who reads is decided on the loop, only the read runs on a worker thread, and a waiter holds none, so a burst on a slow fresh receiver leaves the default executor free (review-H153e MINOR-2). A caller from a thread waits in it, at most four at once (RECEIVER_FIRST_READ_WAITERS, agents/core/webhooks.py:247); any other is refused, closed. A write, a reset or another store wakes the waiters. A state is used for a second before the store is read again. A read that a write, a reset, a reseed or another store overtook while it ran is dropped, a failed one included. A write in this process is seen at once; one from another process (nerva config set) within the second. - The admin routes keep working, and hooks keep their credentials. - The panel's receiver row shows on, off, or "not read" with the reason: - a failed re-read drops the last state instead of showing it; - an answer is shown unless a newer one already is, so an earlier read never overwrites a newer one and a poller slower than its interval still sees each answer (frontend/src/panel-kit.tsx:20); - ↻ re-reads it; - a stored value that is not true or false is shown as off; - a store with no row yet, which the hub answers with its own 404 naming the category, shows the hub's default, "on · not stored", and can be switched off, because the write creates the row (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:197); any other 404 (a proxy's) is a failed read. - Interop and the /v1 panel show every hook as refused while the switch is off. (2) The New-subscription modal: name, description, agent or workflow target, target id, HMAC signing (the secret, shown once), an event list, a prompt template, where the result goes and deliver only. Create is last in the tab order (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:389). - Events: up to 32 printable names of 1 to 64 characters, no comma, stored stripped and kept once each, ignoring ASCII case (agents/core/webhooks.py:137). - The delivery's event comes from the sender's header (X-GitHub-Event, X-Gitlab-Event, X-Event-Key, X-Event-Type, X-Webhook-Event), else from the payload's event or type (agents/core/webhooks.py:121). It is bounded when read but never cut to the 64-character limit, so a longer name matches no subscription. - A hook with a list runs only the deliveries whose event is on it. Every other delivery is answered 202 with the reason, never run, and counted as skipped (agents/core/webhooks.py:605). That includes a delivery that names no event, and a list a hand edit broke (null, not a list, an item that is not a name). - Template: {a.b.0} reads the JSON payload by key and list index, {event} is the event, {payload} the whole body, and {payload.x} a payload key the template reserves (agents/core/webhooks.py:173). - Nothing else is read. A missing value is empty, a string is used as is and anything else as JSON. - The work is bounded by the caps (agents/core/webhooks.py:157): each path is read once per render, a value is encoded only up to 4,000 characters, and substitution stops at 16,000. - A template that renders nothing is skipped, not run. So is an untemplated delivery with no text, which gets its own reason. - A body over 5 MiB is refused with 413 before it is read whole (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:236). (3) Deliver to and deliver only (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:355, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:367). - deliver is log (the default: the reply stays in the sender's response and the session) or one of the owner's own channels that something receives: telegram, voice or ntfy (agents/core/webhooks.py:70). Names are matched exactly. - web is refused by name: nothing in the hub connects a client to the web channel, so a push there would never arrive. /api/channels/targets and nerva send --list say the same: web is not ready while no client is connected (agents/core/channels/outbound.py:88). - The reply, or a workflow's last step, goes through send_to_target and ChannelManager.send to the owner's own destination (Telegram's owner chat). send_to_target writes an IntentLog row per send. The text is labelled "Webhook \<name\> - \<event\>", and each channel carries the label its own way: - ntfy gets it as its title, folded to printable ASCII and cut to 120 characters; - telegram and voice get it on the first line. - The text goes as written: nothing is rendered and nothing is rewritten, so a file name, an address and a Markdown link arrive as the sender wrote them, and a link shows its address. What hides or reorders text is dropped: every format and control character but a newline, a tab, a zero-width joiner and non-joiner (the bidi controls and marks, zero-width spaces, word joiners, invisible operators, the soft hyphen, the BOM, the TAG plane), and the characters that render blank (the Hangul fillers, the grapheme joiner, the Mongolian selectors, the blank braille pattern); a subdivision flag emoji stays (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:313; review-H153e NIT-1). A lone surrogate becomes U+FFFD (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:339). Telegram sends it with no parse mode, no link preview and no voice note, and ntfy unrendered (agents/core/channels/outbound.py:208, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:432, agents/core/channels/ntfy.py:137). Voice speaks it without the markup. A longer text is cut at 4,000 characters with a note that says where the rest is (the session for a reply, nowhere for deliver-only text). - Nothing on the push path renders, so a delivery costs linear time in its text: one long line cannot hold the event loop. The renderers the rest of the hub uses bound every marker span (a link, bold, italic, a heading line), and put inline code spans back in one pass (agents/core/channels/render.py:52; review-H153e MINOR-1), so one long line costs linear time there too, and a span past the bound is left as written (agents/core/channels/render.py:40). - The hook is read again after the turn. If it was deleted or switched off, or the receiver was switched off or cannot be read, nothing is pushed and the hook says why; a hook moved to another destination is pushed there, labelled with its name as it is then. - Quiet hours (ambient.quiet_hours_start/end, 22 to 7 by default, hours taken modulo 24, the jobs' own reading, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:276) keep every push off the phone and the speaker. An hour setting that is not a number (an infinity, NaN, text) keeps its default, for the jobs too (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:82). The push is noted on the hook, not held for the morning as a job's message is. - A hook makes at most 30 push attempts an hour (agents/core/webhooks.py:422, per process). An attempt the channel refused counts, and the panel says so; a push held by quiet hours spends none. A push over it is noted, not sent. - deliver_only skips the agent: the rendered text goes straight to the destination, and no turn runs. It needs a channel: deliver-only with the log is refused on create and on PATCH, with the reason (agents/core/webhooks.py:101). A record from before that rule can still be switched off, and its delivery says the text was dropped. - A workflow delivers its last step's output, or nothing when the run failed or its last step returned an error, never an earlier step's output (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:440). The last step is the last to run, in the pipeline's batch order, not the last listed, and a run that stopped early delivers the last step that ran (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:416). An output the router cannot read is recorded, and nothing is pushed. - Each delivery's outcome (channel, ok, reason, time) is kept on the hook and shown in its detail pane, and the response carries it too. Every exit after a call is counted is recorded, a missing or invalid workflow and a failed turn included (agents/core/webhooks.py:695). - A delivery that fails is recorded and answered 200. Every answer is encodable whatever the sender or a step put in it: a lone surrogate becomes U+FFFD, a NaN or an infinity null, and anything else JSON cannot carry (a date, bytes, a set, an object) its text (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:465), so a delivery is never a 500 after its push. A workflow hook answers with ok, the steps that ran and the delivery, never the run's context, which holds the sender's text and every step's output. A failed turn itself still answers 500, so the sender's retry runs it again. (4) The subscriptions list with per-route enable/disable and delete. PATCH /api/webhooks/{hook_id} (agents/core/webhooks.py:553) takes any of enabled (a strict boolean), events, prompt, deliver, deliver_only and description, and changes them in place. It refuses: - null (and its schema advertises none); - an empty body; - an unknown field; - a destination the create would refuse. The schema names the four destinations as an enum. A hook switched off keeps its token and secret. The trigger refuses it with 403 after authentication, from the live record, before the event filter. Each row has an on/off switch, an HMAC or token badge, an event-count badge, the destination (none for the log) and delete with an inline confirmation. The detail pane shows the description, the calls, the skipped deliveries, where the result goes, how a push goes (on a hook that pushes) and how the last delivery went. It edits all of them and opens at the hook's own destination. After a save focus returns to the hook's row, or to the create form when the hook is gone. The legacy /v1 panel lists, creates and deletes through adminFetch, and shows the receiver as the hub reads it and each destination (agents/web/static/tools.js:441). (5) The restart banners. Nerva needs no restart for either kind of change: the receiver switch is a setting read at most a second old, and hook CRUD is served live. Audit: create, switch, update and delete each write a SecurityEvent (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:143). It names: - the hook id; - the target as a JSON string; - the event list; - the template's length (never its text); - the destination and deliver only; - the signed and enabled flags. It never records the token or secret. A switch alone is recorded as enable or disable; any other change is an update. The receiver switch is audited by the settings route, with its value, and a choice setting's value is in its row (a toggle, a select, a number, a slider). A nerva config set from the shell writes no audit row (H378). Deliveries: an agent-target delivery goes through handle_input(channel="webhook"). origin_for_channel (agents/core/action_origin.py:36) classifies that channel as inbound, so the payload is tainted and the kernel's GRANT-\>QUEUE escalation applies. A workflow target runs its pipeline (agents/core/routers/workflows.py:37) under the inbound action origin. The sender waits for the push as well as the turn. A retried delivery that carries an Idempotency-Key is not run again; it gets the first outcome back (H659, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:552). One without a key runs again. Adapted, not copied: - Deliver to: Hermes offers log, Telegram, Discord, Slack, email and a GitHub comment. Nerva offers the log and the owner's own telegram, voice and ntfy, and refuses the rest by name with a 422 (agents/core/webhooks.py:75): - Email is never an outbound side effect of an inbound delivery (the rule ChannelManager.send keeps). - A GitHub comment writes to someone else's system, so the hook's agent proposes it through the governed write-back path, one approval each. - Discord and Slack are reply-only transports here, with no home channel to address. - The web HUD has no push receiver yet. - Per-subscription skills: a Nerva hook targets an agent or a workflow, and the agent brings its own skills to the turn. Hermes' own modal does not expose skills either. Tests: - tests/test_h153_webhook_subscriptions.py (54 cases) covers the receiver, the event filter, the template and the update. - tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py (35) covers the switch, the audit and the in-flight race. - tests/test_h153b_webhook_delivery.py (63) covers the second round with fakes for the send and the clock. - tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py (80, the fourth round) runs the real path, the router through send_to_target and ChannelManager to a recording adapter per channel. It covers: - each channel (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:104), with and without a turn; - the real quiet-hours rule on a fixed clock (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:273); - the real Telegram adapter's HTTP body (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:229); - fail-soft delivery (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:319); - a switch thrown during the turn (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:455); - the workflow rule (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:534); - the push cap, the single-flight receiver (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:605), and the audit of free text. - tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py (61 cases, the fifth round) covers: - a long line of brackets pushed at once, and the text as written on Telegram and ntfy (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:67); - every renderer in linear time on one long line (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:129); - the hidden characters, the first-read burst (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:151), the workflow answer and the encodable answers (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:253); - the last step as run, the unreadable receiver after the turn, a Telegram refusal, the hour of the allowance, quiet hours and the allowance, the live label, an overtaken failed read, a slider's audited value, the hour settings and web's readiness. - tests/test_h153e_webhook_review.py (49 cases, the sixth round) covers the renderers' scaling on inline code spans, the restore's escaping, an ordinary long link, the waiter cap and wake, a burst through astate holding one thread, every hidden character and what stays, the cut boundary, the quiet-hours end and the encodable answer; frontend/src/test/panel-state.test.tsx (2) a failed poll shown while loading. - frontend/src/test/webhooks-panel.test.tsx has 56 cases, frontend/src/test/use-api-sequence.test.tsx 4 (a poller slower than its interval, loading until the newest answer, an earlier answer never over a newer one, another address), frontend/src/test/interop-live-honesty.test.tsx 14 and the /v1 tests/frontend/tools.test.js 7 (its Webhooks panel cases, of 40 in the file). Mutation: - The fourth round: 92 mutants of its guards and of the reviewer's 35 unpinned survivors (70 backend, 20 panel and Interop, 2 /v1). Three no longer applied to the final code; the reviewer replayed the 89 that apply, and each is caught. - The fifth round: 40 mutants of its guards (32 Python, 8 HUD), each run against its own suites. All 40 are caught, one of them (the answer's dict keys) by a test added for it. Four were first written malformed and were rewritten before they counted. - The fifth review's own 68 mutants left eight unpinned behaviours (five hidden characters, the cut boundary, the quiet-hours end, the link bound); each has a test now. This round's 13 mutants (12 Python, 1 HUD) were all caught. - The fourth review's own 104 mutants left 20 survivors: seven equivalent, one a strengthening (catching an infinite hour, now the code) and twelve unpinned. | <a id="h156"></a>H156 | Edit an agent's persona and description from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | An agent's persona can now be edited in the UI and is live on its next turn. That is Hermes' persona editor, over the version history Nerva already kept. Until now the history was a side record: a commit or rollback in the Prompts panel changed a JSON file while the agent kept its old persona. agents/core/soul_edit.py holds the one path an edit takes, apply_soul (:152). (1) Refusals. An agent the hub has not loaded is refused (404; agent_folder, :100, returns an enumerated folder name in the id alphabet, so no request value reaches a path). So is text over MAX_SOUL_BYTES (256 KiB of UTF-8, :40; 413), safe mode (:171; 409, because overlays are not read there, H275) and a persona the H387 guard would drop (:174; 422 with the verdict). guard_verdict (:60) runs the same scan and cap as Agent._load_soul, and GET /api/agents/{id}/soul now reports it through the same function. A line the guard only quarantines is applied, and the verdict names it. (2) Nothing live is lost. The first edit keeps the file on disk as v1 ("on disk before the first edit"). A file changed on disk since the last version, such as a hand edit, is kept as its own version ("on disk, changed outside the editor") before it is replaced (:185). An agent with no persona file has nothing to keep. (3) Written where the model reads it. The text goes to the owner's overlay (overlay_path, :115): souls/\<id\>/SOUL.local.md in the data home, else the repo-local SOUL.local.md, never the shipped SOUL.md. The write is atomic (_write_atomically, :126: temporary file, fsync, replace; a failure leaves no temporary file and records no new version). (4) Versioned, then live. The text is committed (:195; the same text twice is one version). The agent re-reads its persona (_load_soul, :197), and the result says whether the running agent now reads the written file. (5) Audited without the text. A SETTINGS_CHANGE row names the agent, the version and its hash. A plain commit and a history-only rollback are audited the same way (record_version, :224; agents/core/routers/admin.py:817, :872). Surfaces. PUT /api/admin/agents/{id}/soul {content, message} (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:132, admin). POST /api/admin/prompts/{id}/rollback applies the version through the same path for a loaded agent (agents/core/routers/admin.py:858); the shared contract _identity (served from IDENTITY.local.md) and keys that are no loaded agent keep the history-only rollback. Description. A description: field in the SOUL front-matter is the agent's one-line description (description_of, :84, and description_in, :89: one line, at most 400 characters). It is listed on GET /agents and the SOUL read (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:179, :124) and is never part of the prompt. POST /api/admin/agents/{id}/description/draft (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:150; draft_description, :238) asks the strict-local model for one paragraph from the live persona and saves nothing. With no local model it is a 503, never a cloud call (:255). HUD. The Dossier has an Edit persona button that opens the live SOUL in frontend/src/soul-edit.tsx (SoulEditor, :49, mounted at frontend/src/modes.tsx:96). It offers Preview against the live text, Apply, Draft description with Use it (withDescription, :15, writes the front-matter line), and the hub's refusal in words (refusalText, :32). The Dossier also shows the description. The Prompts panel gets apply beside commit (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2905) and says when a rollback is live. Tests: tests/test_soul_edit_route.py (29) covers the apply, the seed and hand-edit versions, deduplication, blocked and quarantined text, the audit row, size, ids, an unloaded folder, admin, the repo overlay, safe mode, a failed write, no store, a reload that misses the file, rollback (live, to the shipped text, refused, missing, history-only keys, audited), a plain commit, the description (listed, bounded, never prompted) and the draft (a proposal, strict-local, empty). frontend/src/test/soul-edit.test.tsx has 6. tests/test_h10_22_prompt_versioning.py now asserts that the rollback is live, in a throwaway data home. 61 mutants: 58 caught, eight of them after survivors got cases. Two survivors of the first-edit-only seed led to keeping hand edits as versions; that code's own five mutants are caught. The fsync is a crash-only property. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h157"></a>H157 | Edit every configuration key from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Every configuration key is reachable and searchable from the UI, and every one but a secret or a route-only row (settings_db.ROUTE_ONLY: the skill switches, the voice command providers, the security data-handling acknowledgments) is portable and resettable. The backend it builds on: \`GET /api/admin/settings\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:73) returns every category, and the per-category PUT (agents/core/routers/admin.py:194) validates with \`validate_category\` and answers 422 before any write, auditing the changed key names and the new value of a choice only (\`_audited_values\`, agents/core/routers/admin.py:124). New in H157: \`GET /api/admin/settings/export\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:78, registered before the \`/{category}\` read so it is never taken for one) returns the declared settings as a \`nerva-settings/1\` document with an attachment disposition, leaving out every secret (encrypted at rest, or named like a token, secret, password, client id or API key), \`mcp.servers\` (it holds credentials by design) any URL that carries a password (\`http://user:pw@host\`, in a list too), and any free-form value the H495 secret scanner would mask (or every free-form value, when the scanner cannot run), and naming each left-out setting with its reason (export_settings, agents/core/settings_db.py:1064; is_secret_setting, agents/core/settings_db.py:1010; _looks_like_credential, agents/core/settings_db.py:1043; _url_password, agents/core/settings_db.py:1027). \`POST /api/admin/settings/import\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:325) takes such a document (at most 1 MB and 1,000 keys, the body counted as it streams in, so a chunked upload is bounded too: _bounded_body, agents/core/routers/admin.py:312). The import and the reset take only an \`application/json\` request (415 otherwise, a type a browser must pre-check before sending) and refuse one the browser marks cross-site (403), since the admin guard trusts loopback on a default install (_refuse_cross_site_write, agents/core/routers/admin.py:298); every reply is no-store, and only a JSON \`true\` makes a dry run. A NaN, an Infinity or nesting too deep to store is refused (422), never stored, an \`mcp.servers\` entry needs a name (validate_category, agents/core/settings_db.py:870, via _mcp_servers_problem, agents/core/settings_db.py:933; the loader skips a nameless entry too, MCPManager.load_from_config, agents/core/mcp/client.py:814), and 2000 and 2000.0 are the same number, so no change (_same_value, agents/core/settings_db.py:1152). In the document every key must be declared and passes the same \`validate_category\` a single write does, so a posture flag or an out-of-range value is refused like any other; one refusal and nothing is written (422 with every reason) (plan_import, agents/core/settings_db.py:1096). Only the keys whose value differs are written, in one sqlite transaction, all or nothing, secrets encrypted as a single write encrypts them (apply_import, agents/core/settings_db.py:1186), with one audit row naming the settings (and the new value of a choice); \`"dry_run": true\` answers what would change, a secret's values shown as \`(secret)\` and a credential-shaped free value as \`(hidden)\`, so the preview never echoes one (describe_changes, agents/core/settings_db.py:1160). \`POST /api/admin/settings/{category}/reset\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:391) puts one category back to its declared values and names the keys that moved, with an audit row; it keeps the category's secrets and its route-only rows (the skill switches, the voice command providers, the security data-handling acknowledgments) and names them (kept, reset_kept, agents/core/settings_db.py:1202; the HUD counts every kept row as a secret) and names the settings the product posture still forces (overridden, posture_overridden, agents/core/settings_db.py:1211), where the global reseed (agents/core/routers/admin.py:430) was the only reset (reset_category, agents/core/settings_db.py:1306). The v2 console SettingsPanel (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2751; widgets typed by settingsField, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2728) now has a search across every category matching a setting's category.key or label, with a match count and an honest no-match line (settingMatches, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:37); a reset per category heading (only one with declared rows) that asks first, by a second click that names how many settings go back and moves the focus to itself, drops the category's unsaved edits once it runs and names what was kept and what the posture still forces (ResetCategory, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:92); edits hidden by the search are counted beside the save; and a MOVE A CONFIGURATION section (SettingsTransfer, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:187) that downloads the export and names what the hub left out, and imports a pasted or chosen file by showing the hub's dry run (each change old → new) and applying it only on a second step, listing every reason on a refusal; a pasted \`dry_run\` is dropped so the apply step is never a dry run, the text is held while the hub answers, and the file picker is reachable from the keyboard. The standalone /admin app's GlobalConfigPage (agents/web/static/admin.js:447) is unchanged, and \`nerva config list\|get\|set\|check\` (cmd_config, agents/cli/nerva.py:867) still edits single keys offline. tests/test_settings_transfer.py (28 cases) covers the export (every declared key either exported or named as left out, secrets, client ids, mcp.servers, a token-shaped value, a scanner that cannot run, the stored value), the import (an export imports back as no change, valid changes, every refusal a single write makes, nothing written on one refusal, one transaction, the posture flag, a secret encrypted and never echoed, the bound), the per-category reset, and the routes (admin only, the export not read as a category, preview then apply with one audit row, every reason on a refusal, no row when nothing changes, reset and 404); tests/test_h157b_settings_review.py (30, the first review) covers the JSON-only and cross-site refusals, URL passwords, a credential in a list, the hidden preview, NaN, Infinity and deep nesting, nameless MCP servers, number equality, secrets kept on a reset, the posture's settings named, the change listeners, the 1 MB bound for a sized and a chunked body and the JSON-only dry run; frontend/src/test/settings-tools.test.tsx (12) covers the search, the two-step reset, the export note, the dry-run preview and the second-step apply with the admin credential, a refusal's reasons, text that is not JSON, an import that changes nothing, a reset that drops unsaved edits and names kept secrets, edits hidden by the search, a pasted dry run never applied and the keyboard-reachable file button. 25 backend mutants, all caught (one after its case was added: a scanner that cannot run). The first adversarial review (review-H157: 2 major, 7 minor, 12 nit) found that a page the owner visited could post a plain-text import to a loopback hub, and that a URL password was exported; all are fixed, and its 18 mutants were all caught but one equivalent (two after their cases were pinned). Test manual: PNB-171, PNB-172; the API sweep lists the three routes. Adapted from Hermes: no raw YAML editor (the typed widgets and the JSON import cover it, and a free-form editor would bypass the per-key widgets), and export/import are JSON documents rather than a Form/YAML toggle. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. | <a id="h161"></a>H161 | Warn when the box is running out of memory or disk | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The memory/disk warning Hermes shows is here: ranked conditions, only the worst undismissed one on screen, a suspected OOM restart, dismissal per boot, clearing on confirmed recovery, and a watch that does not depend on autonomy. agents/core/resource_pressure.py holds it apart from the autonomy observer, whose thresholds it reuses (85 % elevated and 95 % critical for memory and disk: DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS, :39, pinned to observer.DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS). (1) Ranking. CONDITIONS (:38) orders disk_critical \> memory_critical \> oom_restart_suspected \> disk_elevated \> memory_elevated. PressureMonitor.snapshot (:259) answers every raised condition in that order, with its level, percent, since and whether it is dismissed, plus the worst one not dismissed. Disk covers every watched volume, and the entry names the paths near or over the line. (2) Volumes. take_sample (:81) reads psutil memory and each distinct watched path once (watched_paths, :283: the filesystem root and the data home). A reading that fails is None, never a healthy 0 %, and changes nothing. (3) Hysteresis. Each resource's _Held.update (:109) raises at once. It clears, or steps critical down to elevated, only after RECOVERY_SAMPLES (3, :40) samples in a row under the line by RECOVERY_MARGIN (5 points, :41). A reading just under the line is not counted and restarts the count, so a flapping reading neither clears nor re-raises. (4) Suspected OOM restart. A small state file in the data home (_write_state, :159, atomic through a temporary file, left behind on no failure) holds the boot id (current_boot_id, :69: the kernel's /proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id, else one from psutil's boot time), the memory level, the dismissals and a clean-shutdown mark. start (:172) finds a run on the same boot that ended without the mark while memory was elevated or critical, and raises oom_restart_suspected naming that level. It logs a warning, and says "suspected" because the kernel's log is not read. stop (:191) writes the mark. The web lifespan calls start before the hub serves and stop as the very first teardown step, so a later step that hangs cannot make a clean stop look like a kill (agents/web.py:644, :650). (5) Dismissal. dismiss (:269) takes a condition and this boot's id. It refuses another boot's id, an unknown condition or one not raised. The dismissal persists across a restart on the same boot; a new boot re-arms it, and so does the condition's recovery, because a new episode is a new warning. Unknown names in the file are dropped. (6) Sampling apart from autonomy. SchedulerService.schedule_pressure_monitor (agents/core/scheduler_service.py:419) samples every 60 s on the hub's scheduler, so autonomy off or ESTOP engaged does not stop it. The route samples on demand when the last sample is over a minute old (fresh, :226). Samples from the route's worker thread and the scheduler are folded in one at a time under a lock. (7) Surfaces. GET /api/system/pressure and POST /api/system/pressure/dismiss {condition, boot_id} are both user-guarded (agents/core/routers/pressure.py:30, :36; 409 on a refused dismissal). The HUD's frontend/src/pressure-banner.tsx is mounted under the power chip (frontend/src/app.tsx:527). It polls the route, shows only the worst undismissed condition in one sentence (an alert for critical and a suspected OOM restart, a status for elevated), and dismisses it for this boot. Tests: tests/test_h161_resource_pressure.py (40) and frontend src/test/pressure-banner.test.tsx (6). They cover the ranking, the volumes, the hysteresis (a margin, a near reading, a step-down, an unreadable sample), the dismissal (this boot, stale boot, unknown, re-armed by a new boot and by recovery, persisted), the OOM suspicion and each of its three conditions, the state file (atomic, damaged, unwritable, failed replace, unknown names), the boot id, concurrent samples, the route, the guards, the scheduler job and the lifespan order. 50 mutants: 47 caught after ten survivors got cases. m11 (raise on \> instead of \>=) is equivalent: a same-level reading resets the count on both paths. The since line was simplified rather than kept as an equivalent. m23 (writing in place instead of the atomic replace) is a crash-consistency property that only a crash mid-write shows. | <a id="h165"></a>H165 | In-app documentation | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built on this branch. Hermes has a Documentation page inside its dashboard with an "open in a new tab" affordance. Nerva's written material (the user guide, FLAGS.md on what each posture flag costs, and the privacy and camera-privacy notes) was only in the repository. The Dockerfile copied it into the image, but nothing served it. (1) The route. agents/core/routers/help_docs.py is registered in agents/web.py (agents/web.py:1527) and user-guarded on the router (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:27). - GET /api/help/docs (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:103) lists the allowlist: slug, title, whether the file shipped, and each document's sections. - GET /api/help/docs/{slug} (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:113) returns the Markdown text, capped at MAX_DOC_CHARS with a truncated flag (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:121). - The allowlist is the fixed DOCS table (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:31): user-guide, flags, privacy, camera-privacy. A slug is only ever a key into it, never joined into a path, so an unknown slug, '../' or encoded traversal is 404. - A file must be a regular file under the install root; a symlink is refused (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:85). A file missing from an install reads as "not available", not an error. - Responses are no-store. (2) The page. DocsPanel (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:37) is registered in the console's Start group (frontend/src/console-routes.ts:6), in PANEL_COMPONENTS (frontend/src/gap.tsx:5070) and in the desktop app's route allowlist (desktop/src-tauri/hud-routes.json:49). - It lists the documents, disabling one not shipped, and renders the chosen one through the new shared Markdown component (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:101). - That component emits React elements only (no innerHTML path): headings with anchors, code fences, pipe tables, lists, quotes and rules, plus bold, italic, inline code and links. A link renders only for http(s) or a same-origin path, in a new tab with rel=noopener noreferrer (safeHref, frontend/src/markdown.tsx:25). Raw HTML in a document stays literal text. There is no regex lookbehind, so older Safari can parse the bundle. (3) Open in a new tab. Every document is a standalone deep link, /v2/console/docs?doc=\<slug\>\[#section\] (docHref, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:15). The panel offers it as "open in a new tab ↗" with rel=noopener noreferrer (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:67), and a #section is scrolled into view (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:49). (4) The flag's cost where it is flipped, per the row rationale. - Each document's sections carry the names their headings document. The hub computes them with heading_anchor (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:44), which one shared fixture pins character for character to the renderer's headingAnchor (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:94). - The Settings panel links every setting FLAGS.md documents to its section with a "cost ↗" beside the toggle (sectionIndex, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:20; frontend/src/gap.tsx:2754, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2846). - The security posture panel links to the flags document (frontend/src/gap.tsx:1033). The route/auth/OpenAPI snapshots, the HUD parity rules, the API sweep chapter and frontend/src/api/schema.gen.ts are regenerated. Tests: - tests/test_help_docs_route.py, 25 cases: - the list and each allowlisted document (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:35, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:49); - seven off-list and traversal URLs, all 404 (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:67); - the guard (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:71); - a missing file, the cap and a symlink (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:80, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:88, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:94); - the shared anchor fixture and the section index (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:109, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:114, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:122). - frontend/src/test/markdown.test.tsx: structure, snake_case kept as text, hostile HTML inert, safe links only, no raw-HTML sink and no lookbehind in the source, the same anchor fixture, heading ids. - frontend/src/test/docs-panel.test.tsx: registration, rendering, switching and truncation, deep links to a doc and to a section, and the Settings cost link. When the row was built, all 1,346 vitest cases passed, as did tsc, the e2e typecheck and the build. | <a id="h168"></a>H168 | Shared UI primitives the pages lean on (markdown, destructive-action confirmation, schema-driven fields) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD now has one confirmation for every action that needs one, graded the way the approval queue grades risk, and agent text renders as Markdown wherever it is shown. Both halves of Hermes' confirm-and-render behaviour are covered. (1) One confirmation, graded by RiskTier. frontend/src/confirm.tsx declares RISK_TIER (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:19) with the names and values of agents/core/autonomy/policy.py:26 RiskTier, and tests/test_confirm_tiers.py:22 ties the two lists together. confirmStyle (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:23) gives READ_ONLY one click, REVERSIBLE two steps (the button arms, a second button confirms) and EXTERNAL and IRREVERSIBLE_OR_MONEY a phrase typed exactly (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:143); a tier it does not know takes the most. ConfirmAction (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:74) moves focus to the control that replaced the one pressed. Escape cancels, and a cancelled phrase is not remembered (frontend/src/confirm.tsx:95). Enter confirms only on a match. An action that returns a promise keeps the confirmation open, with both buttons held, until it lands; one that answers false keeps it armed to retry (fire, frontend/src/confirm.tsx:97). A page can open and close it itself (armed/onCancel, frontend/src/confirm.tsx:114, taking focus as it opens, :110) or keep a typed one always open (open: no trigger, no cancel). (2) Every hand-rolled site uses it. The kill switch's HALT ALL stays one click on purpose, because an emergency stop must never wait for a second click; disengage is typed (DISENGAGE, frontend/src/gap.tsx:776, :778). Forget-me types FORGET (frontend/src/gap.tsx:3486) and the acquisition purge its phrase (frontend/src/gap.tsx:4383). Trust-ops rotation types the scope in an always-open confirmation that a refusal leaves filled and a rotation clears (frontend/src/panels/trust-ops.tsx:397). The marketplace uninstall types the folder it deletes, because the rmtree has no undo on this box; a changed folder is typed again, and a refusal keeps it armed (frontend/src/panels/marketplace-admin.tsx:313, confirmRemove :207). The webhook delete is two steps, opened by the row and held while that row's switch is pending (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:420, remove :285). The ESTOP pause is two steps (frontend/src/modes3.tsx:209). tests/test_confirm_tiers.py:26 checks that each of these files imports and uses ConfirmAction and has no window.confirm. (3) Markdown wherever agent text is shown. Chat bubbles, for agent and vision replies (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:78, :95), and decision cards (frontend/src/shell.tsx:144) render through Bubble (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:129). Bubble is memoised on the text, so a streamed token re-parses one bubble. It uses the shared React-only Markdown (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:101): lists, fences, tables and http(s) or same-origin links render, while raw HTML and any other link stay text. Canvas Markdown artifacts go through the same renderer (MarkdownBody, frontend/src/artifacts.tsx:78) and are bounded to MARKDOWN_LIMIT (4,000) code points when shown as well as when saved. A new breaks option (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:171) keeps a single newline as a line break for chat and artifacts, and documents keep paragraph joining. The owner's own turns are shown exactly as typed. Test-manual gap G22 (collapsed newlines, fences as prose) is closed. Tests: frontend/src/test/confirm.test.tsx has 11 tests: the grading, click, two steps, typed and case, busy and disabled, a promise and a refusal, a rejected promise, the controlled and open modes, a cancelled phrase, and inert labels. frontend/src/test/markdown-adoption.test.tsx has 6: the agent, vision, user, decision-card and artifact rendering, line breaks, and the bound cut on a code point. frontend/src/test/markdown.test.tsx gains 1 for breaks. The kill-switch, marketplace, trust-ops and webhooks tests each gain cases, and the purge, marketplace and artifact tests follow the new steps. tests/test_confirm_tiers.py has 2. Of 51 mutants, 48 are caught, eight of them after survivors got new cases. The three survivors were equivalent (the return value of a confirmation the page closes itself, a guard ConfirmAction already enforces, and a pause whose success unmounts the card), and that code was removed. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h182"></a>H182 | Keep the machine awake through a turn, and be aware of power/battery state | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva keeps the machine awake while a turn runs, reads and streams its power state, and eases off on battery, as Hermes does. agents/core/power.py holds one shared power assertion while at least one turn runs (KeepAwake, refcounted). It is off by default and turned on with JARVIS_KEEP_AWAKE=1 (keep_awake_enabled). The mechanism is the platform's own (backend_name, backend_argv): caffeinate -i -w \<hub pid\> on macOS, systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep --mode=block around tail --pid=\<hub pid\> on Linux, so both helpers end with the hub even when it is killed, and SetThreadExecutionState on Windows. The helper is started in its own process group (start_new_session) and released by signalling that group while its leader is still unreaped, so the child systemd-inhibit starts ends too; a helper that already exited is reaped by its handle, and one that ignores SIGTERM is killed after one second. A missing binary or mechanism never fails a turn and is reported (status error), as is a helper that exits on its own. Every turn entry holds it: Orchestrator.handle_input, handle_input_stream and the one-shot process take it at the start (hold_for_turn) and drop it in their finally (release_for_turn), so a reply, an error and a cancel all release it; the lifespan releases every hold at shutdown (agents/web.py) and atexit does at exit. It is gated as host.control (authorize_keep_awake), in the llm_control order: the owner's system-control permission, the host-control contract, whose action list now names power.keep_awake (agents/core/autonomy/remediation.py), an Action Kernel GRANT (the kernel must be on; QUEUE, DENY and anything that is not a GRANT refuse), then a durable audit row (no audit log, or a refused row, refuses too). The action carries the fixed trusted origin, since keep-awake is the owner's switch and not the content of the turn that asks first. It is asked once per process; a refusal keeps it off until the next start and is named in the status. The power state comes from psutil.sensors_battery (read_battery: on battery, percent, plugged). A resume from sleep is seen as the wall clock running more than 60 s ahead of the monotonic clock between two reads (PowerMonitor.tick), which stops while Linux and macOS sleep, and is shown for ten minutes. The scheduler reads it every minute (SchedulerService.schedule_power_monitor, skipped under JARVIS_TESTING), so a resume is noticed without anyone asking. GET /api/power answers the power state, the deferral threshold, whether background work is being deferred and the keep-awake status (enabled, active, holders, backend, refused, error); GET /api/power/stream pushes the same payload to a connected client on every change, with a keepalive while it does not change (agents/core/routers/power.py). Both are user-guarded. Battery-aware throttling: below system.battery_defer_percent (a settings row, 50 by default; 0 never, 100 always on battery) on battery, or on battery with an unknown charge, the heavy background jobs skip their run and return deferred_on_battery, which scheduler health records as skipped: memory maintenance, the tech scout, the learning-loop promotions, prompt evolution and the WorldView KG sync (the last three through SchedulerService wrappers the scheduler now registers). An unreadable power state lets the job run. Nothing the owner asks for directly is deferred. The HUD shell shows a PowerChip (frontend/src/power-chip.tsx): on battery with the charge and whether background jobs wait, a wake from sleep, and keep-awake holding, ready or refused with its reason; a plugged-in machine with keep-awake off shows nothing. It polls GET /api/power every minute and follows the stream where the browser can attach to it. The desktop shell turns webview background throttling off for both windows (desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs, background_throttling), so a HUD in the background keeps reading a streaming reply instead of being suspended and having its turn cancelled; NERVA_DESKTOP_BACKGROUND_THROTTLING=throttle or suspend asks for it back and an unknown value is named and ignored (desktop/src-tauri/src/throttling.rs). macOS 14+ honours the policy; Linux and Windows webviews ignore it. The binding-writer inventory's pinned lines in scheduler_service.py move with the file. Tests: tests/test_h182_power.py (76), including a real helper process whose own child is ended by the release, and rustc unit tests of throttling.rs; src/test/power-chip.test.tsx (6). 100 mutants: 98 killed after five cases were added; the other two were redundant checks, removed. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. [d1](../agents/core/power.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/power.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h209"></a>H209 | Keyboard shortcut customisation with a recorder and conflict detection | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD's keyboard shortcuts live in one registry, as in Hermes. frontend/src/shortcuts.ts defines every action once (ACTIONS: id, category, label, default chord, scope): the ten mode keys, World (w), the Console (\`), Ambient (a), Cinema (m) and its three stages (o, n, b, scope cinema), focus the message box (/), the command palette (mod+k) and the Keyboard Shortcuts panel (mod+/). app.tsx (World's key included: WorldAwareApp passes onWorld and shares the live overrides through useShortcutOverrides) and the cinema overlay in shell.tsx dispatch through matchAction over bindings(overrides) instead of literal key maps, and every key hint (palette, top bar, World, Console, cinema stages) is read from the registry (chordOf, displayChord). A matched action takes the key from the browser (preventDefault); Shift+letter falls back to the bare letter unless it is bound itself. frontend/src/shortcuts-panel.tsx is the panel mod+/ opens: categories Navigation, View, Composer and Session with search (searchBindings); click a chord to record a new one ("Press a key…", Esc cancels, a lone modifier keeps listening; chordFromEvent, rebind); the palette and the panel take only a chord held with Ctrl/⌘ or Alt (acceptsChord), fire in an input only then (firesInInputs), and the palette always has a Keyboard shortcuts entry, so no rebinding can lock the panel out; Space is spelled "space", and a chord with no key is refused on record and on read; Esc closes the panel without closing what lies beneath; per-binding reset and Reset all (resetBinding); an "Also bound to X" note when two actions share a chord (conflictsFor). Overrides persist per viewer in localStorage under hud.shortcuts, validated on read (readOverrides, loadOverrides, saveOverrides), and survive storage that is off. Governance: defineActions refuses any action whose id, category or label names an approval, a decision, an accept, reject, deny or consent (FORBIDDEN_TARGET), so no chord can reach a Decision Inbox action. Tests: frontend/src/test/shortcuts.test.tsx (25), app-routing.test.tsx (+14: panel, rebinding, persistence, World, composer focus, the Space and Ctrl+Space lockout, the palette entry, the typing guard, preventDefault, World behind the panel, storage off, live hints, Esc beneath, Cinema), cinema.test.tsx (+1: rebound stage keys); voice-orb and briefing-wall expect the registry's stage titles; 38 mutants, 36 killed, 2 equivalent; the review round's 19 mutants, all killed (4 by tests added after a first pass). | <a id="h222"></a>H222 | Always-visible microphone / wake-word state indicator | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva shows whether it is listening outside its main window, as Hermes does. agents/core/voice/listening.py keeps one process-wide, thread-safe listening state per source (ListeningState, STATE). The host's own voice path publishes it: the wake-word detector marks hub armed once it holds the mic open and off when it stops (WakeWordDetector.start, stop; agents/core/voice/wake_word.py), and a capture marks hub listening while it records, thinking while it transcribes and answers, speaking while it plays the reply, and falls back to armed (or off when the detector is not running) in a finally, so an early end or a failure never leaves it lit (VoicePipeline._capture_and_process; stop marks it off; agents/core/voice/pipeline.py). Wyoming satellites publish it too: an authenticated satellite's detection or voice-started marks satellite:\<id\> listening and voice-stopped marks it thinking; a transcript marks it thinking while it is answered and clears it after; a closed connection clears whatever it lit; with authentication required an unauthenticated peer can set nothing, and with authentication off the one anonymous satellite is named satellite (LISTEN_EVENTS, WyomingServer._listening_source, handle_connection; agents/core/voice/wyoming.py). The API is get / set / on-change in process: set_state (a bounded, printable source name and one of off, armed, listening, thinking, speaking; off forgets the source; at most 32 sources), snapshot (each source's state, the loudest by ORDER — listening, then armed, both an open mic, then thinking, speaking, off — mic_open, and a seq that moves on every change), subscribe and unsubscribe (callbacks run outside the lock and a failing one is ignored). A transient state not re-said for 120 s expires, and the expiry moves seq, so a crashed capture never leaves the indicator on; an armed mic stays. GET /api/voice/listening answers the snapshot and GET /api/voice/listening/stream pushes it on every change with keepalives (agents/core/routers/voice.py); both are user-guarded and GET-only, so nothing can set the state over HTTP. Every writer and reader loads the one agents.core.voice.listening module (a test pins it, since core.X and agents.core.X load as different modules here). The push to the desktop shell: frontend/src/listening.ts follows the stream with the HUD's own token (EventSource cannot send one) and falls back to polling every 5 s; it combines the hub's state with the window's own mic (useVoice active counts as listening), and only inside the desktop app forwards the loudest to the shell (setDesktopListening in frontend/src/desktop.tsx sends only changes and re-sends after a refused call; a closing window sends off). The indicator surface is the tray icon (desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs): a desktop_listening command, allowed only to the local HUD windows (verify, capability allow-desktop-listening, build.rs manifest), records each window's state on a board and shows the loudest (desktop/src-tauri/src/indicator.rs): a menu-bar title while a mic is open (● listening, ○ wake word) — shown on macOS and next to the icon on Linux, not on Windows — and a tooltip naming the state on macOS and Windows. It is read-only: nothing on the tray or in the command can open or close a mic. Tests: tests/test_h222_listening.py (44), including the capture's state trail, every early end, a failing transcription, authenticated, unauthenticated and anonymous satellites and a satellite dropping mid-capture, the stream framing, the routes and guard, the shell's command, permission and capability, and the rustc unit tests of indicator.rs; src/test/listening.test.tsx (8) and src/test/desktop-listening.test.ts (3). 65 mutants: 64 killed after five cases were added; the other was a redundant check, removed. The desktop crate was not compiled here: its tray calls were checked against the Tauri 2.11.5 source. Named speech compatibility review: listening state, GET-only snapshot/stream and mic authority are unchanged; listening regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/listening.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/pipeline.py) [d3](../agents/core/voice/wake_word.py) |
