| <a id="h227"></a>H227 | "What can it do right now" inspector: live status line plus a session panel of tools, skills, resolved system prompt and MCP servers | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Hermes' "what can it do right now" view is here: one read-only answer, for one agent as seen by one principal, built from the code a turn runs and served on three surfaces. agents/core/inspector.py holds it; build_inspector (:108) returns up to five sections (SECTIONS, :63). (1) Views. VIEWS (:56) names the five tool-profile postures: the owner and a guest on the HUD (owner, guest), the owner and a stranger on an external channel (inbound-owner, inbound; Telegram stands for every channel, under the inbound action origin a real channel turn has) and no human (internal). The look binds that principal and origin in a task of its own (:125), so nothing it binds, and nothing the runtime notes while resolving the offer, outlives it. (2) status (_status, :155): version, the backend and model the router serves, the local model's state through project_llm_status, the transcript budget llm.tool_loop_context_tokens (0 reads as 75 % of the model window), the tool loop and safe mode. (3) tools (_tools, :194): the tool runtime's own resolved offer for the agent under that principal (_resolve_offer: registry or the capability registry's view, job toolset, posture, the agent's tools: list, the shared-session rule), not the raw registry. Each row says whether the tool is gated and whether its output is fenced as untrusted, with the description cut to DESCRIPTION_CHARS (160, :65). The withheld names are listed, a resolver failure offers nothing, and loop_enabled says whether the model sees any of them (with the loop off they are what the posture would offer). (4) skills (_skills, :215): the rows prompt_catalog gives the prompt under that principal and that offer, or none when llm.skills_in_prompt is off. (5) mcp (_mcp, :225): each server's transport, trust tier, transport-aware liveness (is_connected, so an HTTP server reads connected; a probe that raises is down) and visible tools, with no URL or command line. (6) system_prompt (_prompt, :253): the agent's system prompt and the user part Agent.build_prompt sends for an empty user message: persona, agent context, the core-memory block, the runtime, language and grounding rails and the skills block. tokens (:266) estimates what that fixed part costs every turn before history. The texts are masked by the log redactor (SecretRedactionFilter; nothing is shown when it cannot load, _redactor :73) and capped together at PROMPT_CAP_BYTES (64 KiB, :64) on a character boundary. The core block is read with freeze=False (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3198, :3258, :3338): the frozen block when a turn froze one, else a fresh render that is not kept, so a look never fixes the day's snapshot. (7) Surfaces. GET /api/admin/inspector?agent=&view=&section= (agents/core/routers/status.py:123, admin-guarded; 404 for an unknown agent, 400 naming the views or sections, 503 before the hub is up). nerva inspect \[agent\] \[--as VIEW\] \[--section tools\|skills\|prompt\|mcp\|status\] \[--json\] (cmd_inspect, agents/cli/nerva.py:1029; render_inspector :1046) prints the same payload section by section, or as it came. The HUD's Inspector panel (frontend/src/panels/inspector.tsx, Console → Admin) reads the same route with a status line, an As switch and folding sections. Tests: tests/test_inspector.py (43) builds a partial orchestrator over the coordinator's real ToolRPC registry. It covers the guest's tools equal to ToolProfileResolver's set and not the registry, every view's posture and bindings, the agent's list, the marks and the cut, the loop off, a failing resolver, an unwired runtime, the skills, the prompt (skills block, core memory, rails, empty message, tokens), the unfrozen core block, masking, the withheld prompt, the 64 KiB cap on a split character, no leaked binding, MCP liveness, the status line and safe mode, section order, the route (payload, defaults, refusals, admin token, guard snapshot, 503) and the CLI (--json equal to the payload, rendering, --section, the loop-off line, the tree, one list of views and sections). frontend/src/panels/inspector.test.tsx has 4. 82 mutants: 79 caught, ten after survivors got cases. One redundant reset was simplified away. The origin passed to classify_turn is equivalent, because each view's principal alone decides the surface. | <a id="h242"></a>H242 | Workspace/project model, repo discovery, strict setup verification and diagnostics bundle | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva copies Hermes's setup.runtime_check into the owner's terminal. The hub resolves the route with the same llm_router.select_backend call a Jarvis turn makes (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:296) and names its verdict: a local provider/model pair must be resident, and a cloud route (Gemini, Claude, or the cloud-compatible adapter set by llm.compatible_provider) counts only when the router handed back the very backend that route names (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:145, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:334-343), and (H380) unless its provider refuses the key that route would use; a provider that says nothing about the key reads unknown, and a 429 stays runnable. A real HybridRouter pins this for the local, cloud-flash, claude and both cloud-compatible paths (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:536). \`nerva doctor\` (and \`python scripts/doctor.py\`) adds the advisory runtime_resolves row: one GET of /api/onboarding/command-center at the hub \`nerva status\` reads (scripts/doctor.py:283), run only after /readyz answered at that same address and otherwise reported as skip, never ok (scripts/doctor.py:432-436). It sends one credential, the user token when both are set and the admin token only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address (scripts/doctor.py:400-407, scripts/doctor.py:353), never prints it, and is ok only on a ready verdict that names the route, provider and model (scripts/doctor.py:483-487). A refused token, a reply cut off mid-body, a listener that does not speak HTTP, a bad URL or an odd resident list is a named warn, not a traceback (scripts/doctor.py:463-468; tests/test_doctor.py:433, tests/test_doctor.py:458). \`nerva status\` now labels the old any-model state 'resident' and adds a runnable: line from the same verdict (agents/cli/nerva.py:629); a timeout or reset on that read shows 'unknown — hub_unreachable' and the rest of the status still prints (agents/cli/nerva.py:639). A reply cut off mid-body or a garbled status line on any hub read is HubUnavailable, so \`nerva status\` reports an unreachable hub instead of a traceback (agents/cli/client.py:181, tests/test_nerva_cli.py:183). Both tools dial a wildcard bind (0.0.0.0, ::) over loopback (agents/cli/client.py:56). | <a id="h247"></a>H247 | Wake word arming per surface, push-to-talk capture, TTS, and liveness/config primitives | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now knows which surface holds each microphone. A surface arms only if the owner allowed its kind; one device's microphone has one holder at a time; and arming, pausing, resuming and stopping carry the surface's identity and are audited. This is the adaptation's missing concept, on top of the voice primitives that already existed (wake word, push-to-talk and hands-free capture, TTS, STT, capabilities, and H222's listening indicator). The lease table is agents/core/voice/mic.py (MicArbiter, agents/core/voice/mic.py:93; one process-wide ARBITER, agents/core/voice/mic.py:232). (1) Surfaces and devices. A surface is kind:client: host:hub (the hub's own wake-word pipeline), hud:\<tab\> or mobile:\<device\> (ARMABLE, agents/core/voice/mic.py:39). A Wyoming satellite has its own microphone and is not armed here. A lease is for one device. host is the machine the hub runs on, and a browser that the route sees on loopback shares it. The route decides that from the address a trusted proxy vouches for (_mic_device, agents/core/routers/voice.py:527), so a LAN client cannot claim to be local. Otherwise a browser gets remote:hud:\<tab\>, and every phone gets mobile:\<device\>. (2) One holder per device. A second surface arming a held device is refused, naming the holder (MicRefused mic_busy, agents/core/voice/mic.py:49; HTTP 409). With take_over, it pauses the holder and tells it (on_change, agents/core/voice/mic.py:144). A pause frees the device (agents/core/voice/mic.py:199). A resume asks again, both for consent and for the device's holder (agents/core/voice/mic.py:212). A stop releases the lease (agents/core/voice/mic.py:221). (3) Consent. A kind arms only if the setting voice.mic_surfaces allows it (allowed_kinds, agents/core/voice/mic.py:59). The setting is a declared tags setting whose values are validated against host, hud and mobile (agents/core/settings_db.py:218, :902). The default is hud and mobile (DEFAULT_ALLOWED, agents/core/voice/mic.py:40): surfaces someone uses on purpose. The hub's always-on listener needs host added. So VoiceChannel.start (agents/core/channels/voice.py:29) opens the host microphone only with a lease. Before this, it started at boot with no check. It stops the pipeline when taken over and starts it again when given back (_on_lease, agents/core/channels/voice.py:48). Withdrawn consent ends a lease at its next renewal. (4) Leases lapse. A browser or phone lease lapses LEASE_SECONDS (45, agents/core/voice/mic.py:42) after its last renewal (_expire_locked, agents/core/voice/mic.py:108). The host pipeline's lease does not lapse. The table holds at most 32 leases. (5) Audit. Every arm, take-over, pause, stop and refusal is one audit row naming the surface and the device. A renewal is not. (6) Routes, all user-guarded. GET /api/voice/mic answers who holds each device, the paused leases and the allowed kinds (agents/core/routers/voice.py:548). POST /api/voice/mic/arm (agents/core/routers/voice.py:557) takes hud or mobile and answers 403 or 409. /pause, /resume and /stop act on a lease by name (agents/core/routers/voice.py:576, agents/core/routers/voice.py:587, agents/core/routers/voice.py:601). (7) The HUD. The voice loop arms its lease before getUserMedia (frontend/src/voice.ts:414; armMic, frontend/src/mic-lease.ts:47) and renews it every 20 s (frontend/src/voice.ts:434). It releases the lease on every end of the loop (frontend/src/voice.ts:135). If a renewal is refused, it stops and says who took the microphone. Only a hub refusal blocks: a hub that cannot answer does not. The loop offers take over (takeOver, frontend/src/voice.ts:454; the voice pill, frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:255). The voice settings list the holders, with pause, resume and stop (MicLeases, frontend/src/mic-leases.tsx:17). Tests: tests/test_h247_mic_lease.py has 27. They cover consent, kinds, client and device names, withdrawn consent, a conflict and a take-over, renewal, lapse, pause and resume, stop, the bound, status, the numbers, the setting and its reading, the voice channel's lease, loopback against LAN against a phone, the routes and their guard. frontend/src/test/mic-lease.test.tsx has 10: arm before the mic, a named holder and take-over, no consent, an unreachable hub, renewal and loss, a cancelled start, an unmount, a click handler, the pill and the settings list. 61 mutants: 60 caught, eleven of them after survivors got cases. One of those survivors exposed a device name that was too long for a 64-character client id; it is fixed. The last was a redundant check, removed. Not in this row: no install path installs a voice engine. The named STT/TTS command-provider change touched only the voice router's provider catalog, intake and selector fields; the mic lease routes and the consent check are unchanged, and both test files above pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/mic.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/voice.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/voice.py) |

| <a id="h259"></a>H259 | Reset to defaults, scoped and previewed, plus export/import of the whole configuration | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A settings reset now shows what it will change, never touches a secret and can be undone, and the HUD marks every setting that differs from its default. Hermes' config-reset and defaults surface is covered, on top of H157's search, secret-free export, dry-run import and per-category reset. (1) Defaults. Every declared row that is not a secret carries its declared default (agents/core/settings_db.py:616, in _mark_overlay :608, used by GET /api/admin/settings and each category read). A secret carries none. The Settings panel marks a row whose value, or staged edit, differs from its default ("changed", with the default in its tooltip); a ↺ button stages the default and the usual save writes it through the validated PUT (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2853). (2) A preview before any reset. plan_reset (agents/core/settings_db.py:1238) computes what a reset of one category, or of every category, would write: each declared setting that is neither a secret nor a route-only row (settings_db.ROUTE_ONLY: the skill switches, the voice command providers, the security data-handling acknowledgments) and whose stored value is not its default. POST /api/admin/settings/{category}/reset and POST /api/admin/settings/reseed take {"dry_run": true} and answer the changes as current → default without writing anything (agents/core/routers/admin.py:391, :430; _dry_run_asked, :379; only a JSON true, in a body of at most 4 KB). The changes use describe_changes, so a credential-shaped value shows as (hidden). Arming the reset in the HUD asks for that preview and lists it, and counts the settings kept (secrets and route-only rows, all labelled secrets) and names the settings the posture still forces (ResetPreview, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:64; ResetCategory, :92). (3) The reseed no longer deletes the store. It used to run DELETE FROM settings, wiping every setting, secrets included, with no preview and no way back. It is now the per-category reset over every category: secrets and route-only rows are kept (and named), rows nothing declares are left, it takes a JSON request from this origin only (415/403 otherwise, as H157's writes), and it is audited with the settings that moved (agents/core/routers/admin.py:430). The v1 admin page sends it as JSON (agents/web/static/admin.js:1520). The HUD gets "reset every category…" with the same preview (ResetAll, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:115). (4) Undo. reset_settings (agents/core/settings_db.py:1277) writes the defaults and records what it replaced in settings_resets (:119) in the same transaction, keeping the last 20 (RESETS_KEPT, :1260). POST /api/admin/settings/undo (agents/core/routers/admin.py:461; undo_last_reset, agents/core/settings_db.py:1332) puts back the latest reset not yet undone. A setting changed since that reset, one its declaration no longer accepts, or one no longer declared is left as it is and named in skipped. The undo is audited, and the listeners hear every value a reset or an undo writes, so the webhook receiver switch follows it. GET /api/admin/settings/resets (agents/core/routers/admin.py:91; list_resets, agents/core/settings_db.py:1319) lists the resets, newest first, naming settings but never their values. The HUD shows "last reset: \<scope\> · N settings · \<time\>" with "undo…" (UndoReset, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:135). Every reset and undo in the HUD is a two-step ConfirmAction at the REVERSIBLE tier (H168). Adapted from the row's ask: the reseed does not go through the approval queue's irreversible tier, because it is no longer irreversible. It keeps secrets, previews, and can be undone. Routing it through the queue would need a new kernel action kind for it, and none is declared (agents/core/kernel/registry.py's one settings kind is H613's settings.voice_command). Tests: tests/test_settings_defaults_undo.py has 24: defaults on rows and none for secrets; the plan (what moves, never a secret); a recorded reset and its undo; nothing moved means nothing recorded; the listeners; a setting changed since, no longer valid or no longer declared left as it is; latest first; the list keeps 20 and names no values; everything reset with secrets and undeclared rows kept; and the routes (admin-only, dry run, a hidden credential, JSON only with a 4 KB bound, the reseed's preview, audit and undo, nothing to undo 404, and /resets not taken for a category). frontend/src/test/settings-reset-undo.test.tsx has 7. The H157 reset test, the H153b reseed test (a JSON body) and the settings row-shape test (default) follow. Of 54 mutants, all 54 are caught, ten of them after survivors got cases. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h273"></a>H273 | Tell the owner where an effective value actually came from | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207, then fixed after eight adversarial reviews. The first found the stdlib parser was python-dotenv's pre-1.2.3 grammar, while the lock pins 1.2.3: the doctor could print value material as key names, a named-pipe .env was no longer loaded, and a data home named in the repo .env was no longer followed. The second found that the doctor sent the admin credential to whatever answered at the hub address, that more keys than serve.py's four were read before any .env was loaded, and that a one-case line of value material could still be printed as a name. The third found that the start itself read 31 more names before the load (so ten .env knobs, the audit key and the public-profile gate among them, were shown as in effect and were not), that two read-again entries were false, and that the MCP transport ignored a user token kept in .env. The fourth found that the MCP transport and the HTTP user guard asked different questions, so a lapsed or revoked user credential locked every route but left MCP open to a local caller; that the coordinator built its Orchestrator before the load, so its audit rows went unkeyed beside the hub's keyed ones; that an IPv6 bind (JARVIS_HOST=::1) escaped both loopback checks; and that the CLI sent the admin token to any hub. The fifth found that the admin tier still trusted a loopback caller once no admin credential was active, so after the owner revoked every credential a local process with no token could mint a fresh user token over HTTP and reopen the user routes and MCP (older than H273, but it falsified this row's credential sentence); that a managed user token that had expired counted as never configured; that the CLI asked the owner to set an admin token it had withheld; and that the supervisor wrote its respawns apart from a run-log named in .env. The sixth found that the sentence about minting did not hold where no admin credential was ever configured, that the offline recovery wrote a tokens.db the hub never read when the data home was named only in a .env (and a packaged build had none to run), that the CLI's withheld message fired on refusals https would not fix, and that a named-pipe .env still split the run-log. The seventh found that the supervisor now told its child a default the hub never read (a named-pipe .env hid the runtime from the brief), that the packaged recovery revived env tokens that had been rotated away or revoked, and that the CLI still asked for a token that was set. The eighth found that the packaged recovery still missed a token in the start environment or a moved data home, and left a LAN-bound app unable to start, and that the new hint called a valid user token refused on an admin verb and proposed a rotate that revoked the owner's working admin token. Every effective configuration key now carries the layer that supplied it, the note says when a .env value is not in effect, and every surface the row names reports names and layers only. The table. load_layered_env (agents/core/env_provenance.py:356) loads the files. Precedence: the process environment (the shell, a systemd EnvironmentFile, docker -e), then the repo .env, then the data-home .env, and the first layer to set a key wins (override=False). It keeps python-dotenv's behaviour: - Each file is read once, whether a regular file or a named pipe, the way python-dotenv reads it. 1Password mounts a .env as a FIFO. - The load is python-dotenv's public load_dotenv on the text that was read. The names come from python-dotenv's own parser (dotenv.parser.parse_stream), which logs nothing, so a bad line is warned about once. No private python-dotenv API is used. - PYTHON_DOTENV_DISABLED is checked before each layer, as python-dotenv checks it on every call, so a repo .env that sets it stops the data-home layer. Its spellings are python-dotenv's own, kept in env_config.py, the repo's one home for env-flag parsing (agents/core/env_config.py:112); a parity test pins them against python-dotenv's own switch. - The data home is a callable resolved after the repo layer is loaded, so a JARVIS_USER_HOME set in the repo .env names it, as before. A first-run home is scaffolded before its .env is read. - A second load in one process keeps a file's attribution only while the environment still holds the value that load set (a private digest, never the value), and works out afresh what the files shadow. The same file as both layers is read once. - It records which layer supplied each key, by diffing the environment around each file, and the lower layers that also define it (shadowed: their value is ignored). That answers "why is my .env change not applied?". - files() records what the load did with each file: its kind (file, fifo, absent, the same file as the repo .env, or disabled), whether it is present, and whether it was read. provenance() (agents/core/env_provenance.py:531) is the queryable in-process table; a key that appeared after the load reads as set while running. The hub loads first. load_hub_env (agents/core/env_provenance.py:454) is the hub's own load, once per process, so a named pipe is read once: - serve.py calls it before it builds the server and runs its guards (serve.py:244). The server's host, port, log level and shutdown timeout, the posture guards and the bind guard see a .env value, so a token in .env satisfies the bind guard. - The lifespan calls it before its boot guards (agents/web.py:457). The data home, logging, the orchestrator with its audit log, memory and budget ledger, and the router's detect() all read after it: the audit key, the public-profile seed gate, turn embeddings, the budget, the router's URLs and cloud keys in a .env are in effect. The boot guards, the front door among them, judge the loaded environment, so the late re-check was removed. - The coordinator (the systemd jarvis-runtime unit, compose runtime-coordinator) calls it first in run(), before its run-log path and cycle (scripts/coordinator.py:106), and again, idempotently, before it builds its Orchestrator (scripts/coordinator.py:73): it shares the hub's data root, so it keys the audit chain the hub keys. The supervisor that spawns it (scripts/runtime_supervisor.py) does not load, since its child would then read every file key as the process environment's; it reads the run-log path through hub_value (agents/core/env_provenance.py:481), the value the files give without loading them, and it hands its child only a path it found (the process, or a regular .env), which the child's load does not override, so both write that one run-log (scripts/runtime_supervisor.py:72; review-H273g m4). A path in a named-pipe .env, which the supervisor never reads, is not handed down: the child reads the pipe as the hub does, so the cycles land where the brief reads them, and only the supervisor's own events stay in the default file (_child_env, scripts/runtime_supervisor.py:110; review-H273h m1). An empty value is unset on both ends. The reality-evidence harness does too (agents/core/observability/reality_evidence.py:183). - agents/run.py, the REPL, loads before its logging and its imports (agents/run.py:21), so a log file or scanner pattern in .env is in effect. - PluginManager.build asks for it too (agents/core/plugin_manager.py:83), for any other entry. scripts/install_smoke.py does not load before it builds its isolated hub (a temp data root, from the process environment); its PluginManager.build loads afterwards, as for any entry. - Log redaction is imported first, so its switch stays the boot environment's alone: a .env cannot turn it off. Keys read before the load. note_for (agents/core/env_provenance.py:517) flags what is still read before any file can be. That is what importing agents.web freezes or resolves: the ASGI root path, the rate limit, CORS, CSP, the router's auto-deep switch, the analytics cap, the graph's Neo4j defaults, the app and data roots, and the log-redaction switch (agents/core/env_provenance.py:60). A .env value for them is recorded but not in effect, and the note says to set it in the process environment. - What locates the hub's own files (JARVIS_APP_ROOT, JARVIS_HOME, JARVIS_MEMORY_DIR) is never taken from a file (agents/core/env_provenance.py:69). A .env value would have split the stores between two roots. It is recorded and kept out of the environment, and its own note says so (agents/core/env_provenance.py:91). - JARVIS_USER_HOME from the repo .env names the data home and its .env. When JARVIS_HOME is not set, the stores opened while the hub was imported stay under the default root, and its note says so (agents/core/env_provenance.py:72). - Read again after the load, so a .env value is in effect (agents/core/env_provenance.py:84): DEV_MODE; the admin and user tokens (every guard reads them per request, the MCP transport included); the OAuth client ids; the trusted proxies. The lists are measured, not declared: - A test spies on the environment in a subprocess across the import and the whole start up to the load, with the data root set either way. Every Nerva name read there must be listed, and every listed name must be read there. - A second spy runs the start and a few requests. Every read-again name must be read after the load, and no name listed as read before the load may be (tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py:666). The two false read-again entries are fixed: - The MCP transport, the HTTP user guard and the MCP identity check ask one predicate, _user_credential_required (agents/web.py:286; agents/core/routers/mcp.py:393). A token from .env guards MCP on this machine and lets a remote client in. The question is whether a credential was ever configured (_ever_configured, agents/web.py:108): an env token, active or superseded; a rotation or a revoke with --revoke-env, which leave the store's persistent flag; an issued token, live or expired. Such a credential that has lapsed, been rotated away or been revoked locks MCP exactly as it locks HTTP. The admin guard asks the same question for its tier (_admin_configured, agents/web.py:136), and so does the owner flag on /chat, so where an admin credential was ever configured and every credential is revoked or has expired, no local process mints its way back in over HTTP. Recovery is scripts/token_recover.py rotate admin on the box, which loads the hub's .env files first so it writes the tokens.db the hub reads, and says which (review-H273g m2); a packaged build first removes every JARVIS_ADMIN_TOKEN and JARVIS_USER_TOKEN from both places the app reads them, the data home's .env (wherever the home is) and the environment the app starts from, unsets JARVIS_HOST for the restart (the boot guard refuses a LAN bind with no token), then removes security/tokens.db under its data root (JARVIS_HOME and JARVIS_MEMORY_DIR named), because that file is also what records the rotations and revokes that superseded env tokens (docs/PACKAGING.md; review-H273h m2, review-H273i m1). The check lists the token table once per store: once a tier shows configured it stays so for that store (review-H273g n1). Only a hub that never had a credential of the tier trusts a localhost caller without one (for the exception, see Known limits). - The OAuth routes use the plugin manager's copy of the OAuth module (agents/core/routers/oauth.py:35), which re-reads the client ids after the load. Key names come from python-dotenv itself whenever it can be imported (text_keys, agents/core/env_provenance.py:193): every binding in file order, a rebound key each time. The stdlib port (_port_keys, agents/core/env_provenance.py:134) is 1.2.3's grammar and is the fallback for a broken install. A parity test pins it on 25 edge cases, including the BOM, escaped backslashes and a key rebound without "=". A seeded differential fuzz of 4,000 random files pins it too. derive (agents/core/env_provenance.py:318) builds the same table offline. It follows a data home named in the repo .env, or built from ${VAR}, resolving every binding in file order as load_dotenv does: the environment wins over the file's earlier values, a rebound key takes its last value, and a last binding without "=" sets nothing (agents/core/env_provenance.py:285). It parses each file once, keyed on its size, times and inode, and never opens a named pipe. Surfaces: - GET /api/admin/env/sources (agents/core/routers/admin.py:576) is admin-guarded and no-store, at the same boundary as GET /api/admin/env. Each key comes with its layer, a label, the layers it overrides, whether /api/admin/env masks its value, and its note. It also names what the load did with each file (path, kind, present, read); values are never included. - \`nerva doctor\`'s advisory config_sources check (scripts/doctor.py:702): - Reading the hub. It reads the running hub's own table when /readyz is ok, asking without a credential first. Every request to the hub goes over http or https, has a timeout, refuses a redirect, and never goes through a proxy to a hub on this machine (scripts/doctor.py:379). The hub address brackets an IPv6 bind (JARVIS_HOST=::1 gives http://\[::1\]:8080; scripts/doctor.py:297), and an unbracketed IPv6 address, whatever its first group (0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1 too), is read the way http.client dials it (scripts/doctor.py:337). The admin credential goes only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address rather than its spelling (127.0.0.2, 127.1, localhost., ::1 and ::ffff:127.0.0.1 count; scripts/doctor.py:353). config_sources sends it only after a refusal, and runtime_resolves only when no user token is set (scripts/doctor.py:395). Otherwise the doctor predicts the table from its own shell and says which of the two it did and why. - Which keys. It shows every key a .env file sets, and the shell's keys that the hub's code reads or that carry Nerva's prefixes. The hub's reads are found by reading agents/ and serve.py: helper calls, a bound mapping, \*_ENV constants (private ones too), a pool's from_env names and a descriptor's \*_env keyword (scripts/doctor.py:570). - Which names are printed. A .env name is printed when the hub reads it, .env.example declares it, it is an identifier with Nerva's prefix, or it is a plain one-case name with a value, an empty placeholder included (scripts/doctor.py:816). Any other name is counted, never printed (withheld_env_names). That includes a name shaped like a digest, a seed or an access key id, whatever follows its "=" (scripts/doctor.py:588); a value that is only = padding; a PEM line; a mixed-case name; and a name with control characters. - Hub mode. On this machine it reads the files the hub names to tell names from value material. For a hub on another machine it shows only the names Nerva knows, and says why. - Other reports. It names a .env that is not UTF-8, which the hub's load fails on (env_not_utf8), and a named pipe it does not open. For each key it prints the .env values a higher layer overrides and the note. Any failure inside the check becomes a named reason, never a crash. - The \`nerva\` CLI's client (agents/cli/client.py:111) follows the same transport rules: no redirect (urllib carried both tokens to wherever one pointed), no proxy to a hub on this machine, and http or https only. Its loopback rule is the doctor's, pinned equal over one table of spellings. It sends the admin token only to a hub on this machine or over https (agents/cli/client.py:151), never in clear text to another host, whether or not a user token is set; the user token goes to the hub it names. When it withheld the admin token and the hub refuses, it names that as one possible cause beside the user token, never as the only one (review-H273g m3). The hint reads the hub's own reason for the tier it wanted: a verb that needs the admin tier asks for JARVIS_ADMIN_TOKEN when only the user token is set, only a token of the wanted tier that was sent is named as refused, a hub with no token of the tier configured is named as such, and the recovery offered is the additive token_recover.py issue (rotate only for a leaked token, since it revokes every token of the tier; review-H273i m2); nerva status reads the same reason on its runnable and e-stop lines (_auth_hint, agents/cli/nerva.py:696; review-H273h m3, review-H273i m2, n4). - The settings plane: every row says default, set, undeclared, or unreadable (an encrypted secret whose key is lost) (value_source, agents/core/settings_db.py:576). A value a selected product posture puts in effect is named beside the stored one (in_effect, overlay). An unknown or unreadable stored posture is off, and the posture is read in the listing's own connection. \`nerva config list\` prints both (agents/cli/nerva.py:867). - The HUD's Admin "API keys & secrets" list shows each key's layer, the layers it overrides and its note (hydrateAdminKeys, frontend/src/api/live.ts:205; frontend/src/modes3.tsx:254). Hermes records provenance only for vault-supplied names, and its own notes ask for this per-key table; Nerva has it. Nerva has no managed or secret-source layer in the environment: vault secrets are injected at action time, not loaded into the environment. Known limits: - On Windows, key names are compared case-sensitively although the environment is not; that case is theoretical and not handled. - A raw uvicorn start binds where uvicorn's own flags say. JARVIS_HOST there, from a file or from the shell alike, reaches the Host-header guard and the boot guard's bind check, which judges that value rather than the address uvicorn bound. - In a process that loads more than once (tests), a value equal to one an earlier load set keeps that load's attribution. The hub loads once. - JARVIS_USER_HOME set only in a .env, with no JARVIS_HOME, still splits the stores: what the hub opens while it is imported stays under the default root, and the rest follows the home (autonomy.db opens under both). Its note says so on the route, in the doctor and in the HUD. Moving either half would move an existing install's history, so it is left, and set in the process environment it does not split. - The CLI sends the admin token to a hub on another machine when that hub is reached over https: the owner named that hub, and its admin verbs need it. The doctor, which only reads, never sends the admin token off this machine. Over plain http to another machine the admin verbs (the e-stop, approvals, tools, jobs) are refused, and the CLI says why. - A box that never had an admin credential trusts a direct localhost caller as admin, whatever happened to its user tokens: after revoke user --revoke-env, or once a user token has lapsed, a local process still mints a user token. The user-token lock binds callers from other machines; revoke all --revoke-env closes it for this one too (pinned, review-H273g m1). - A run-log path set only in a named-pipe .env reaches the coordinator and the hub, not the supervisor, which cannot read the pipe: its own spawned, child_exited and respawned events go to the default logs/runtime.jsonl, apart from the cycles. Setting the path in the process environment keeps them together. - A token issued with token_store issue and deleted by a revoke without --revoke-env leaves no trace in the store, and so does anything that empties it (purge_expired, which nothing in the hub calls, a deleted tokens.db, a new data root), so that hub reads as never configured for its tier at its next start and trusts a localhost caller again (a running hub that saw the credential stays locked until then). Closing it needs a persistent flag in agents/core/security/token_store.py, the owner's security lane. A rotation, a revoke with --revoke-env, or an expired token keep the hub locked. - A managed user token alone now guards this machine too (ENV-163): an install that has only a store token needs it in the HUD ("store in this browser"), the CLI, the doctor and nerva_mcp_stdio.py. - Under a raw uvicorn start, uvicorn's own proxy knobs (FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS, UVICORN_PROXY_HEADERS) are read before the app is imported, so a .env value for them is not in effect, while announce_trusted_proxies reports the file's value. - A trailing-dot name (localhost., 127.1.) counts as this machine, though the resolver treats it as a DNS name; a resolver that answered it with another address would receive the admin token. - The value-material shape misses uppercase hex, digitless base32 and short mixed ids, and withholds digit-bearing names with no underscore (contrived lines). - The data home is scaffolded (its README, a .env copied from .env.example) before the boot guards run, so a start they refuse still writes it. - Hub mode on this machine trusts the paths the hub names: a hub in a container names paths that do not exist on the host, and every plain custom key is then withheld with a reason that is false for it. - The scan for the hub's reads misses computed and mapped names (a URL key built from a prefix, a budget key read through a mapping); both are declared in .env.example or carry the prefix today. - JARVIS_HOST=0::0 or ::ffff:0.0.0.0 gives a URL judged not this machine (the proxy is used and the admin token withheld, the safe direction); JARVIS_HOST=localhost:9000 gives an invalid URL, as it did before. Tests: - tests/test_env_provenance.py (61 cases): parity and fuzz (tests/test_env_provenance.py:130), the named pipe, the plugin manager's order and the posture overlay. - tests/test_doctor_hub_credential.py (8): a real redirect and a real proxy, pinning where the credential goes (tests/test_doctor_hub_credential.py:93). - tests/test_h273c_provenance_review.py (56): the import, the names withheld, the bound-mapping scan and the second round's nits. - tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py (74 cases, the third round): - the start measured and the lists by where they are used (tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py:119); - knobs the start reads, in effect from a .env (tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py:158); - the bind guard through serve.py and the lifespan, the redaction floor, one load per process, the refused roots; - the MCP transport, the OAuth routes, and the doctor's and CLI's transport rules; - hub mode, the names withheld, the widened scan, the reviewer's survivors and the nits. - tests/test_h273e_provenance_review.py (48 cases, the fourth round): the MCP transport, its per-tool identity check and the HTTP guard in every credential posture, rotated, expired, revoked, a managed token only and never configured (tests/test_h273e_provenance_review.py:59); the coordinator keying the audit chain from .env; the IPv6 bind; the CLI's admin-token destination; the CLI and doctor loopback rules over one table (tests/test_h273e_provenance_review.py:238); the REPL's log file; and the refused roots' note. - tests/test_h273f_provenance_review.py (43 cases, the fifth round): an admin env token alone closing the loopback fallback; no local caller mints a token once every credential is revoked or has expired, in three postures, while a box that never had an admin credential still mints its first from loopback; the owner flag on /chat; the offline recovery; an expired managed user token keeping the hub locked, and the recorded residual; the CLI's admin token for an admin-only environment and over the whole table of spellings, and what it says when it withholds it; the supervisor's and the coordinator's run-log from .env; hub_value's layers; the reality-evidence harness's load; a bare IPv6 hub whose first group is not empty. - tests/test_h273g_provenance_review.py (18 cases, the sixth round): a user-only box still trusting this machine as admin, and the docstrings that say so; an issued-only admin token, live and expired, closing the loopback fallback; the configured scope remembered per store; nothing purging expired tokens; the recovery writing the store a .env names; the CLI's hint for the client that was refused, with and without an admin token, and on nerva status's two lines; one run-log for a named-pipe .env and for a path the child is told; an empty run-log value; hub_value's disabled and interpolated layers; the supervisor run as a script. - tests/test_h273h_provenance_review.py (14 cases, the seventh round): a named-pipe run-log path reaching the coordinator and the hub alike, through a real load of the pipe; a process path handed down as it is; nothing handed down when the app cannot import; the packaged recovery removing the env tokens before the file, and naming the JARVIS_HOME path; a refused token that is set named as refused for each tier and both; the status lines, loopback and LAN; scripts/token_recover.py run as documented, as a script; both spawn sites in the supervisor's main() carrying the run-log. - tests/test_h273i_provenance_review.py (12 cases, the eighth round): the hint for each tier the hub names (a user token on an admin verb, a refused admin token and its issue verb, the user tier, a hub with no token of the tier), the verb passing the hub's reason, the status lines naming a hub with no user token, the child keeping its parent's environment, the packaged recovery naming both places, the bind and JARVIS_MEMORY_DIR, the test manual's recovery rows, and the supervisor's docstring. - tests/test_doctor.py keeps the JSON contract, and frontend/src/test/env-sources.test.ts the HUD mapping and its note. Mutation: - The first round's 41 mutants and the second's 29 were each caught, as were the second review's twelve survivors. - The third review's own 67 independent mutants left 16 survivors, four of them equivalent. Each of the other twelve now has a test: the lists' classification, a mixed-case name, a missing value, the data home's values, the cache key, a commented declaration, a 403, a posture that is not JSON, a third load, and the same-file order. Re-run against the new tests, all twelve are caught. - This round's 46 mutants (44 Python, 2 HUD) were each run against their own suites: 45 are caught. The survivor drops the IPv4-mapped check in the loopback test. It is equivalent on the test interpreter, whose patched ipaddress already counts ::ffff:127.0.0.1 as loopback, and the check stays for the 3.12 releases before that fix. - The fourth review's 50 independent mutants left three survivors (the MCP gate's predicate and two CLI loopback spellings); each is caught now. This round's 20 mutants (the bracketed IPv6 address and the bare IPv6 host in both copies, the CLI's admin-token destination, the shared predicate in the guard, the MCP gate and the identity check, the coordinator's and the REPL's load, the refused roots' note, the CLI's spellings) were all caught, one after the identity-check case was added. The fifth review's 22 independent mutants left four real survivors (the CLI with an admin token and no user token, a localhost-prefix spelling for the admin token, the coordinator's run() load, the reality-evidence harness's load); each has a case now. This round's 15 mutants (the ever-configured rule for both tiers and each of its terms, the CLI's hint, the supervisor's read and hub_value's layers, the bare IPv6 order in both copies) were caught but one, the admin rule ignoring the env token, which is caught now that the case exists; one more is equivalent (hub_value's early process-environment return, which the repo layer's merge gives anyway). The sixth review's 30 independent mutants left nine real survivors (an issued-only admin token, the generic hint, two hub_value layer rules, the supervisor run as a script, an empty run-log value, and three bare-IPv6 spellings in the safe direction); the first six have cases now. This round's 13 mutants were all caught, one after its case was added (the e-stop status line). The seventh review's 22 independent mutants left three real survivors (the recovery's import path run as a script, and the supervisor's two spawn sites); each has a case now. This round's 12 mutants were all caught, one after the supervisor's two .env readers became one (its import path, now covered by the script-run case). The eighth review's 22 independent mutants left one real survivor (the child's environment dropped whole) and one unpinned hint verb; both have cases, and this round's 12 mutants over the hint and the child's environment are all caught. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Image-turn confirmation review (2026-10-02): the agents/cli/nerva.py diff adds selection flags only to chat image turns, rejects them on text chat, validates optional bounded requirements and forwards explicit flags. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. Nous account collateral review (2026-10-02): compared the complete diff from 7a73bf03. Existing CLI command implementations are unchanged; only auth parser/dispatch registration is additive. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. Existing OAuth handler bodies are unchanged; four separate owner-only Nous routes and documentation are added. All pre-edit evidence hashes matched the prior checkpoint. This preserves the existing verdict without claiming new capability completion; the 266-pass/3-skip integration suite includes existing OAuth, secrets and route gates. | <a id="h283"></a>H283 | Know what kind of machine this is, and tell the model | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub now speaks systemd's sd_notify, and the operator can tell the model what kind of machine this is. Host detection was already richer than Hermes' (probe_host, agents/core/host_probe.py:406; detect_hardware, agents/core/hardware.py:215; /healthz and /readyz, agents/core/routers/ops.py:42 and :149), but nothing wrote to NOTIFY_SOCKET and the model was told only the live backend. agents/core/sd_notify.py: notify (agents/core/sd_notify.py:66) sends one datagram to NOTIFY_SOCKET, a path or an @abstract name (_address, agents/core/sd_notify.py:48), and never raises (a socket nobody listens on is a debug line); with NOTIFY_SOCKET unset (a shell, Docker, launchd, Windows) nothing is sent (enabled, agents/core/sd_notify.py:61). serve.py runs sd_notify.NotifyingServer (agents/core/sd_notify.py:159), a uvicorn.Server subclass: after the port is bound its startup calls NOTIFIER.ready with /readyz's own body (Notifier.ready, agents/core/sd_notify.py:129), so READY=1 follows the bind and a failed bind is never announced. The lifespan sends nothing, because uvicorn runs it before binding, and a raw \`uvicorn agents.web:app\` does not speak the protocol (the shipped unit runs serve.py): READY=1 and STATUS=serving once the orchestrator and its agents are loaded, a STATUS naming why when they are not, and from then on a heartbeat task on the event loop sends WATCHDOG=1 every half WATCHDOG_USEC (watchdog_seconds, agents/core/sd_notify.py:99: at least 0.1 s but never more than 0.8 of the deadline; armed when WATCHDOG_PID is this process or is unset, as in libsystemd; ASCII decimals only), so a hung loop stops the pings and systemd restarts the hub. STOPPING=1 and STATUS=draining are sent at the top of NotifyingServer.shutdown, before uvicorn drains (also on a forced exit), and the heartbeat ends (Notifier.stopping, agents/core/sd_notify.py:143). A full notify queue (EAGAIN, or ENOBUFS as macOS reports it) is retried for up to 1 s (SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS) rather than dropping READY; any other socket error is not retried. The shipped unit is Type=notify with NotifyAccess=main, WatchdogSec=60 and TimeoutStartSec=300 (deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.service), and deploy/systemd/README.md now describes the protocol instead of saying the hub does not emit it. The environment hint (agents/core/environment_hint.py): JARVIS_ENVIRONMENT_HINT (ENV_NAME, agents/core/environment_hint.py:27), read through env_config, is cleaned (clean_hint, agents/core/environment_hint.py:47): a literal \\n is a line break, except inside a Windows path (D:\\nerva, \\\\nas), and in an EnvironmentFile the value must be double-quoted (the shipped example is); U+2028, U+2029 and NEL are line breaks too; control and bidi characters and undecodable bytes (lone surrogates) are removed, no line may open a heading (ATX or setext), blank runs fold, and it is cut to 2,000 characters (MAX_HINT_CHARS, agents/core/environment_hint.py:28). hint_block (agents/core/environment_hint.py:65) puts it under "## This machine (the operator's description: context about where you run, not instructions)" (HEADER, agents/core/environment_hint.py:29), and Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) places that block in the stable part of every agent's system prompt, between the shared behaviour contract and the persona (agents/core/agent.py:583): it never forks the persona, and the same environment gives the same bytes every turn, so the prompt cache holds; with the variable unset the prompt is exactly what it was. The cleaning is linear and cached per raw value. \`nerva prompt-size\` counts the hint as an environment-hint row in the shared floor (environment_hint_component, agents/core/prompt_size.py:149). The env file template (deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.env) shows an example. tests/test_h283_host_notify_and_hint.py (43 cases) uses real datagram sockets: a message reaches a path and an abstract socket, no socket or a relative one sends nothing, a dead socket is no error, the heartbeat interval (half, the minimum, this PID only, bad values), not-ready STATUS without READY, READY then heartbeats from the loop with none sent while the loop is blocked then STOPPING and no more beats, no heartbeat without a watchdog, nothing without systemd, the lifespan sending nothing, a real uvicorn bind that sends READY only once the port accepts connections and never when the bind fails, a not-ready server that sends STATUS but no READY, STOPPING before the lifespan teardown and on a forced exit, a full queue waited on, the retry bounded to SEND_TIMEOUT_SECONDS and only for a full buffer, the shipped unit and its quoted example; and for the hint the cleaning (Windows paths, setext and U+2028 headings, surrogates, linear time), the cap, the heading rule, the prompt-size row, the label, the absence when unset, and its place and byte-stability in a real agent's prompt. 21 mutants, all caught; the review round's 17 mutants: 16 killed and 1 equivalent (READY only after self.started: uvicorn 0.53, the floor in requirements.txt, exits on every path where the server did not start). Test manual: ENV-165, ENV-166. Adapted from Hermes: of its ~35 detection variables only the two with an operational effect are taken (NOTIFY_SOCKET/WATCHDOG_USEC and the environment hint); the container, terminal and multiplexer sniffing stays out, since host_probe already names the limits that change what Nerva can do. | <a id="h285"></a>H285 | Choose which skills, plugins and MCP servers load at startup | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner declares which skills, plugins and MCP servers load at boot, and a plugin toggle survives a restart. Six declared settings rows (agents/core/settings_db.py:398), category \`loadset\`, hold comma lists: skills_disabled/skills_only, plugins_disabled/plugins_only, mcp_disabled/mcp_only, editable from the settings page and \`nerva config set\` (which says a loadset.\* key or a plugin switch applies at the next start). A disabled name does not load; a non-empty only-list loads only what it names; the disabled list wins (permits, agents/core/load_set.py:96). A list is read at call time (declared, agents/core/load_set.py:74), trimmed, de-duplicated and bounded to 256 names of at most 128 characters without control characters (parse_names, agents/core/load_set.py:43), and an unreadable store narrows nothing. For plugins the settings page's existing per-plugin \`plugins.\<id\>\` switches, which nothing used to read, count too: an explicit off is off (agents/core/load_set.py:84). SkillLoader.discover reads the lists once per pass (agents/core/skills/loader.py:652-653) and a skill named by its registry name or its folder is not registered at all, before any quarantine row (agents/core/skills/loader.py:731); a registration from an earlier pass is taken out when a later pass denies it, the per-pass snapshot is cleared in a finally, and in safe mode (the owner folder unread) no declared name is reported as unknown. PermissionGate switches off the plugins the lists name when it is built (apply_load_set, agents/core/plugin_gate.py:647), and PluginManager.build drops them from orch.plugins before binding oracle_bridge and Argus (_drop_switched_off, agents/core/plugin_manager.py:198), so the probes, the dashboard and WorldView never reach a switched-off plugin. Each gate holds its own copies of the built-in manifests (agents/core/plugin_gate.py:641), so switching one off never reaches the module table or another gate, and the readers of a plugin's enabled state ask the live gate: CloudImageRuntime is wired with orch.permission_gate (_available, agents/core/cloud_image_runtime.py:183) and the capability board reads the gate's manifests (_plugin_records, agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:127). The plugin toggle (toggle_plugin, agents/core/routers/plugins.py:99) keeps the choice in one place, the plugin's plugins.\<id\> settings switch when it has one and otherwise plugins_disabled; switched on, it leaves plugins_disabled and joins a declared plugins_only (persist_plugin, agents/core/load_set.py:140). It reads the lists fail-closed (read_setting), so an unreadable store is not overwritten, and answers \`persisted\` (false when the store refused or could not be read, the live toggle standing). It writes a SETTINGS_CHANGE audit row naming the keys, and a re-enable that cannot be audited is switched back off and answers 503 audit_failed. The MCP load registers only the permitted saved servers (agents/web.py:1823-1830); a switched-off one is held (_MCP_HELD, agents/web.py:1800) and written back unchanged by every save, so the saved configuration never loses it, and adding a server of that name or removing it answers 409 \`switched_off\` naming the list (_held_refusal, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:145). The lists only narrow: nothing is installed, approved, registered or written to the approval store, and a declared name that matches nothing installed is reported as unknown (finish, agents/core/load_set.py:121). The status rows carry it: /skills (agents/core/routers/skills.py:76), /plugins (agents/core/routers/plugins.py:88) and /api/admin/mcp (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:118) return \`load_set\` with the declared lists, what was switched off and what is unknown (status, agents/core/load_set.py:132). Safe mode (H275) applies first; the load set narrows further. tests/test_h285_load_set.py (49 cases, plus one in tests/test_cloud_image.py) covers the parsing and its bounds, the six declared rows, the permit table, an unreadable store, a disabled skill and an only-list with their status rows and unknown names, a skill named by its folder, a named-but-absent skill installing or approving nothing (asserted on a private approval store), the skills route, MCP servers switched off and still saved, an MCP only-list, the add and remove refusals for a held server, adding and removing other servers keeping the held one, the MCP list route, the gate's disabled and only lists, the settings-page switch and that only an explicit off counts, that the lists never enable or register, the toggle kept across a restart both ways, a plugin without a settings switch, enabling under an only-list, a toggle the store refuses, and the plugins route. 33 mutants over the parser, the permit table, the persistence, the three loaders, the refusals and the status rows: all caught (two after their cases were added: an explicit off, a refused write). Test manual: ENV-169, ENV-170. Adapted from Hermes: the lists live in the settings store Nerva already persists and edits rather than a YAML file, the plugin toggle writes them, and an MCP server switched off is kept in the saved list rather than dropped. There is no glob file-finding mode: the glob only narrows which files' contents are searched, a pattern is required, and file_list lists a single directory. | <a id="h313"></a>H313 | Speak | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The model can decide to say one short text aloud on one room's device, and can do it again. register_speak_tool (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:684) adds a ToolRPC \`speak\` tool with gated=True, trusted execution and its own gated intake (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:711, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:717). It is registered only while JARVIS_MEDIA_DIRECTOR is on (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:697), and AutonomyCoordinator wires it with the broker kernel and the governed enqueue (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:725, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1618). It reaches the same Media Director instance the media routes use, so the owner's device registry and session board apply (agents/core/routers/media_director.py:272). SpeakTool.preflight (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:388) runs at proposal and again before the approved run. It refuses text over the spoken-reply cap and code-only text (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:394, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:396). It resolves a room to that room's one announce-capable default device, so the card title and the persisted args name the speaker (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:268, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:467). It raises no card the run could only refuse: unified API off, kernel off or unbound, no media root, no speech backend, no driver for the device, or no configured presence room each refuse by name (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:415, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:410). SpeakTool.intake (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:445) shows the kernel the exact toolrpc.speak row it enqueues. Under the production composition (worker.kernel_gate over MediationKernelBridge, agents/core/autonomy/worker.py:648, agents/core/kernel/binding.py:108) that decision binds as the row's intake evidence, and a DENY raises no card (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:471). Only the human-accepted durable toolrpc.speak row runs. The kind is trusted (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:68) and the executor routes it to the trusted rail (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1639-1642). ToolRPC re-runs the preflight and requires the trusted execution context (agents/core/tool_rpc.py:595, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:623), and the handler refuses a row of any other kind (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:541). SpeakTool.execute (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:536) synthesizes the clip through SpokenReply on the host TTSEngine in the owner's configured voice (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:552, agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:265, agents/core/voice/tts.py:216). It writes the clip owner-only under the first media root (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:639, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:653) and presents it only through CapabilityActionAPI.perform('action:media.present') in announce mode (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:592, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:597). The facade asks the Action Kernel before the handler runs (agents/core/capability_actions.py:176) and refuses on a DENY (agents/core/capability_actions.py:183), so the kernel authorizes the effect and the tool never reaches a driver. A kernel QUEUE on that present is honoured as the accepted row and audited as speak.durably_approved (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:500, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:522). A DENY becomes kernel_denied with the kernel's reason (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:614). The Media Director treats an announce as a one-shot notice (agents/core/media_director.py:738): it never blocks the next present and costs no interrupt budget (agents/core/media_director.py:890), and whatever it cut into stays the restore point (agents/core/media_director.py:992). Because the tool is gated, tool_profiles offers it only to the owner on the operator surface (agents/core/tool_profiles.py:190, agents/core/tool_profiles.py:194), and the snapshot pins this (tests/_snapshots/tool_profiles.json:50, tests/test_tool_profiles.py:202). Tests cover: registration and postures (tests/test_speak_tool.py:170, tests/test_speak_tool.py:196); named preflight refusals that enqueue nothing (tests/test_speak_tool.py:271, tests/test_speak_tool.py:320); and the card naming the device (tests/test_speak_tool.py:286). With the real kernel.authorize, AutonomyPolicy and KillSwitch behind worker.kernel_gate, an approved speak is mediated and audited, and an engaged kill switch refuses both the run and the card (tests/test_speak_tool.py:520, tests/test_speak_tool.py:543, tests/test_speak_tool.py:576). Three back-to-back normal speaks on one device all play at no budget cost (tests/test_speak_tool.py:591). Speaking over music needs high urgency, pays once and keeps the music as the restore point (tests/test_speak_tool.py:608, tests/test_media_director.py:762). A kernel DENY, a missing TTS backend, a missing media root and a driverless device each refuse by name with no playback, and an engine that yields no audio refuses as tts_failed with nothing presented (tests/test_speak_tool.py:681, tests/test_speak_tool.py:469, tests/test_speak_tool.py:666, tests/test_speak_tool.py:703, tests/test_speak_tool.py:716). A row of another kind cannot speak (tests/test_speak_tool.py:753), and the clip spool stays bounded (tests/test_speak_tool.py:779). Known limits outside the accepted adaptation: an accepted speak runs at the next autonomy tick (default 60 s, floor 15 s, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:475), like every gated tool. A presence:auto card names the sentinel, because only play time knows whether the owner is in the presence room. Audible output needs an owner-wired speaker driver (the in-repo NullMediaDriver refuses and LocalFileMediaDriver is a reference), the same host seam as every media.present. The speech-backend gate (tts_installed, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:191) needs edge-tts, Kokoro, Piper with a model or the approved TTS command, with edge-tts and the command left out under voice.local_only, like the spoken-reply channel; a named TTS provider alone does not pass it. An announce of any length, including one sent through POST /api/media/present, no longer holds its device, as docs/FLAGS.md states. Hermes's tts.provider, custom command providers and tts.speed are outside the accepted adaptation, because Nerva's TTS subsystem was already ahead. Named speech compatibility review: the host TTS engine now dispatches explicit named providers through the common registry. The governed speak tool, device binding and audio publication remain unchanged; speak-tool/Media Director regressions pass. Auto-vision collateral review (2026-10-02, 876c3e2b): the only changed pinned source is provider documentation in docs/FLAGS.md. The speak-tool implementation, guarded command path and device publication were unchanged; the refreshed document and existing test evidence preserve this equivalent verdict. | <a id="h327"></a>H327 | SKILL.md frontmatter contract (agentskills.io-compatible) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The SKILL.md frontmatter contract Hermes parses (agent/skill_utils.parse_frontmatter and tools/skills_tool_plugin.py) now lives in one shared module, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py, used by both the loader and the importer. Parsing. split_frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:47): - drops a leading UTF-8 BOM before the fence check (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:56); - takes the closing fence at column 0 only, as Hermes' \`\\n---\\s\*\\n\` does, so an indented \`---\` inside a block scalar is content (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:63); - falls back to key: value lines for a block that is not valid YAML (_naive_key_values, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:83), instead of demoting the file to the heading dialect. The fallback is stricter than Hermes' line split: - only column-0 keys count, so a nested \`name:\` cannot replace the skill's own; - a value wrapped in matching quotes loses them; - a fallback that finds neither a name nor a description counts as no frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:77), so a heading-dialect file framed by fences keeps its heading parse. Identity decisions pass lenient=False (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:72), so a name only the fallback can find never decides one: the Hermes pin gate (agents/core/skills/importer.py:271) and the migration and rescan slugs (agents/core/skills/importer.py:1192, agents/core/skills/importer.py:1376). SkillLoader._parse_manifest decodes with utf-8-sig on both the snapshot and the path read (agents/core/skills/loader.py:854, agents/core/skills/loader.py:856). A manifest the normaliser cannot read registers under the directory name instead of failing (agents/core/skills/loader.py:871). The manifest: - The name is capped at 64 characters and stripped after the cut (agents/core/skills/loader.py:890; the caps are at agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:28-29). - The description is capped at 1024 characters with an ellipsis, quotes kept (cap_description, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:351). When none is declared, it is the first non-heading body line, as Hermes' listing reads it (agents/core/skills/loader.py:892-893). - A list-valued author or license is joined (agents/core/skills/loader.py:895). - agents and requires read a YAML list, \`\[a, b\]\` or \`a, b\` as plain strings (agents/core/skills/loader.py:882-883). - Command entries are reduced to JSON types (agents/core/skills/loader.py:967). contract_fields (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:359), merged into every frontmatter manifest (agents/core/skills/loader.py:902), keeps the keys Hermes recognises: - homepage and compatibility; - platforms, read as Hermes matches them (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:204): a list, or one string as one name. Each name maps through PLATFORM_MAP and matches the OS whose sys.platform starts with it, so darwin and win count and osx and win64 do not. An unmatched name is kept, so a gate refuses it everywhere, as Hermes hides it; - environments and requires_apps, where a string is one item (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:224, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:234); - triggers and dependencies; - tags and related_skills, where a declared metadata.hermes value wins over the top-level key (skill_view's \`hermes_meta.get(k) or frontmatter.get(k)\`); - setup, prerequisites and required_credential_files; - required_environment_variables (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:256), which matches Hermes' _get_required_environment_variables: it merges the declared list, setup.collect_secrets and the legacy prerequisites.env_vars, first entry wins, names are validated, and \`optional\` is read for truth; - metadata.hermes.{tags, category, upstream_skill, supersedes, session_platforms, requires_toolsets, requires_tools, fallback_for_toolsets, fallback_for_tools, config} (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:340). A config entry needs a key and a description. Skill exposes platforms, environments, requires_apps, required_env (names only; no value is read) and hermes_meta, and to_dict carries them (agents/core/skills/loader.py:476, agents/core/skills/loader.py:524, agents/core/skills/loader.py:532, agents/core/skills/loader.py:536, agents/core/skills/loader.py:542). Imported frontmatter is third-party text: - json_safe (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:128) bounds depth, width, string length and total nodes, with a 2,048-node budget per value (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:134). A 2 KB YAML alias bomb that would expand to 64^6 nodes parses in about 0.02 s. - Dates become ISO strings, and NaN, infinities and integers past 64 bits become null or "". A hex integer past Python's str() limit no longer raises (_scalar_text, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:112), so the security-posture route serialises Skill.to_dict with allow_nan=False. - discover() skips a skill whose load raises and keeps the rest, while the signing misconfiguration still stops discovery (agents/core/skills/loader.py:662-667). - A second skill taking an occupied registry name is logged: a warning within one tree (e.g. two names sharing their first 64 characters), info for a user skill replacing a bundled one (agents/core/skills/loader.py:679). Deliberate differences from Hermes: - A closing fence at end of file with no trailing newline is accepted, as Nerva always did. - The registry is last-writer-wins with that log line; Hermes' listing keeps the first. Tests: tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py has 51 cases, including the alias bomb (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:286), a hex integer in seven fields (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:306), NaN and huge numbers through to_dict (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:315), one broken skill among others (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:328), the fallback rules (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:354), Hermes platform matching (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:406), the strict identity parse (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:434) and the snapshot BOM path (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:482). A mutation pass over 25 mutants (first-wins, precedence, the depth, width and node caps, both utf-8-sig decodes, the fence, the fallback rules, optional, the strict gate, NaN and big ints, the discover guard, the collision log, the platform string, author join, quotes, the body-line fallback, name strip, command sanitising, requires_apps) killed all of them. 454 tests across 28 skill, import, marketplace, migration and posture files pass. Gating on these fields is not this row: H328 (visibility) and the setup rows consume them. [d1](../agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py) [d2](../agents/core/skills/loader.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/importer.py) |
| <a id="h328"></a>H328 | Conditional skill visibility (OS platform, runtime environment, channel, tool availability) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A skill is shown where it makes sense, by the four Hermes gates split into hard and soft (agents/core/skills/visibility.py; HARD_GATES and SOFT_GATES, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:47-48), reading the fields H327 keeps. Hard: \`platforms\`. readiness (agents/core/skills/visibility.py:92) answers \`unsupported on \<host\> (it declares ...)\` for a skill that declares only other operating systems, naming only the OS names Nerva knows (macos, linux, windows, android) and counting any other declared name (\`N unknown platforms\`), so no manifest text reaches a reply. The host is sys.platform mapped to macos, linux or windows (host_platforms, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:83), and on Termux it also counts as linux and android. A skill that declares none is ready everywhere. An unsupported skill is left out of every offer (catalog_gate answers \`unsupported\`, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1362, after the disabled, sandboxed, untrusted and agent gates, so skill_view answers \`skill_unknown\` for a skill the caller may not see, whatever its platforms). skill_view answers \`skill_unsupported\` with \`readiness_status: unsupported\` (agents/core/skills/tools.py:315). Its bare command words are not matched (parse_command, agents/core/skills/loader.py:994, considers only skills that run on this host, so a supported skill that shares the word gets it, and otherwise the text goes to the model). Named as \`skill:\<name\>\`, it is refused by Skill.execute (agents/core/skills/loader.py:548) when it has code to run; a code-less skill answers nothing, so the turn goes to the model. Skill.to_dict and GET /skills carry \`readiness\` and \`readiness_reason\` (agents/core/routers/skills.py:61), and the Skill Switches panel marks it. Soft: the other three gates only hide a skill from what the model is offered, meaning the prompt catalog (read once per catalog, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1466) and skills_list (read once per list, agents/core/skills/tools.py:283). Named explicitly, it still works: skill_view reads it (agents/core/skills/tools.py:229) and its command runs, because an explicit request is consent (offer_gate, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:186). \`environments\` are shown only where one holds (host_environments, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:111). \`docker\` and \`container\` come from /.dockerenv or /run/.containerenv, and \`s6\` from /run/s6 or /command/s6-svscan. \`kanban\` holds for a turn with no human in it (the internal surface: a job, a workflow, the heartbeat). Any tag the owner lists in JARVIS_SKILL_ENVIRONMENTS also holds. \`metadata.hermes.session_platforms\` shows a skill only on the channels named. \`cli\` names the operator channels (the HUD and voice), and a turn bound to no channel is not gated. The unbound principal's \`unknown\` channel now counts as no channel for the H329 switches too. \`requires_tools\` / \`requires_toolsets\` hide a skill unless every named tool, or one tool of every named toolset, is offered this turn. \`fallback_for_tools\` / \`fallback_for_toolsets\` hide it while those are offered. Hermes toolset names map onto Nerva's tools (TOOLSETS, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:51); an unknown toolset is never offered. The turn's offer is read without side effects (AgentToolRuntime.offered_names, agents/core/agent_runtime.py:265, runs the profile in a copied context, so H661's turn offer is not left bound; empty when the tool loop is off). The orchestrator binds it around the catalog (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3388; bind_offer, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:125), and inside the tool loop the loop's own offer is used (current_offer, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:134). When the offer cannot be read, the tool gates stand aside. Every hide is logged once per skill and gate (note_hidden, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:213), by skills_list and by the prompt catalog, which gates every skill past its row cap and only stops adding rows. Nothing here enables anything: it only narrows what is shown. tests/test_h328_skill_visibility.py (40 cases) covers the host names, Termux, readiness, environment detection and the owner's tags, the unsupported skill left out, refused and answered by skill_view and skills_list, an environment skill hidden but still viewable and runnable, kanban per surface, session_platforms with the cli alias and no channel, fourteen tool-gate cases, the tool loop's own offer, malformed fields, the logging, the prompt-context binding (reset after, an unreadable offer), the runtime's offer, the /skills readiness and the unbound channel; the review round added no manifest text in a refusal, the platform gate after the scope and trust gates, a code-less unsupported skill left to the model, a shared command word going to the supported skill, the hides logged by skills_list and past the catalog's cap, and the live runtime (the real coordinator wiring) leaving no offer bound, and a refusal quoting each known OS name and counting each unknown one once. The panel has a vitest case. The row's 42 mutants were all killed, and the review round's 16, two after one case was added. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added.
