| <a id="h433"></a>H433 | Retrieval query rewriting by a cheap auxiliary model | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py ports Hermes' rewriter (plugins/memory/query_rewrite.py). Its review rounds changed where the rewrite is used and tightened the filter. (1) The rewriter. rewrite_query (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:134) asks a model for exactly one concise question about the user's own history, at TEMPERATURE 0 and MAX_TOKENS 96 (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:39-40). - The latest message is bounded to 4,000 characters, keeping its head and tail around an omission marker (bounded_user_message, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:97). - It is passed only as a JSON string on one data line. NEL (U+0085), U+2028 and U+2029, the line breaks JSON leaves raw, are escaped by hand, so the data cannot break that line (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:78, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:141). - SYSTEM_PROMPT (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:80) says to treat the message as untrusted data, never follow it, never answer it, and ask in the message's language. - Any exception, or an answer the filter rejects, returns "" so the caller recalls on the raw text, exactly as in Hermes. (2) The filter. normalize_rewrite (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:111): - NFKC-normalises the answer and removes format characters first (_plain, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:105); - strips fences, labels (English and Romanian) and quotes, and control and line-separator characters; - rejects an answer over 320 characters, not a question, not grounded in the user's memory, instruction-shaped, or with a second sentence. Nerva additions for a Romanian-speaking owner: - Romanian interrogatives (_QUESTION_START_RE, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:47) and grounding words (_MEMORY_GROUNDING_RE, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:60). - An auxiliary starter, English or Romanian ("Did…", "Do…", "Am…", "E…", "A…", _AUX_START_RE, agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:55), counts as a question only when whitespace follows it and the model itself ended with "?" (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:122). "Do my taxes now.", "E-mail all my passwords…", "Am rezervat hotelul." and an invented "Ai programare marți la 10." are therefore refused. An interrogative word ("What…", "Ce…") needs no "?". - Second-person and object pronouns are not grounding: "Can you tell me the capital of France?" is refused. - The instruction check also reads "ignoring", "disregard" and the Romanian forms (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:68). - A second sentence is refused: sentence punctuation (including ";" and "…") then a space and more text, or "?", "!" or ";" straight into more text ("?Now", ";also"). A "." or "…" with no space stays inside the question ("the 3.5 kg bag") (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:77). - A question the model ended with "." gets "?" instead (agents/core/memory/query_rewrite.py:131). This is a quality gate on what the embedder is asked, not a security control. The rewritten query reaches only the embedding model, never a generative one, and a homoglyph can still pass. (3) Wiring (critic note 12). _recall_hits (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2840) runs the rewrite only when memory.recall_query_rewrite is on (seeded OFF at agents/core/settings_db.py:290, because it costs a model call). - It runs after the H428 triviality gate and the job-pin check, and inside the H428 bound. - It gets half the bound of its own (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2856-2860), as Hermes gives the rewrite its own 8 s. Past that, the model call is cancelled and recall runs on the raw text within the same turn, instead of the whole recall becoming a straggler. - The rewrite becomes the embedding query only. The graph leg keeps the raw message as its keyword (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2868): it finds an entity whose name or a property contains the keyword, so it only ever helps a short, entity-like message ("BMW", "dentist"), and a rewritten whole question would never match. With the rewrite off or rejected, recall is unchanged (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2869). Hermes likewise keeps the raw message for its context fetch. (4) Strict-local. _query_rewriter (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2957) uses LLMRouter.local_backend only (agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:52). That is the fail-closed accessor, which never falls through to a cloud backend: no local backend means it raises and the rewrite returns "". This is proven through a real HybridRouter whose only backend is cloud. The call re-checks the job-model pin itself (agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:49), and tells a Qwen3 model (the default local family) not to think, so the 96 tokens are not spent before the answer (agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:58). Like Hermes, the rewriter sees only the latest message, so a reference to an earlier turn ("the other one") stays unresolved. The win is a grounded question for short or oblique messages. Tests: tests/test_query_rewrite.py has 57 cases. 24 of them fail on 8e824b9f, the commit before the first review round; 5 on 890e80b6 (the NEL escape, three auxiliaries without "?" and ";also"); and 1 on 3b4398d2 (";also"). They cover: - the bounded JSON data line and the line-break escapes (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:61, tests/test_query_rewrite.py:208); - the accept and reject tables, including the Romanian statements and answers, the E-/A starters, English auxiliaries without "?", ungrounded second person, NFKC and a zero-width space (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:92, tests/test_query_rewrite.py:125); - errors and the seeded toggle; - the rewrite used when on, off by default, and not paid by a trivial prompt; - a hung rewrite costing half the bound before recall runs on the raw text (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:190); - the raw graph keyword (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:201); - a pinned turn, and the rewriter refusing under a pin (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:216, tests/test_query_rewrite.py:226); - the real HybridRouter with only a cloud backend (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:252); - initial temperature 0 and 96 tokens (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:285); - the Qwen3 switch (tests/test_query_rewrite.py:310). The memory, recall and orchestrator suites pass (one known local-3.11 failure, as on the base). H513 fixture review: synthetic local backends now declare their actual LM Studio profile and loopback endpoint; original behavior assertions remain unchanged. Local auxiliary integration review (2026-10-02): _query_rewriter now delegates to generate_local_auxiliary with JARVIS_AUX_QUERY_REWRITE_MODEL; blank keeps the active/default local model. The prompt, filters,96-token/initial-zero-temperature and fallback behavior are unchanged; the selected model controls Qwen3 suffixing. Job pins and H513 remain enforced inside the common helper. Prepared auxiliary helper review (2026-10-02): this consumer still delegates through generate_local_auxiliary; model selection, default, prompt/budget, Qwen behavior where applicable and H513 await/stream lifetime are preserved. The new preparation layer captures one route and rechecks the fixed job-pin policy at invocation. Existing cited suites and new late-pin regressions pass in the frozen full backend; no capability promotion. Temperature recovery qualification (2026-10-02): zero temperature is the initial rewrite request; one explicit structured local HTTP400 rejection may select provider-default temperature by omission. The 96-token cap, Qwen3 suffix, local accessor, filters and job-pin exclusion remain unchanged. Actual producer and H513 regressions pass. | <a id="h441"></a>H441 | Getting back into a conversation: continue, resume, and a free recap | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Getting back into a conversation now comes with a free recap, rendered locally from the stored turns and never by a model, on resume, as /recap in every channel and in the HUD. render_recap (agents/core/memory/recap.py:80) groups a session's turns into exchanges (an owner turn and the replies that follow it), keeps the last 10 by default (DEFAULT_EXCHANGES, agents/core/memory/recap.py:24; at most 50, MAX_EXCHANGES, agents/core/memory/recap.py:25), and puts each turn on one line of plain text, whitespace folded, control and bidi characters removed and cut to 280 characters with an ellipsis (_clean, agents/core/memory/recap.py:35; the raw text is cut to four times the limit before cleaning, so a huge stored turn costs no more than a short one). A reply's tools collapse to a count and their distinct names, "\[3 tool calls: web_search, web_fetch\]" (tool_line, agents/core/memory/recap.py:57), names only, never arguments or results, and an owner turn never shows one. The answer carries the exchanges as data and the same recap as text lines (● you / ◆ the agent) with "the last n of m exchanges" when some are left out. The tools had to be recorded first: turns persisted only role, content, agent, time and tokens. The orchestrator opens a per-turn collection when an owner turn arrives (turn_tools.begin, agents/core/memory/turn_tools.py:23; agents/core/orchestrator.py:2014 and :2216), the agent's tool-event sink notes each finished call's tool (turn_tools.note, agents/core/memory/turn_tools.py:30, from the sink's record, agents/core/agent.py:903) while still landing every event in the trail, and the reply that closes the turn is stored with the names (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3621) on Turn.tools (Turn, agents/core/memory/conversation.py:36; a stored value that is not a list of names reads as none, _tool_names, agents/core/memory/conversation.py:62), persisted, reloaded at start, on resume and on an explicit session load (session_continuation._load_locked, agents/core/session_continuation.py:285), carried in a continuation's immutable seed (seed_json, agents/core/session_continuation.py:89), and absent from a turn that called none. The collection is a context variable, so concurrent turns in other sessions never mix, and a tool loop run through asyncio.to_thread still lands in its own turn. POST /sessions/resume answers with "recap" beside the last 20 raw turns it always returned (agents/core/routers/sessions.py:197), rendered from the whole stored session. /recap \[n\] (_recap, agents/core/commands.py:375; registered at agents/core/commands.py:408) answers in any admitted channel from that channel's own session (a chat's session is its own, pinned by the channel handler); on the owner's shared session (a widget visitor, an agent-target webhook, an MCP caller, or any channel with memory.cross_channel_sessions on) it answers only the owner and refuses anyone else before reading a turn, with n exchanges (at most 50; anything else, a non-ASCII digit included, is the default ten), and leaves out its own /recap line, recognised by the command parser in any case (/Recap, /recap@bot). The HUD's Sessions panel (SessionsPanel, frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx:51) now shows the recap after a resume (RecapView, frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx:30; toolLine, frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx:23) as text, never markup, names a refused resume and drops the old recap then, and says "resumed \<id\>" when a hub sends none. tests/test_h441_session_recap.py (22 cases) covers the renderer (last exchanges and the count, tool lines, one bounded line, odd input, no model), the collection (terminal events only, names only, concurrent turns and a worker thread, no turn open, a caller's copy), the agent's sink, the turn's tools stored and reloaded at start and on resume, the manager passing them down, the resume route's recap with no model call, /recap in a guest's channel with a count, with nothing and with no memory, /help listing it, and the orchestrator storing a turn's tools; the review round added /recap refused to a widget visitor, a webhook and MCP through the real routes while the owner still gets it, a guest on the shared session refused before anything is read, the /recap line left out in any case, a non-ASCII digit, a huge turn cut before cleaning, and tools kept through prepare_session and a continued session's seed, resume and next turn; frontend/src/test/sessions-recap.test.tsx (3) covers the panel. 28 mutants (24 Python, 4 HUD), all caught, three after their cases were added (an owner turn with tools, a caller's copy of the list, the reload on resume). Test manual: CHT-118 (and CHT-011 names the recap). Adapted from Hermes: no per-terminal breadcrumbs for -c/--continue (they solve a multi-pane CLI problem Nerva does not have), and the recap is served to the HUD and the chat channels rather than drawn as a terminal panel. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. | <a id="h444"></a>H444 | Reading documents into the conversation | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The part this row says is worth copying is missing. A scanned (image-only) PDF extracts to empty or near-empty text, and file_read returns that as a normal successful read, with no coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text. No size ceiling applies to a document: the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned, where Hermes refuses documents over 50 MB. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. The wider format matrix (.ipynb, .xlsx, legacy Office, OpenDocument, .rtf, .epub) and Markdown conversion are not built; the row's rationale does not ask for them. | <a id="h450"></a>H450 | Say when in plain words (schedule syntax) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Plain-words scheduling now covers the four families Hermes accepts that the job store did not. It also no longer turns a one-shot said in words into a daily job. (1) One-shots. nl_schedule.parse_schedule (agents/core/autonomy/nl_schedule.py:196) takes a now and a zone and answers a one-shot as {ok, at (UTC ISO), description} (_parse_one_shot, :120). It accepts a delay: in 30m, in 1h30m, in 2 hours and 5 minutes, in an hour, peste 2 ore, în 10 minute, peste 2 zile (_duration, :85). It accepts an ISO timestamp (2026-10-01 09:00, anchored to the zone, or with Z / ±HH:MM kept). And it accepts a day word with a time (tomorrow at 9, mâine la 9, today at 18:00, azi la 18, once at 9am, o dată la 9, the next 9 o'clock). A one-shot must be at least one minute ahead and within a year (MAX_ONE_SHOT_AHEAD, :29, _one_shot, :101); a passed time, a date without a time, an impossible date, a bad time, and a delay with anything else after it ("in 2 hours at 9") are refused by name. So 2026-10-01 09:00, tomorrow at 9 and once at 9am are no longer armed as the daily cron 0 9 \* \* \*; a bare time with no day word still means daily, as documented. resolve_schedule (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:271) stores a one-shot in the cron column as @at \<UTC ISO\> (ONE_SHOT_PREFIX, :229; is_one_shot, run_at, :233-238). The store anchors times said in words to the scheduler's zone, which the runner binds (_schedule_zone, :990; :1126). schedule_trigger (:249) gives a DateTrigger for it. register arms a date job (:1184). tick fires it in the minute it falls in (:1279). slot_timing gives its one slot and none after (:1347). A one-shot runs once. Job.runnable is false after one attempt (:131). reserve_attempt refuses a second (:1004). _fire_once reserves the one run before any executor, script jobs included, and records a later firing as skipped: "one-shot already ran" (:1495). register_all does not re-arm a spent one-shot. Giving it a new time re-arms it (_reset_attempts, :981), and a rename does not. It stays out of H687's first run even when first_run is asked for (first_run_policy and first_run_decision answer "one-shot", :563, :589), so it never spends its only run early. The confirmation names its time (arm_confirmation, :614), and as_dict carries one_shot and run_at (:157). /remind in 30m \| … works, and its usage says so. (2) Compact intervals and bare durations: every 2h, every 30m, 2h, 30m, la fiecare 2 ore (_COMPACT, _interval, nl_schedule.py:174-177). 120 minutes becomes every 2 hours. An interval a cron cannot keep (every 45 minutes fired at :00 and :45; 7h, 48h) is refused with the divisors to use. (3) Repeat normaliser: normalize_repeat (jobs.py:410) maps forever/unlimited/always/∞/mereu/la nesfârșit to unlimited, once/1x/x1/o dată to 1, twice/de două ori to 2, and Nx/N times/de N ori/N to N (1-10000). Anything else is refused. validate_options applies it, so options.repeat takes the words on every surface. (4) The HUD jobs panel shows a one-shot by its time (once at …, the viewer's clock) instead of @at, and shows a spent one as done (onceAt, frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:30, :62). Tests: tests/test_h450_schedule_words.py (fixed now and Europe/Bucharest zone; store and runner with a fake scheduler; the fallback ticker; script jobs), frontend jobs.test.tsx and jobs-first-run.test.ts. 62 mutants, all caught after fourteen cases were added. One survivor was a real bug: "in 2 hours at 9" read as a daily 9 o'clock; it is now refused. | <a id="h464"></a>H464 | Park a run on real async work instead of poking it | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Not reachable in production yet: - \*\*No process producer (owner decision 1; blocked on H302).\*\* Nothing in the hub starts run-owned background work and registers its pid: register_process has no caller outside tests. The best first producer is \`terminal_run\` with \`background: true\` on the local-host transport. It needs WorkRunLedger.run_for_task; a spawn with start_new_session, bounded log files and a held proc.wait() that records the exit code; a lifetime cap and killpg on cap, ESTOP or stop; a reaper for stopped runs; schema, approval-card and parity updates; and a KillMode in deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.service (today a restart kills the child and the barrier clears as \`exited\`, indistinguishable from success). The owner must say whether the task/hook trigger is an accepted adaptation of Hermes's pid/session barrier, or whether a pid wait must be reachable for "equivalent". - \*\*Pid waits are unusable on macOS/BSD.\*\* There is no start token there, so every pid wait is refused \`pid_unprovable\`. The fix belongs in exec_cache.py (ctypes libproc proc_pidinfo(PROC_PIDTBSDINFO)) or in a producer-held Popen handle, and needs macOS hardware or a macOS CI runner to verify. - \*\*The judge's \`wait\` verdict and planner waits are not reachable in production (owner decision 2).\*\* WorkJudge.wait_probe needs a model rubric, and the shipped runtime passes no rubric, model planner or graders. Wiring one needs the owner's choice of local model and provider under H513 (auxiliary_request_scope, strict-local, security.data_training_ack) plus opt-in settings forced off in safe mode. Before any model planner, the rest of the intake arguments must be clamped: since H464c the task's kind is scope-checked as well as Action.kind, but the task's agent, title and payload still reach govern_enqueue unchanged. - \*\*No graders are wired in production.\*\* After the hub park clears, a run idles until its budget ends. Deterministic verifier and judge adapters belong to the company-mode grading work; they must reuse an existing verdict, because _grade has no try/except and a judge that raises after the verifier recorded leaves \`verdict_already_recorded\` / \`tick_failed\` on every later sweep. Deferred: - Wake-on-fire: a parked run resumes at the next scheduled sweep. - A \`job:\<id\>\` trigger. - A process output-pattern trigger (needs the process producer). - An owner-set wait like Hermes's \`/goal wait\` (owner decision 3): a clock or own-task wait only, never an owner-typed pid. - Waiting between checklist steps: the checklist still advances when a step's task is approved, not when it finishes. - A judge-sourced wait reserves no grading margin (only the hub's park does since H464c), so a judge wait that runs to the end of the budget still leaves the run ungraded. It is reachable only with a judge wait probe (owner decision 2). - The grading margin bounds one run's way back to a sweep, not a queue: with several runs due at once (max_concurrent 1), a parked run can lose the sweep's one slot after its cap. - A blocked run whose budget runs out is not settled (H464d settles only runs the sweep skips as budget_spent; a blocked one is skipped as blocked and its resume is refused once the owner answers), so it stays blocked on the record. - The HUD's live wait credit for an open ask runs to now; if the owner then edits the ask, the durable credit falls back to the last sweep that saw it open (at most one sweep's worth). - An owner stop of a working run is never closed out: request_stop moves it to \`stopping\` and settles only a second stop (agents/core/autonomy/work_runs.py:1382-1384), while ScheduleRuntime.due skips a \`stopping\` run (agents/core/autonomy/schedule_runtime.py:227-228), so the supervisor that would settle it is never ticked and the run reads \`stopping\` until the owner stops it again (reproduced on 03643762). Needs network: re-checking Hermes's goal-wait behaviour at the pinned commit 59b2aeef (hermes_cli/goals.py was not among the inspected upstream sources). Review residuals after round 3 (latent or bounded): a dropped grading sweep is not absorbed by the margin; a transient queue read during the per-sweep wait observation cuts approval-wait credit; a hold open when a run stops is never ended; an expired ask would be re-asked; the upgrade note's 're-approve' advice needs a new card; four test gaps (docs/handoff/2026-09-28-handover/h464-round3-brief.md). [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/run_barriers.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/work_runs.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy/schedule_runtime.py) |
| <a id="h465"></a>H465 | Run the self-improvement review on demand | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 and fixed after six adversarial reviews: review-H465 (one major, seven minors, seven nits), review-H465b (three minors, six nits: a per-turn pass could still run beside /refine and borrow its label, a cut-off review read as nothing, and four mutants were unpinned) and review-H465c (three minors, six nits: a per-turn review cut off by the token cap was refunded, so a model that always truncates reviewed every turn; the settings the replies named could not be set; four survivors) and review-H465d (two minors, seven nits: a day of cut-offs made /refine name the wrong setting, seven real survivors, and a GA4 private key the CLI printed unmasked) and review-H465e (two minors, seven nits: the cut-off refusal on a day of mostly real reviews, nine real survivors, a budget read outside its bounds, a secret echoed by nerva config set, a pass in flight at midnight counted on the next day, a burst of turns past the cadence) and review-H465f (seven nits: a /refine refund keyed on the wrong day, a burst given too few passes, a later bracket closing an unclosed error, a fraction below one read as reviews off). All are fixed. Hermes' /refine \[focus\] forks the background memory and skill review against a snapshot of the conversation, so the live session is untouched. It refuses while a turn is in flight and reports the new or updated memories and skills back into the chat. Nerva's review engine is the per-turn BackgroundReviewer (strict-local, budgeted); it now also runs on demand. The command: - /refine \[focus\] is an owner command (agents/core/commands.py:350, registered at agents/core/commands.py:416). It answers from any channel's owner, and a guest is refused like any owner command. - The focus is one line of at most 200 characters. - It replies with what the review kept, one line per action (profile or core memory updated, corrections recorded, skill patch proposed, new skill quarantined, facts found but not kept because living memory is off). A skill change is said to wait in the Decision Inbox, and a new skill in the pending skills list. - Every non-run is named: a review already running, the daily budget spent, no local model answering (reviews never leave the machine), a model that spent its whole answer thinking (review_cut_off), a review cut off or malformed (review_unparsed, which names learning.review_max_tokens), reviews switched off (a daily budget of 0, reviews_off), the review outlasting its bound, nothing to review, no reviewer. An empty reply, a cut one, or a JSON object carrying none of the review's keys ({} or {"error": …}) is unparsed; only an object that says it found nothing reads as nothing worth keeping. A reply that parses as a review is the model's answer, whatever it opens with or mentions; only a reply that does not parse and is the local degraded message or opens with a bracketed \[… error …\] closed on its own line by its own bracket is a backend failure; an unclosed "\[Note: … error", or one a later \[1\] or link would close, is prose (review-H465e nit 7, review-H465f nit 3). - A local backend that is configured but down answers with a degraded string rather than raising. That is no review: it is reported as no local model and costs no budget (review-H465 M-1). A cut-off or malformed /refine costs none either; a per-turn pass cut off the same way does spend its unit, since the model ran to its cap, so the daily budget still bounds local GPU use when a model always truncates, and it is logged at INFO once a day, naming learning.review_max_tokens as the cause of a thinking cut-off and as a possible one of a malformed answer; a /refine cut-off does not use that line up. When such cut-offs are at least half of the day's spent units, /refine answers daily_budget_cut_off and says that, if they were cut off, the token cap is what to raise rather than the budget; one cut-off among real reviews is plain daily_budget (review-H465e m-1). A pass still waiting on the model at midnight refunds or counts against the day it spent its unit on, never the next (review-H465e nit 4), and GET /api/cognition/learning counts cut_offs_today. The unit is spent in run() after the budget is checked again there, so a burst of passes that all saw budget left in should_run cannot pass it; the cadence is checked there again too, so a burst of turns cannot pass it either (review-H465e nit 6), and a pass uses up only the turns that admitted it, so a burst gets the passes the same turns one at a time would (review-H465f nit 2) (_cut_off, agents/core/learning/background_review.py:513). - It runs whether or not the per-turn learning loop (cognition.review_enabled) is on: the owner asked, as Hermes' manual /refine does. Orchestrator.refine (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3476): - It reads a snapshot of the session's history and never writes to it. - The snapshot is conversation_snapshot (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:98): the newest turns whole and contiguous, each cut at 2,000 characters, up to 12,000 characters in all. Slash commands and the hub's replies to them are left out, so a chat of commands only has nothing to review. This replaces the per-turn review's last-6-turns, 300-character window for this path. - In flight, as it really behaves: every path that dispatches /refine holds the session's turn lease first. A /refine sent while another turn runs therefore waits for that turn (up to the lease's 180-second bound) and then reviews the conversation as it stands; it is not refused (review-H465 m-1 corrected the earlier claim). The review itself is bounded at 150 seconds (REFINE_TIMEOUT_S), below that wait, so the chat is busy for at most that long. - A direct caller holding no lease while another turn does is refused turn_in_flight: the lock is locked and the key is not among this context's held leases (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3496). No new in-flight set was added (critic note 1). - A review's result is the reviewer's last_result, which GET /api/cognition/learning shows (reviewer.status()); a refusal does not overwrite it. Each action is logged. The learning loop's knobs are declared settings (category learning, agents/core/settings_db.py:311): review_max_tokens (512, 1–32768), review_daily_budget (20, 0–1000), review_cadence (every_turn, every_n_turns, idle_gap), review_every_n (3), review_idle_gap_s (90) and review_max_facts (3), with the defaults the code already used, bounded in _LEARNING_INT_BOUNDS (agents/core/settings_db.py:758). The admin settings API, the HUD and nerva config set can change them; nerva config get shows a number named …max_tokens as the number it is, not masked as a credential, and masks every key the settings store encrypts (the GA4 service-account JSON among them), and nerva config set echoes such a value masked too (review-H465e nit 3). A model id is masked only under a hinted name: llm.default_model shows as typed. Every knob reads within its declared bounds whatever the stored row (a row from before the bounds, restored or hand-edited, reads as the default), the review's model call's token cap included (bounded_learning_int, agents/core/settings_db.py:773). A declared 0 for review_max_facts or review_idle_gap_s is honoured, not read as unset; corrections have a cap of their own (3), so a review_max_facts of 0 keeps no facts, still records corrections, and /refine says what it found but did not keep (_number, agents/core/learning/background_review.py:293). BackgroundReviewer.run_on_demand (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:335): - It skips the cadence gate but spends the daily budget, rolling over at midnight, and refunds it when no review was had (a model down, a cut-off answer, a timeout, a cancellation), on the day the pass spent it (review-H465f nit 1). A budget of 0 means no reviews and /refine says so (reviews_off); 2.5 reads as 2, and a value that is not a number or lies outside 0–1000 (text, infinity, a boolean, a hand-edited -3, a fraction below one) is logged once and 20 used (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:268). A per-turn pass that does not run leaves a DEBUG line naming why. It keeps the strict-local model, and is bounded at REFINE_TIMEOUT_S (150 s, below the lease's 180-second wait); no setting raises it. - It neither resets nor delays the per-turn cadence. It runs one at a time and never beside a per-turn review: a /refine while a per-turn pass runs is refused busy, and a per-turn pass that would start while /refine runs is skipped, not deferred. The exclusion is taken in run() itself (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:394), so a per-turn pass spawned before the /refine, still waiting on the memory lock, is refused on_demand when it enters. A cancelled review leaves no busy flag. - Its proposals, approval cards and new skills are labelled refine, not background_review; the label is passed down per pass, never kept on the instance. An injection-flagged correction is not recorded (on both paths). Facts found while living memory is off are counted after the injection scan, and said only to the owner who asked. - run gains a focus slot in REVIEW_PROMPT ("Focus for this review: …", absent when there is none, so the per-turn prompt is byte-identical) and a context bound, which this path sets to the snapshot's. Tests: tests/test_h465_refine.py (35 cases), tests/test_h465c_refine_review.py (9, the second review), tests/test_h465d_refine_review.py (31, the third review), tests/test_h465e_refine_review.py (37, the fourth review), tests/test_h465f_refine_review.py (34, the fifth review), tests/test_h465g_refine_review.py (18, the sixth review), tests/test_nerva_cli.py (a token budget shown unmasked), and tests/test_slash_commands.py pins the command in the owner's list. They cover: - the focus slot and its bound; - cadence skipped while the budget is spent, and the day's rollover; - one review at a time, and facts written; - the snapshot's bound, order, cutting and junk; - a whole snapshot reaching the prompt; - the reviewer receiving this session's snapshot while the session stays unchanged; - busy while another turn holds the lease, and not busy inside the command's own lease; - the empty and unavailable cases; - the owner-only command and its report, each named refusal, and /help; - the review's cases: a degraded reply is no review and costs no budget; the timeout; a cancelled review; mutual exclusion and the cadence kept; facts found with living memory off; an injected correction; the refine label; commands left out and contiguity; a chat of commands only; a refusal keeping the last result; the focus cut and where a new skill waits; a guest told /refine is the owner's. Mutation: 20 mutants; 18 were caught at once, and the other two after cases were added (the snapshot's bound reaching the prompt, the day rollover). The review's 28 mutants left four real survivors (a cancelled review's flag, the snapshot's contiguity, a refusal overwriting the last result, the focus cut); all are now caught. The first round's 19 mutants were all caught. The second review's 29 mutants left four real survivors (a new skill's label, a setting raising the bound, a garbage bound setting, a timeout's stored result): the bound setting is gone and the other two have cases. The third round's 14 mutants were all caught. The third review's 36 mutants (29 killed) left four real survivors (an empty reply, a reply cut after an inner }, a thinking cut-off's stored result, a cancelled /refine staying cancelled); all four have cases. That round's 22 mutants: 21 caught, one equivalent (an explicit unset check the except branch already covers, since removed). The fourth review's 40 mutants left seven real survivors (the documented values accepted, a search for an error anywhere, a /refine cut-off using up the day's line, a skip reason other than on_demand); each has a case, the day's line after a /refine only since the fifth round. That round's 19 mutants were all caught, two after their cases were added. The fifth review's 51 mutants left nine real survivors (the cut-off refusal's threshold, which pass logs the day's line, the orchestrator's bounded token cap, the rollover of cut_offs_today in both entry points, a /refine cut-off counted among them, the corrections cap, the found-not-kept count against injected and agent facts); each has a case. That round's 20 mutants were all caught. The sixth review's 40 mutants left two real survivors (a regex closed by a later bracket or line), each with a case now. This round's 6 mutants: four caught, two equivalent (the refund's day check and its reset, since nothing rolls the day while a /refine runs, and a /refine refunds at most once). Local auxiliary integration review (2026-10-02): _review_llm uses JARVIS_AUX_REVIEW_MODEL through the common local helper. Blank preserves active model then google/gemma-4-31b-a4b; bounded learning tokens,JSON system prompt,initial temperature0.2 and review lifecycle/output rules are unchanged. The selected model is passed to the existing policy scope and generator. Prepared auxiliary helper review (2026-10-02): this consumer still delegates through generate_local_auxiliary; model selection, default, prompt/budget, Qwen behavior where applicable and H513 await/stream lifetime are preserved. The new preparation layer captures one route and rechecks the fixed job-pin policy at invocation. Existing cited suites and new late-pin regressions pass in the frozen full backend; no capability promotion. Temperature recovery qualification (2026-10-02): review initially requests0.2; a typed local rejection may omit only temperature once. Learning-token bounds, JSON prompts, review state and validators remain unchanged. Real review producer and focused lifecycle regressions pass. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Image-turn confirmation review (2026-10-02): the agents/cli/nerva.py diff adds selection flags only to chat image turns, rejects them on text chat, validates optional bounded requirements and forwards explicit flags. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. Nous account collateral review (2026-10-02): compared the complete diff from 7a73bf03. Existing CLI command implementations are unchanged; only auth parser/dispatch registration is additive. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. Existing OAuth handler bodies are unchanged; four separate owner-only Nous routes and documentation are added. All pre-edit evidence hashes matched the prior checkpoint. This preserves the existing verdict without claiming new capability completion; the 266-pass/3-skip integration suite includes existing OAuth, secrets and route gates. ## security

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h481"></a>H481 | Refuse a catastrophic shell command no matter who approved it (hardline floor + risky-pattern tier + de-obfuscation) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The middle tier does not exist.\*\* The row asks for three stacked layers; this is the first, with its matching repaired (and repaired again in PR #1177). There is still no ~40-rule severity/reversibility-tagged risky-pattern catalogue feeding the approval card's description, so \`rm -rf ~/projects\` is neither hardline nor pattern-classified — re-checked 2026-09-19 on 494a13b2: \`hardline_match("rm -rf ~/projects")\` and \`hardline_match(\['rm','-rf','/home/x/projects'\])\` both return None, and the module carries no severity/reversibility/catalogue vocabulary at all — and the owner's card still says "run this argv" rather than "recursive delete", which the row's rationale names as the point. No user-editable \`approvals.deny\` fnmatch list at hardline level (no \`fnmatch\`/\`deny\` in the module). No parser limits that themselves hardline-block and save an oversized payload to a file for manual review (128k chars / 4096 separator-free / 25k segments); \`MAX_ARG_CHARS = 4000\` is argv validation, not a refusal. \*\*Variants Hermes produces and this still does not:\*\* NFKC normalisation, \`$HOME\`/\`$HERMES_HOME\` folding, grep-pattern span masking, and \`python -c\` / \`perl -e\` / \`node -e\` payload extraction — the last left alone on purpose, since \`jobs_scripts.snapshot_script\` legitimately builds \`\[sys.executable, '-I', '-c', source\]\`. \`$()\`/backtick substitution is now extracted in ONE place only: #1177's \`_substituted\` narrows a heredoc BODY line to its substitutions, so \`cat \<\<EOF\` / \`$(rm -rf /)\` / \`EOF\` refuses; an ordinary line is still not expanded, so \`echo $(rm -rf /)\` and a backtick \`rm -rf /\` inside an assignment return None (a \`$(mkfs.ext4 …)\` is caught only through \`_CMD\`'s \`(\` anchor). The row's own examples \`$(printf mkfs).ext4 /dev/sda\` and \`echo bWtmcw==\|base64 -d\|sh\` still return None. \*\*Gaps this module knows it still has, listed honestly rather than as a complete set:\*\* nesting deeper than \`_MAX_SHELL_DEPTH\` (depth-3 \`sh -c "sh -c 'sh -c …'"\` returns None, pinned by test); a quote at a word BOUNDARY (\`'mkfs.ext4' /dev/sda\`) rather than inside a word; \`powershell -EncodedCommand\` base64; a \`cmd /c\` payload split across several argv items; an obfuscated shell HEAD in argv form (\`\['s""h','-c',…\]\`), because the de-obfuscation pass runs after payload extraction and its output is not fed back through it; and, since #1177, two in the false-refusal direction — a \`case\` label with a leading \`(\` (\`(shutdown) echo bye ;;\`) is still read as a command, and a \`case\` nested inside another is not modelled: its inner first label IS stripped (\`case $1 in\\na) case $2 in\\nreboot) echo r ;;\\nesac ;;\\nesac\` went power_cycle→None), so the docstring's 'not handled' is about the region boundary, not a guaranteed refusal. \*\*Refusals #1177 cleared in the dangerous direction — closed by this slice (H481, 2026-09-22).\*\* The \`case\`-label strip fires only inside a real command-position \`case WORD in … esac\` region now, so \`(echo case in; reboot); echo esac\` and the \`$IFS\`-spliced \`(echo case in; mkfs.ext4$IFS/dev/sda); echo esac\` / \`wipefs$IFS-a$IFS/dev/sda\` refuse again; and \`_heredoc_feeds_a_shell\` now also sees a body a shell consumes AFTER the redirection through a pipe (\`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| bash\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| grep … \| sh\`) or via a file a shell later runs (\`cat \<\<'EOF' \>x.sh; sh x.sh\`), so those refuse — and, after the review round, so do \`(sh x.sh)\`, \`{ sh x.sh; }\`, \`if true; then sh x.sh; fi\`, \`cat x.sh \| sh\`, \`sh -c '. x.sh'\`, \`. x.sh\`, \`source x.sh\`, \`tee x.sh \<\<EOF; sh x.sh\`, a quoted \`\> "x.sh"\` target, \`sh /dev/stdin \<\<EOF\`, \`bash -c sh \<\<EOF\` and \`cat \<\<EOF \| eval "$(cat)"\`, each verified to run the body. Pinned in tests/test_local_transport.py, reproduced against /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash first, and screened by a 671,995-case differential fuzz vs origin/main whose every difference is explained by the two changed mechanisms (numbers in the summary; the earlier \`refusals_cleared = 0\` claim is withdrawn). Still open in this direction, listed in \`_detection_variants\` and pinned as misses: a heredoc body written to a file run by a bare path (\`… \>x.sh; ./x.sh\`), by a variable-carried name (\`for f in x.sh; do sh $f; done\`) or by the environment (\`BASH_ENV=x.sh bash -c :\`); a heredoc opened inside a command substitution (\`eval "$(cat \<\<EOF …)"\`, read as quoted text); \`xargs -I{} sh -c '{}'\` (each line becomes the command line); and a \`;\` inside a \`( )\` group of a pipe stage (\`cat \<\<EOF \| (cd /tmp; sh)\`). \*\*Two pre-existing false positives are unchanged\*\* (\`git commit -m "fix;mkfs.ext4 handling"\` → mkfs; \`grep -r "(shutdown" docs/\` → power_cycle), and \`_PATH_PREFIX\` added a third class no approval can lift: a path to a script whose basename starts with a hardline name — \`./mkfs.sh\` (mkfs), \`./reboot.sh\`, \`./scripts/shutdown.sh --dry-run\`, \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` (power_cycle) — all None on 1c5054ea, all refused on 494a13b2 (bare \`reboot.sh\` already refused before; the prefix extends it to every path spelling). This slice left that (c) class unclosed on purpose: any name- or path-only rule that cleared \`./mkfs.sh\` or \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` would also clear a TRUE refusal — \`./mkfs.sh\` is structurally \`mkfs.\<suffix\>\` exactly like the real \`/sbin/mkfs.ext4\`, and \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` is \`\<path\>/\<hardline-name\>\` exactly like a copied \`/tmp/mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` — so it needs approval-context (is the target a workspace script or a real system binary) the pure static floor does not have. \*\*And a measured cost, re-measured after #1177 removed the breadth cap\*\* (\`_MAX_SHELL_EXPANSIONS\` and the \`MAX_ARG_CHARS\` re-expansion guard are gone; every level-1 payload is unwrapped again): on a maximal 64x4000-character argv the pass is roughly unchanged (79.6 ms on the pre-#1177 module vs 82.3 ms after, same machine, best of 5), but a command made of 2,000 nested \`sh -c "sh -c 'echo ok'"\` segments (57k chars) goes from 196 ms to 380 ms — synchronous, on the event loop, before every exec. The docstring's linear-in-input argument holds (each level's payloads are disjoint substrings); an independent re-run on another machine saw 79.0→80.6 ms and 125→151 ms, so the constant grows for nested input but the factor is machine-dependent and no test pins either number. This slice's first pass claimed no measurable cost on that maximal 64x4000-character argv (78.7 → 78.4 ms); the review showed the claim held only for input with no \`case\` word and never exercised the redirect-target branch, which was quadratic. Re-measured after the review round (best of 3, same machine, origin/main → this branch): the maximal 64x4000 argv 62.0 → 58.6 ms; the same argv filled with case/in prose 18,207 → 105 ms (main's own \`\\bcase\\b.\*?\\besac\\b\` was super-linear there too); 4000 chars of \`case x in\` 21.6 → 6.5 ms; eight 4000-char argv items of prose 316 → 14 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\` blocks (12 KB) 39.8 → 64.9 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<E \>x\` blocks in 4000 chars 25 → 39 ms (2,471 ms on the first pass); 1,600 such blocks (33.6 KB) 132 → 201 ms (97.5 s on the first pass); 2,000 nested \`sh -c\` segments 146 → 148 ms; 300 real \`case\` statements 57 → 60 ms. The heredoc-with-target shapes cost about 1.5x main, linear, because the statement lines after a heredoc are tokenised once and shared; nothing else pays. Six test items pin wall-clock bounds (0.25–2 s) two orders of magnitude above these; no test pins a ratio. \*\*Adversarial review of the original slice found four more defects, all fixed and each reproduced first\*\* against real /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash: (1) \`_shell_c_payloads\` demanded the word after the wrappers BE the shell, so \`env FOO=1 sh -c 'mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda'\` — and \`sudo -u root sh -c …\`, \`nice -n 10 sh -c …\` — skipped the whole \`sh -c\` screening while deleting \`FOO=1 \` made the same string \`hardline_denied:mkfs\`; \`_CMD\` had been widened for that assignment prefix and the unwrapper had not. (2) \`_unquoted_lines\` read a backslash-newline as a statement separator when it is a line CONTINUATION, so \`mk\\\` + newline + \`fs.ext4 /dev/sda\`, which all three shells splice into \`mkfs.ext4\`, was seen as two harmless fragments. (3) every heredoc body line became a command position, so \`cat \> x.yaml \<\<'EOF'\` / \`shutdown: graceful\` / \`EOF\` was refused as \`power_cycle\`; body lines were joined as data in the flat text. \*\*#1177's second review showed that fix was incomplete in three ways, all closed there:\*\* \`segments\` still tokenised every body line, so \`cat \> README.md \<\<'EOF'\` / \`rm -rf / will destroy the box\` / \`EOF\` stayed a \`recursive_root_removal\` refusal; the rule had no exception for a heredoc a SHELL consumes, so \`bash \<\<EOF\` / \`mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` / \`EOF\` returned None (a bypass the heredoc work itself opened); and any \`\<\<\` — inside quotes, inside \`$(( ))\`, a \`\<\<\<\` here-string, or with a delimiter that never returns — opened a heredoc and switched the newline screening off for every following line. (4) \`{\` anchored bare, so \`jq '{reboot: .needs_reboot}'\` was a \`power_cycle\` refusal; POSIX requires whitespace after \`{\` for a brace group, and \`{ mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda; }\` is still refused. Each fix is red-proofed on its own. [d1](../agents/core/environments/terminal_contract.py) [d2](../tests/test_local_transport.py) |

| <a id="h484"></a>H484 | One dial that sets how much Nerva may do without asking | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h485"></a>H485 | A second, cheap model opinion on a flagged action before bothering the human | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Hermes's cheap guardian runs before human escalation and returns a strict APPROVE/DENY/ESCALATE decision for exactly one flagged command. Nerva's judge is advisory only: no pre-card disposition, no one-command grant through kernel.authorize, and no strict tri-state parser or operator smart-policy system-channel contract. | <a id="h495"></a>H495 | Never let a credential reach a log, a transcript, an approval card or an export | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | This is one of the row's seven paths. Hermes applies \`redact.py\` to logs, verbose output, tool output, approval prompts, gateway messages, transcripts and memory; this slice covers LOGS. Still owed, and the rationale calls them the part that matters most: the hash-chained audit DB (a \`cat .env\` from \`terminal_run\` still writes a live credential into an append-only log that by design cannot be edited), the decision-inbox approval card the owner reads on a phone, task payloads, terminal output, and the observability exports — which Hermes scrubs unconditionally with no knob, returning \`\[redaction-unavailable\]\` rather than raw text. Also absent: the second mask mode (a syntactically INVALID \`«redacted:ghp_…»\` sentinel for file reads, so an agent cannot write a masked-but-plausible key back and silently kill the credential), \`code_file=True\` sparing source constants, \`_mask_control_split_tokens()\` for tokens smuggled across ANSI/zero-width characters, and the shared \`.env\` basename list. The two silent-degradation paths named at review are now CLOSED, after the SAST gate forced the question: a handler whose \`addFilter\` fails is counted and published through \`uncovered_handler_count()\` and a warning naming how many handlers are writing unredacted, and a \`loggerDict\` walk that raises warns that coverage fell back to root-only. The one silent swallow left is the filter's own last-ditch blank (\`# nosec B110\`), where a \`logging.Filter\` that raises would take down the caller's log call and the scanner has already failed. \`install_log_redaction()\` remains exported although the \`everywhere\` variant is what production uses. \*\*A later slice's full-suite run surfaced one more thing about this control, recorded here rather than fixed:\*\* the scanner's \`high_entropy_secret\` pattern matches an ordinary filesystem path. Under \`-n auto --dist loadfile\` a warning naming a pytest tmp file arrived as \`truncated: \[REDACTED:high_entropy_secret\].md\`, which is what a real operator would see when a log line names a file with a long random-looking segment — a debuggability cost the module docstring already warns about in general terms, now with a concrete case. Separately, \`setup_logging()\` installs the filter on every handler of every logger and nothing took it back off, so every later test on the same xdist worker ran under a live redactor. The installer is PRODUCTION code, not the tests: \`agents/run.py\` calls \`setup_logging()\` at module level, so importing it installs the filter during pytest COLLECTION — before any fixture runs — and eight test files import it; \`agents/web.py\` calls it from the FastAPI lifespan, so any test that drives the app installs it again; five test files call it directly on top of that. There is no list of leaking files to fix. The cleanup therefore lives in \`tests/conftest.py\` as an autouse fixture that strips unconditionally BEFORE each test as well as after — a snapshot-and-restore fixture, which is what the two neighbouring isolation fixtures do and what this was first written as, treats a collection-time install as pre-existing and preserves it for the life of the worker. That version went green locally and RED in CI on exactly the test it was meant to fix. The first attempt at it matched by \`isinstance\` and silently did nothing: \`agents/\` is on \`sys.path\`, so \`core.security.log_redaction\` and \`agents.core.security.log_redaction\` are two module objects over this one file with two distinct \`SecretRedactionFilter\` classes, and the root handler was carrying the other one. The match is by class name now, and four paired tests pin both halves — including the alternate import path, which is the case the first attempt missed. | <a id="h502"></a>H502 | Refuse an MCP server entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume, and never hand one the owner's keys | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The documented per-server \`env\` migration path does not exist as a reachable surface.\*\* \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field (\`routers/mcp.py:131-143\`, no \`model_config\`), \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` drops the key (pydantic's default \`extra="ignore"\`), and neither \`to_config\` (\`client.py:798-812\`) nor \`load_from_config\` (\`:814-849\`) writes or reads one — the module docstring says as much at \`client.py:41-45\`. The only remedy an owner can actually reach is the \*\*host-wide\*\* \`JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV\` (\`:92-98\`, "Host-wide, not per-server"), which is a strictly weaker grant than the per-server one the docs describe: an owner whose forge server needs \`GITHUB_TOKEN\` must allow it for every stdio server on the host. Whether to ship on that or build the per-server surface first is a product decision, written up as \`docs/OWNER_TASKS.md\` §P28.3 with both options. \*\*Related and also owner-level:\*\* \`XAUTHORITY\` and \`DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS\` are both on \`STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST\` (\`client.py:169\`), and today one is listed-but-silently-dropped (\`_SECRET_LIKE\` at \`:174-176\` matches the \`AUTH\` in \`XAUTHORITY\`) while the other is listed-and-handed-over — they should be consistent in one direction or the other. \*\*And the honest ceiling, stated wherever the guarantee is:\*\* this is \*\*defence in depth, not a security boundary\*\*, and since #1179 the module states that ceiling by TWO routes rather than one (\`client.py:17-36\`). The child is same-UID with no namespace, so a server that can run a subprocess reads the withheld variables straight out of \`/proc/\<ppid\>/environ\`; and the baseline narrows the \*\*filesystem\*\* not at all — \`cwd\` defaults to the hub's own working directory, so \`open(".env")\` hands back exactly the keys that were withheld, which \`cwd_secret_files()\` now says out loud at connect (WARNING, filenames only, \`client.py:565-571\`) rather than leaving the owner to infer a protection from the withheld count alone. What the baseline buys is that a \*careless\* or \*curious\* server does not get them by accident, and that an owner's \`JARVIS_\*\` settings are not ambient in a third party's process. It does not stop an adversary, and the row says so rather than implying otherwise; closing the filesystem route as well as the \`/proc\` one needs real process isolation (separate UID, namespace or container — \`client.py:33-36\`), which is not shipped. \*\*Unaddressed from the row:\*\* there is no refusal of an MCP entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume — no command allow-listing, no signature or provenance check on the spawned binary (the docstring itself calls the screener "still-unshipped", \`client.py:34\`), and \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` still stores \`req.command\` verbatim after checking only transport, trust tier, tool-filter shape and bounds, and duplicate name (\`routers/mcp.py:174-227\`, \`:184\`); the only shape check is \`_command_argv()\`'s shell-metacharacter/NUL refusal at connect (\`client.py:519-529\`), which is not a screener. #1179 changed none of this — it explicitly chose disclosure over refusal. This slice hardens what the child \*receives\*; the row also asks who the child is allowed to \*be\*. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py) |
| <a id="h503"></a>H503 | Unpack an archive from another machine without letting it write outside the destination | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | archive_safe is the only module in Nerva that reads an archive. Backup verify and restore unpack through extract_tar_path (agents/core/archive_safe.py:573; agents/core/backup.py:598, agents/core/backup.py:667). Marketplace install_from_zip unpacks through extract_zip_bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:637; agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:816). The old backup._safe_extract and the marketplace _safe_targets+extractall pass are gone. An AST scan (tests/test_archive_safe.py:702, with probes at tests/test_archive_safe.py:688) fails when any other product module imports tarfile or zipfile outside a write-only allowlist, opens an archive for reading or calls an extract-family method. normalize_member (agents/core/archive_safe.py:122) folds backslash to '/' first. It then refuses empty, NUL, POSIX-absolute, Windows-absolute, UNC and drive-lettered names, and any '..' or empty part. Only regular files and directories are written. Symlink, hardlink, device, fifo and sparse tar members raise (agents/core/archive_safe.py:504), and so do symlink, device, fifo, socket and encrypted zip entries (agents/core/archive_safe.py:596). _Planner (agents/core/archive_safe.py:182) validates every member before the first write: name, type, in-archive conflicts and duplicates, member count and declared bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:257). It also checks the destination's existing content (agents/core/archive_safe.py:286): a link on the way to a member is refused, and so is a directory where a file goes (tests/test_archive_safe.py:365, tests/test_backup.py:471). The pre-scan reads the stream to its end (agents/core/archive_safe.py:522), so a bad gzip CRC, trailing data or a corrupt header part-way through is a refusal, not a shortened tree (tests/test_archive_safe.py:482, tests/test_archive_safe.py:517). Bytes are counted again while streaming (agents/core/archive_safe.py:389). The byte budget is clamped to free space plus the blocks of the single-link files the archive replaces (agents/core/archive_safe.py:314), so an in-place --force restore fits (tests/test_backup.py:443). PAX and GNU long-name header payloads are capped before tarfile reads them, 1 MiB each and 256 MiB in total (agents/core/archive_safe.py:461, agents/core/archive_safe.py:483; tests/test_backup.py:515). A link or hardlinked name at a member's path is unlinked and the file is created O_EXCL\|O_NOFOLLOW, never written through (agents/core/archive_safe.py:373; tests/test_archive_safe.py:323, tests/test_archive_safe.py:349). Backups take their caps from JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_MEMBERS and JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_BYTES (agents/core/backup.py:86); skill packages use SKILL_PACKAGE_LIMITS (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:42). On the producing side, create_backup writes both the encrypted and the plaintext branch through atomic_write, a sibling temp file that is fsynced and then os.replace'd (agents/core/archive_safe.py:654; agents/core/backup.py:415, agents/core/backup.py:420). Archives are GNU-format tar (agents/core/backup.py:474). The export walk skips symlinks (agents/core/archive_safe.py:715; agents/core/backup.py:401). publish_skill uses it on the staged copy it signs (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:392); the copy itself is built from the bytes of one source snapshot, which refuses a linked folder, a link or non-regular member and a file that changed while read (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:375), which has its own test (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:219). Every other name, hardlinked ones included, is archived as its own regular member (agents/core/backup.py:434, agents/core/backup.py:458; tests/test_backup.py:388). verify and restore compare the manifest's file_count with what the archive held (agents/core/backup.py:625; tests/test_backup.py:430). install_from_zip stages inside skills_dir, runs the signature gate there, and only then renames the tree into place (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:854, agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:885). A non-zip upload is a 400 refusal (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:194). Staging left by a killed install is swept once it is an hour old (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:773; tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:203). Restore stays an operator CLI action. It prints 'partial restore' when a failure came after the first write (agents/core/backup.py:716) and 'nothing restored' only for a pre-scan refusal (agents/core/backup.py:722). tests/test_backup.py:606 pins that no product module, script or subprocess caller reaches it. The pre-scan also refuses a component longer than the destination's NAME_MAX or a path past PATH_MAX, which the existing-path walk cannot see under a new directory (agents/core/archive_safe.py:251; tests/test_archive_safe.py:726), and a write the destination refuses mid-restore (permissions, a full disk) is a named ArchiveRejected marked partial, never a traceback (agents/core/archive_safe.py:445; tests/test_archive_safe.py:738). A restore leaves the archive's manifest in the target; create_backup never archives a top-level backup_manifest.json from the data root (agents/core/backup.py:482), so a backup of a restored root carries one manifest and verifies, restores and serves the forget purge's verified pre-snapshot (tests/test_backup.py:636, tests/test_backup.py:675). A backup of a restored root made before this change carries that name twice; verify and restore accept exactly that one repeat, last wins as tarfile resolved it, and count the dropped copy against the manifest's file_count (agents/core/backup.py:599, agents/core/backup.py:642; tests/test_backup.py:651). The repeat is bounded: the dropped copy still counts against the member and byte caps, and a third copy or any other duplicate is refused (agents/core/archive_safe.py:220, agents/core/archive_safe.py:223; tests/test_archive_safe.py:768, tests/test_archive_safe.py:775, tests/test_archive_safe.py:790). Known limit: backups made on main from a data root holding a symlink or hardlink fail verify and restore by design. [d1](../agents/core/archive_safe.py) [d2](../agents/core/backup.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/marketplace.py) |
| <a id="h504"></a>H504 | Fail loudly when TLS trust is misconfigured instead of silently not verifying | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now fails loudly when TLS trust is misconfigured, and verification is never quietly off, as Hermes requires. agents/core/tls_trust.py is the one place that decides what outbound clients verify against; agents/core/http_client.py re-exports its tls_verify for the plugin clients (:180). (1) Boot validation: validate_ca_environment (agents/core/tls_trust.py:170) checks every CA variable that is set, JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE, SSL_CERT_FILE, REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE and CURL_CA_BUNDLE as files and SSL_CERT_DIR as directories, and certifi itself. Each file must exist, be a file, load into an SSLContext and hold at least one certificate: any certificate, so a pinned self-signed server certificate counts (_check_file, :145; _count_certs through cert_store_stats, :137). certifi must also be at least 1 KB and importable. Each problem is reported with the variable, its value and what is wrong. It also carries a copy-pasteable repair for this host's shell (_repair, :107): export X="$(python -m certifi)" or unset X on POSIX, the PowerShell form on Windows, a path to the root for JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE, a certifi reinstall. When H273's provenance says a .env file set the value, the problem names that file (_set_in, :123). enforce_ca_environment (:217) raises SystemExit with every problem at once. The web lifespan runs it right after enforce_boot_posture (agents/web.py:467), before the data home is scaffolded or anything dials out, so a broken SSL_CERT_FILE is no longer an unnamed FileNotFoundError on the first model call. tls_verify's warnings now name the variable that actually held the bad value (SSL_CERT_FILE when that was the source). (2) Model egress on the anchor: llm_async_client (agents/core/llm/egress.py:103-106) gives every model backend's client verify_for(backend, base_url), so the Anthropic, Gemini, OpenRouter, OpenAI Responses, xAI, LM Studio, Ollama and VLM clients all verify against certifi plus JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE (or SSL_CERT_FILE). That is always an explicit SSLContext (trust_context, agents/core/tls_trust.py:94), so a client that keeps trust_env for the owner's proxy never has httpx read SSL_CERT_FILE itself; a caller's own verify or test transport is left alone. (3) Per-target switch: verify_for (:252) returns False only for a provider id or a host named exactly (case-insensitive, trailing dot ignored) in JARVIS_TLS_INSECURE_TARGETS (insecure_targets, :237). Every client built that way logs a WARNING naming its URL, and the start logs the list once. Under JARVIS_HARDENED=1 the list is ignored with an ERROR, and a bad or missing CA path never yields False. docs/FLAGS.md and .env.example document both variables. Local probes that build a plain httpx client (router.py health checks, ollama_control, lmstudio_control, model_setup, local_model_inventory) talk plain HTTP to loopback, and the boot check covers the variables they would read. Tests: tests/test_h504_tls_trust.py (45) uses real certificates made by cryptography (a CA, a leaf, a pinned self-signed leaf, a CRL-only PEM). It also runs end to end against a real HTTPS server on 127.0.0.1 whose certificate only the test CA signs: refused without the owner's root, accepted with JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE or SSL_CERT_FILE, accepted only for a listed target, and refused again when hardened. Auto-vision collateral review (2026-10-02, 876c3e2b): .env.example and docs/FLAGS.md gained auto-route instructions only. The TLS trust implementation, boot guard and HTTPS validation tests were unchanged. Existing equivalent behavior remains the recorded verdict; no new TLS behavior is claimed. | <a id="h507"></a>H507 | Warn the model when the code it just wrote contains a known-dangerous pattern | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Code the agent writes is checked against a table of known-dangerous patterns, and the findings go back to the model and the owner. Nothing is ever refused. agents/core/code_guidance.py holds 26 rules (RULES, agents/core/code_guidance.py:89), each scoped to the file types it applies to. They cover unsafe deserialization (pickle/dill/joblib/marshal loads; yaml.load without SafeLoader, and yaml.unsafe_load; torch.load without weights_only=True); command and code injection (os.system/popen, shell=True, Python eval/exec, JS eval and new Function, child_process.exec and execSync, require('child_process').exec, a bare exec( in a file that imports child_process, a Go exec.Command of a shell); SQL built from an f-string or \`%\`; tempfile.mktemp; XSS sinks (innerHTML/outerHTML assignment, document.write, dangerouslySetInnerHTML); and crypto and TLS footguns (ECB mode, createCipher without an IV on any receiver, verify=False, unverified SSL contexts, CERT_NONE, rejectUnauthorized: false, NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0, InsecureSkipVerify: true, md5/sha1 over a password). They also cover XXE parser flags and a remote \`\<script\>\` without integrity=. A separate check (_workflow_findings, agents/core/code_guidance.py:189) flags \`${{ github.event.\* }}\` inside a GitHub Actions \`run:\` step, on the run line or in its \`\|\`/\`\>\` block, whose column is the run key's own, so a \`- run: \|\` step's env:, with: and if: keys end it and are never flagged. The guards keep the usual false positives quiet: \`model.eval()\` and \`obj.eval()\`, \`def eval(\`, \`yaml.load(..., Loader=SafeLoader)\`, \`torch.load(..., weights_only=True)\`, \`regex.exec()\`, an \`===\` comparison with innerHTML, \`hashlib.md5(data)\`, a script with integrity=, whole-line comments and non-code files. Every rule is linear: a guard reads the call's balanced arguments across lines, at most MAX_CALL_CHARS (400) of them and at most MAX_CALLS_PER_LINE (8) calls a line (_call_args, agents/core/code_guidance.py:211; _guarded, agents/core/code_guidance.py:226), or the tag it matched. scan (agents/core/code_guidance.py:265) returns \`{rule, line, message}\`, at most 20 findings over the first 1 MB, with lines split at \`\\n\` only (CRLF and CR normalised), and stops after SCAN_SECONDS (0.5 s; agents/core/code_guidance.py:50); a scan cut by size or time ends with a \`scan-incomplete\` finding at the first line it did not finish, so a cut scan never reads as clean. \`.env\`, \`.env.\*\` and the shell dotfiles are scanned as shell. The findings go to five places. On the file_write approval card, FileTools.classify_mutation (agents/core/file_tools.py:1159) adds \`code_warnings\` (rule@line, at most 200 characters), \`code_warning_count\` and a title notice (\`code not fully scanned\` when a limit cut it), scanning the name as spelled and the file the write lands on (a symlink, a \`./\` or \`//\` spelling; labels, agents/core/code_guidance.py:296, names each finding once and in line order); it never sets a class, so H506's approval binding is untouched, and an instruction-file notice and the warning share the title. The gated call's \`approval_required\` answer carries \`code_warnings\`/\`code_warning_count\` (_ANSWER_LABEL_KEYS, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:197), and the tool loop's pause reply names them as warnings, not refusals (_approval_reply, agents/core/agent_runtime.py:1572): the loop ends the turn there, so the model reads them on its next turn and the owner at once. On an ungated write's result (FileTools.write_file, agents/core/file_tools.py:958), the model reads \`code_warnings\` with a \`code_guidance\` note that they are warnings; an approved write's result lands on the approved task's record, which no model turn reads. A refused write carries none, and a failing scan never fails a write. For a marketplace install (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:858; install and install-zip answer \`code_warnings\` when there are any, agents/core/routers/skills.py:506) and a generated skill (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1208), scan_tree (agents/core/code_guidance.py:340) walks the folder, skipping symlinks; only code files count toward its 200-file bound, a file past 1 MB is read as cut, the whole walk stops after TREE_SECONDS (2 s), and the file a bound stops at gets a \`scan-incomplete\` finding. skill_propose answers the generated skill's \`code_warnings\` (agents/core/skills/tools.py:462), and generate_skill resets them first, so a skipped generation never keeps another's; the background-review and \[learn:\] generation paths only log them (their main.py is a fixed template). The owner's switch is \`security.code_guidance\` (agents/core/settings_db.py:240, on by default; only an explicit off turns it off, an unreadable store keeps it on, and safe mode forces it back on: safe_mode.FORCED_SETTINGS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:105). An aliased child_process module (\`cp.exec\`) is not flagged. tests/test_h507_code_guidance.py (136 cases) covers every rule firing, the guard cases, the cross-language scoping, the finding shape, the table size, the workflow rule with a blank line in a block and every non-run key, the bounds, the labels, the switch, the real ToolRPC card and approved execution, a clean and a non-code write, the shared instruction notice, a failing scan, a refused write, scan_tree (symlink, bounds, switch), generation, install with the route, and the setting row. The review round added crafted megabytes (one per quadratic rule) scanned in under a second and a card labelled promptly through the ToolRPC intake, the time and size cuts, the pause reply driven through the agent loop, skill_propose's warnings and the reset, the symlink and path spellings, a package padded with assets, the \`- run: \|\` block, the wrapped and nested calls, the child_process and createCipher spellings, CR/form-feed/U+2028 lines, the dotfiles and the safe-mode switch. The review round's 44 mutants were all killed, ten after seven cases were added. [d1](../agents/core/code_guidance.py) [d2](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/marketplace.py) |

| <a id="h510"></a>H510 | Stop a web page or a rebound DNS name from driving the local API | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The Host guard exempts _PROBE_PATHS (agents/web.py:767, applied at :824-825), so /healthz, /readyz and /metrics answer a rebound name: live-verified, \`Host: evil.test\` returns 200 on /healthz and /metrics and reaches the route on /readyz, while every real API route returns 400. Because DNS rebinding makes the browser treat those responses as same-origin, an attacker page still reads the full Prometheus body (route templates, request counters, latency buckets) and the /readyz body naming the configured LLM backend and the agent/channel counts. The code names this residual itself at agents/web.py:800-802 ("a rebound page can therefore still read the golden-signal counters"). Close it by narrowing the exemption rather than removing it — the operability reason for it is sound. Suggested shape: keep /healthz and /readyz exempt but strip /readyz's detail to a bare ready/not-ready when the Host is not accepted, and require an accepted Host for /metrics unless the request is unambiguously non-browser (no Origin and no Sec-Fetch-Site, or an explicit JARVIS_METRICS_ALLOW_ANY_HOST opt-in for a monitor that scrapes by an unlisted name). Needs a test alongside test_healthz_from_any_host_is_exempt asserting that a refused Host cannot read the counter body. This acknowledgment foundation is not a VRAM swap: canonical aliases, production backend/controller identity, atomic per-generation leases, owner control coordination and authoritative hardware snapshots remain unresolved. | <a id="h518"></a>H518 | Browse, search and export everything the assistant has generated | keep | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The unified gallery now scans retained generated and attachment metadata through bounded keyset pages. Search and kind filters apply during each scan, including MIME matches; zero-match pages retain continuation to older records. Cursor fields, ordering and filter/source binding are checked before catalog or header I/O. Each request inspects at most 200 candidate headers and retains the existing authenticated path/byte validation. The HUD resets and cancels stale searches, reports retained versus loaded/scanned counts separately, and exports an explicit selection of at most 200 files/128 MiB. Independent review and desktop/responsive-browser tests cover older-only matches, empty-page continuation, deletion boundaries, source flags, malformed cursors and exact export selection. Shared attachment previews now abort on card unmount and reject late fetch/body continuations; owned object URLs are revoked even before a pending React state update commits. Reassessment against the frozen keep/superior row confirms the bounded retained-catalog browse/search/export contract. Retention and unavailable-byte handling match the original scope; deleted/evicted history restoration, native controls and unlimited ZIPs are not acceptance requirements. Timestamp/key traversal excludes newer appends without promising exact membership against backdated inserts. The current local generator catalogs produced assets; legacy cloud proposals do not execute a cloud image backend. Future approved cloud completion must persist catalog output as part of H515, not be inferred from generic text-task completion. No live-provider or universal producer-provenance proof is claimed. The implemented cloud-image producer now writes canonical validated output and an idempotent opt-in catalog record before successful completion (if only that catalog write fails after the image and its completion record are durable, the task still succeeds with warning catalog_record_failed and a later recover, e.g. the generation-task status read, adds the row idempotently); independent signed-worker/mock-provider/blob/ZIP checks cover the previously conditional writeback obligation. The legacy generic text task is not treated as an image producer. Current-source reassessment 2026-09-27: reviewed the changed shared HUD, multimodal router, local image runtime/writer, Telegram decision routing and protected settings against this frozen contract. Gallery pagination/search/selection/export, opaque authenticated delivery and scheduled-media recipient/content/deadline/uncertain-state controls remain intact. New registered local producer reaches the opt-in gallery and authenticated readback with its correct backend label. At that reassessment the full offline suites passed 19,363 backend tests (35 skipped) and 1,829 frontend tests. Limits remain retained opt-in metadata, bounded bytes/exports, signature-level non-PNG validation and no live provider/native-codec guarantee. See the H517 local-provider review for the specific shared-path analysis. Shared-registry compatibility review: image execution now calls the selected concrete adapter while preserving ComfyUI option validation, exact config/approval checks and backend factory semantics. Image/edit/workflow regressions pass; no extra edit/upscale capability is claimed. ComfyUI shared-source review (2026-10-01): the changed backend now forwards the existing runtime guard and rechecks authority after download and immediately before atomic publication. Reviewed the adapter delta and the new backend/worker regressions: revoked results are withheld without a PNG or catalog success, while machinery failures preserve their own reason. The unchanged gallery finalization/readback/export flow and image option vocabulary remain in force. This fixes the handover publication defect; it adds no provider inventory, VRAM orchestration or live-service acceptance. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review.
