| <a id="h526"></a>H526 | Make a spoken reply sound like speech rather than read-out markdown | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A spoken reply now sounds like speech rather than read-out markdown, as Hermes requires, at every text-to-speech entry point. agents/core/voice/speech_text.py is the one normaliser (for_speech, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:152). It drops \<think\>/\<thinking\>/\<reasoning\> blocks, an unclosed one to the end, and everything before a closing tag that never opened. It drops fenced code (\`\`\` or ~~~, an unclosed fence to the end) and HTML tags. \[text\](url) reads as its text, !\[alt\](url) as its alt, and a bare or \<autolinked\> URL as "link"; the URL no longer eats a closing parenthesis or the sentence's full stop, so "doc (https://…)." is "doc (link).". Headings, list items (-, \*, +, •, 1., 1), task boxes), block quotes and table rows lose their markers and end with a full stop (_line, :124). Table cells read with commas; separator rows and horizontal rules are dropped. Bold, italic, strikethrough and inline-code markers go and their words stay (_inline, :113), with word guards so 2\*3\*4 and my_var_name survive. Symbols are said in the reply's language (_symbols, :104, WORDS, :79): & as and/și, % as percent/la sută, -\> → =\> as to/spre, and °, °C, °F as degrees/grade (Celsius, Fahrenheit). The language comes from the request, else a ro-RO-… voice, else the engine default (speech_lang, :95). Emoji go: pictographs, flags, keycaps, skin tones, ZWJ sequences, variation selectors and tag flags (_EMOJI, :61). Fish \[emotion\] tags are kept intact. Text with no letter or digit left is "" (_SAYABLE, :76), and the result is a fixed point. Wiring: TTSEngine.speak normalises before any backend, Fish included, and synthesises nothing when nothing is left (agents/core/voice/tts.py:212-220). An emotion-tags-only reply is also nothing for the other backends (:263). speak_stream normalises the whole reply before splitting sentences (:315), so a code or reasoning block spanning sentences is dropped whole. Through TTSEngine.speak this covers the wake-word VoicePipeline, VoiceChannel.send and chat voice notes: speakable() is for_speech plus its cap, and SpokenReply passes the chat's language (agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:69). POST /tts answers 204 with X-Nerva-Speech: nothing_to_say instead of synthesising (agents/core/routers/voice.py:76), and /tts/stream frames carry the spoken sentences. The HUD twin is frontend/src/speech-text.ts (speechText, frontend/src/speech-text.ts:105). Both implementations are pinned by the same cases (frontend/src/test/speech-text-cases.json, 47 cases, read by both suites). SpeechStreamFilter (:129) drops a fenced block or reasoning block whose markers arrive split across token deltas, holding back a tail that could still become a marker (including a \<reasoning …\> tag split inside its attributes). An unclosed block at the end is never spoken. frontend/src/voice.ts uses it on the live sentence stream (:281, :332, :343). It normalises the whole reply for /tts/stream, /tts and the browser's own speechSynthesis voice (:204), and every streamed sentence (:302). A 204 is silence, never an empty clip or the fallback voice (:244, :314). Mobile sends the reply to /tts, so the hub normalises it; ttsFetchBase64 reads 204 as '' and speak() finishes at once (mobile/src/api/client.ts:2402, mobile/src/audio/tts.ts:23). Tests: tests/test_h526_speech_text.py (Python, every shared case plus the fixed-point property), frontend/src/test/speech-text.test.ts and voice-speech-text.test.tsx (a live push-to-talk turn with a fence split across deltas), and mobile/src/api/__tests__/tts.test.ts. 126 mutants: 124 caught after nine cases were added; the other two were redundant code, removed. Named speech compatibility review: normalization still precedes synthesis, persona consent and tags retain existing behavior, and HTTP nothing-to-say/stream paths are unchanged. Shared speech-text and voice regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/speech_text.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/tts.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py) |

| <a id="h529"></a>H529 | Transcribe a voice message or dictated speech | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Un singur backend. Hermes are opt (faster-whisper local, \`local_command\`, Groq, OpenAI, Mistral Voxtral cu diarizare și timestamp-uri pe cuvânt, xAI, ElevenLabs Scribe, DeepInfra) plus provideri pe comandă de shell, un ABC \`TranscriptionProvider\`, selecție explicită strictă cu auto-detect doar la o instalare neconfigurată vreodată, un plafon pe lungimea promptului Whisper, trunchierea liniștii înainte de upload ca să scadă octeții trimiși, un picker și \`POST /api/audio/transcribe\`. Nimic din astea nu e livrat. Nu din lene: clauza de guvernanță a rândului spune că orice STT în cloud trece prin \`plugin.egress\` și trimite vorbirea înregistrată a owner-ului în afara cutiei — e aceeași clasă de dezvăluire ca TTS-ul în cloud și trebuie clasificat, nu pus ca implicit de config. Cache-ul motorului stă tot în \`routers/voice.py\`, care e casa greșită pentru el; canalul îl importă în loc să-l dubleze, iar mutarea lui e o schimbare separată. NEVERIFICAT pe servicii reale: niciun model Whisper încărcat cu adevărat. | <a id="h530"></a>H530 | Not turn a silent recording into a message | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whisper nu tace când nu aude nimic — inventează. Pe liniște întoarce o propoziție fluentă din datele de antrenament: generice de subtitrare („Subtitles by the Amara.org community", „Subtitrarea: ..."), formule de încheiere („Thanks for watching!", „Mulțumesc pentru vizionare!"). Pentru o casetă de dictare e o enervare; aici e un control de siguranță, exact cum spune clauza de guvernanță a rândului: un mesaj vocal devine o tură \*inbound\*, o tură inbound poate cere lucruri, deci un transcript halucinat e o instrucțiune pe care n-a autorizat-o nimeni, intrată într-un sistem guvernat. \`hallucination.py\` o oprește. Designul e dictat de asimetria erorilor: a pierde un mesaj real e inacceptabil (cineva zice „mulțumesc" și dispare fără răspuns), a lăsa unul să treacă costă o tură ignorabilă. De aici două niveluri, nu o listă: frazele distinctive (\`CANNED\`) cad pe text; cele pe care oamenii chiar le spun (\`AMBIGUOUS\`) cad doar când durata \*decodată\* arată că înregistrarea era prea scurtă ca să le conțină — iar un apelant care nu știe durata le păstrează. Potrivirea e pe transcriptul întreg, niciodată pe substring; regula pentru generice cere marker ȘI vocabular exclusiv de generic, fiindcă prima variantă (doar markerul) mânca „am văzut pe amara.org un film bun". Filtrul rulează în \`STTEngine.transcribe\` și întoarce \`\[silence\]\` — santinela care exista deja și pe care fiecare apelant o tratează ca „nu s-a spus nimic" — deci ruta de dictare din browser o primește gratuit, nu doar calea nouă de pe Telegram. CORECTARE la auditul din 07.09: verdictul „missing" era pe jumătate greșit. A doua cerință a rândului — auto-stop pe liniște (VAD), ca utilizatorul să nu apese o tastă — EXISTĂ de mult în \`frontend/src/voice.ts\`: \`recordUtterance\` eșantionează RMS la 60 ms și oprește după \`SILENCE_MS\`, cu plafon dur și abandon când nimeni nu vorbește. Auditul a dat un \`rg -i 'vad'\` peste \`frontend/src/\` și a raportat „nimic relevant"; linia 111 spune „VAD" în litere. Ce lipsea cu adevărat era dovada: nimic nu o testa, fiindcă harness-ul comun folosește un MediaRecorder care se oprește singur și scurtcircuitează intervalul. \`voice-vad.test.tsx\` folosește unul care NU se oprește niciodată singur — dacă o tură se termină acolo, doar detectorul de liniște a putut-o termina — și acoperă fereastra de liniște, plafonul, abandonul și faptul că vorbirea continuă resetează ceasul. Named speech compatibility review: the Whisper hallucination filter and decoded-duration handling remain on the native transcribe path; named commands preserve the existing exact-sentinel behavior. Hallucination and speech selection regressions pass. [d1](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d3](../frontend/src/voice.ts) |

| <a id="h545"></a>H545 | Let the editor hand the agent MCP servers that exist only for that session | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | Every accepted part is missing: (1) an ACP session entry point (session/new, load, resume, fork) that accepts an mcpServers array; this depends on H542. (2) A session-scoped MCP registry layered over the global MCPManager, so that a server registered for one session is invisible to every other session and is torn down when the session ends. (3) Off-loop registration (asyncio.to_thread) with a bounded discovery join (default 1.5 s), so a dead server cannot block session creation. (4) A scheduled late refresh that re-reads the session's tool list after slow discovery finishes. (5) Governance: session-supplied servers pass through the same outbound MCP authorization gate as configured servers (H554, still partial), and their trust tier is capped at no wider than a configured server's. | <a id="h551"></a>H551 | Choose which of a server's tools the model may see | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Filtrele allow/deny pe nume și glob (\`tools_allow\`/\`tools_deny\`, potrivite cu \`fnmatchcase\` pe numele original al uneltei) restrâng atât listarea, cât și apelarea uneltelor MCP; deny prevalează (\`tool_visible\`, agents/core/mcp/client.py:603-610). \`_list_tools\` nu oferă o unealtă ascunsă (:596-601), iar \`MCPServer.call_tool\` răspunde \`tool_filtered\` înainte să plece vreun frame (:616-617), deci scoaterea unei unelte din filtru o revocă imediat, și la apel, nu doar la înregistrare. Un filtru malformat îngustează, nu lărgește: un allow care nu e listă devine \`\[\]\` (\`_clean_tool_patterns\`, :124-135). Limitele (64 de pattern-uri / 128 de caractere, :120-121) sunt fail-closed. Ruta de add (\`admin_mcp_add\`, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:200-209) refuză cu 400 \`{"error": "invalid_tool_filter", "field": ...}\` un \`tools_allow\` sau \`tools_deny\` peste limite, în loc să-l taie (\`tool_patterns_over_bounds\`, client.py:138-154), la fel ca pe o valoare care nu e listă sau pe o intrare goală. Un deny care nu poate fi păstrat (peste 64 de pattern-uri, un pattern peste 128 de caractere, sau care nu e listă) venit dintr-un config editat manual prin \`load_from_config\` îngustează serverul la nimic (\`tools_allow = \[\]\`) și loghează un avertisment (:407-418), pentru că un deny tăiat ar face din nou vizibilă unealta pe care o numea. Filtrele fac round-trip prin \`to_config\`/\`load_from_config\` (:808-809, :852-853). Teste (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py, 13/13 verzi): listare și deny prevalează (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:47-57), unealtă filtrată refuzată la apel pe nume (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:60-70), filtru malformat → nimic și round-trip de config (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:73-84), ruta primește filtre și refuză formele malformate (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:98-109), ruta refuză 129 de caractere / 65 de intrări pe ambele câmpuri (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:121-129) și acceptă un filtru exact la limită (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:132-138), un deny imposibil de păstrat ascunde toate uneltele cu avertisment (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:141-147), iar un config editat manual cu deny peste limite se încarcă fail-closed fără să atingă serverul vecin (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:150-159). Resursele/prompts sunt urmărite separat în H552. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py) |

## docs-features

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h575"></a>H575 | Read the product's own per-feature manual (published docs site) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h576"></a>H576 | Read a PDF / Office document / notebook as text from inside a tool call | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Formats stop at .pdf and .docx: no .ipynb, .xlsx, .doc, .ppt, .odt, .rtf or .epub, and the text comes back plain rather than converted to Markdown. There is no size refusal for documents (Hermes refuses over 50 MB): the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned. There is no scanned-page coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text, which the row calls the clever half. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. A missing parser is named (\`parser_missing\`) rather than installed lazily. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../tests/test_file_search.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_read_documents.py) |
| <a id="h577"></a>H577 | Give the current conversation one recurring instruction (session heartbeat) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h578"></a>H578 | Re-run a prompt on a cadence inside the session, self-paced, with stop conditions (/loop) | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h579"></a>H579 | Pull a file or slice of a file into a message inline (@ context references) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner's own chat attaches @file:path, @file:path#L10-40 and @file:"a b.md" for the turn. agents/core/context_refs.py holds the rules. expand (agents/core/context_refs.py:209) finds a reference that starts a word or follows an opening bracket, quote or backtick, and not one inside a word or a path (x@file:, a/@file:, @@file:) (_REF_RE, agents/core/context_refs.py:49); a double-quoted path may hold spaces, #L7 is one line and #L2-L3 a range, and trailing punctuation or a closing quote is not part of it. The routes keep the owner's message as typed: commands, skills, LLM-backend control, the router and memory see only it. The "--- Attached Context ---" section is bound to the turn (bind_attached, agents/core/context_refs.py:251; agents/web.py:1245 on /chat, and inside the stream task at agents/web.py:1312) and appended after the message only in the model prompt (attached_block, agents/core/context_refs.py:266; Orchestrator._build_agent_turn_text, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3331), so the file text is never stored as the owner's words, replayed in a later turn's history or embedded. Hermes keeps the expanded message in the user turn; Nerva shows the attachment to the model for the attaching (tainted) turn only. Each file's text is in a fence longer than any backtick run in it, so no line of it can close the fence (_fence, agents/core/context_refs.py:194), under a header that names the file as typed and the lines actually attached, \`(line 7)\` or \`(lines 2-3)\`, as attached file content, not instructions; injection patterns and a forged Attached Context marker are flagged in that header (\`flagged: …\`, _flags, agents/core/context_refs.py:180), not withheld. Each path is resolved through the same FileScope as file_read (_attach_one, agents/core/context_refs.py:133), so outside the file roots, .. traversal, a symlink escape and a secret-looking path such as .env are refused. A missing file, a directory, a binary file (a NUL in its first 8 KB), a bad line range, a range past the end, an unreadable file, an over-long name, a denied stat and an unknown ~user each become a one-line warning in the same section instead of failing the message. With JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS unset, the file tools' default root \<data home\>/workspace is used; only an unusable JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS makes the section say nothing was attached. The limits are 8 distinct references per message, 64 KB per reference and 128 KB in all, counted in bytes. A cut reference says so, and a repeated one is attached once. A line range skips the lines before it a 64K-character chunk at a time, up to 64M characters into the file (MAX_SCAN_CHARS; past it, the warning "line N is too far into the file"), keeps at most the byte budget and never reads a line whole (_read_lines, agents/core/context_refs.py:105); invalid UTF-8 is read with replacement characters. The expansion runs on /chat and /chat/stream (agents/web.py:1234, agents/web.py:1399), off the event loop, so both the HUD and nerva chat get it. It never runs for a channel message, as Hermes' gateway does not expand either. A turn that attached anything is tainted: handle_input and handle_input_stream call _taint_attached_context (agents/core/orchestrator.py:357, :1966, :2169), which raises the turn's action origin to recall:untrusted. An action planned from attached content therefore escalates GRANT to QUEUE at the kernel, while an inbound turn keeps its more specific origin. complete (agents/core/context_refs.py:272) lists in-scope completions for what follows @file:, with directories ending in / and a path with a space quoted (a name no reference could name is left out). It shows dot files only when asked and never secret paths or links out of the roots, is bounded to 50 entries, and returns nothing for a prefix over 1,024 characters. GET /api/context-refs (agents/core/routers/commands.py:63, user-guarded, read-only) serves it and the reference types, and answers 503 file_roots_unusable when the roots cannot be used. The HUD composer (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:228, :283, frontend/src/context-refs.tsx) offers the reference types while \`@\` or \`@fi\` is typed (Tab takes \`@file:\`), then the in-scope paths; a list is shown only for the text it was fetched for. tests/test_h579_context_refs.py (70 cases) covers the bounds, parsing, whole files, line ranges (single, L-form, past the end), every refusal and warning, a symlink, punctuation, word starts, dedupe, the byte cut, the shared budget, the reference limit, an unreadable file, invalid UTF-8, the roots, an explicit scope and the marker; completion (prefixes, dot files, secrets, roots, a long prefix, a file or missing folder, the bound); both routes, the completion route, the turn taint on both paths, and an inbound turn. The review round added attached text never read as the owner's command (a slash command, a skill, a backend switch) and never stored or carried to a later turn, through the real routes; a file that cannot close its fence; injection and a forged marker flagged; a range in one huge line bounded in memory and a range of many short lines linear; a range too far into a file; quoted paths and their completion; references after brackets and quotes and not inside words; the distinct-reference limit; the header's path and lines; paths the system refuses to look at; and the roots. frontend/src/test/context-refs.test.tsx has 6 cases. The row's 53 mutants predate this code; the review round's 34: 28 caught at once, five after five cases were added, and one equivalent (a kept read stops at the budget either way, since readline(0) answers ""). Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. | <a id="h583"></a>H583 | Steer which upstream provider serves an OpenRouter request (provider routing) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | OpenRouter provider routing ships, and the owner's changes reach the live backend on the next request. Six llm rows are seeded right after compatible_provider (agents/core/settings_db.py:171-176): openrouter_sort, openrouter_only, openrouter_ignore, openrouter_order, openrouter_require_parameters, and openrouter_data_collection, seeded deny. The HUD PUT /api/admin/settings/llm and \`nerva config set\` both validate through settings_db.validate_category, and the three slug lists share one slug check (agents/core/settings_db.py:841 -\> agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py:97). build_provider_block (agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py:110) turns the knobs into OpenRouter's \`provider\` object. It lower-cases and de-duplicates slugs, caps each list at 32, sends require_parameters only as a literal true, omits empty knobs, and raises on an invalid value instead of dropping it. HybridRouter.detect hands OpenRouterBackend a reader over the rows, not a snapshot (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:396). Before every text or tool-turn request the backend re-reads and re-validates them off the event loop (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:95, called at agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:168 and agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:207) and puts the object in the body (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:129). A HUD change, or a \`nerva config set\` run from another process against the same settings DB, therefore governs the next request, so the CLI's '(a running hub picks it up within 30 s)' holds for these rows. The object goes only to the openrouter profile, never to openai-compatible (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:71). Fail-closed limits: a stored value that decodes but does not validate refuses to build the backend at detect (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:399, logged). After detect it refuses that request: '\[OpenRouter error\]', nothing sent, and an error log naming llm.openrouter_\* (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:108-112). The next request flows once the row is fixed. Known limit: a row that cannot be read or decoded (a sqlite error, or malformed JSON written around the API) is swallowed by settings_db.get_value into that knob's seeded default with no log line. Such a request keeps data_collection deny but loses only/ignore/order/sort/require_parameters. A 400/404 to a request that carried the object logs OpenRouter's own message (log-safe, 200 chars), the object sent and the settings to loosen (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:133), so the seeded deny does not fail opaquely after an upgrade. Coverage is tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py (30 tests). It checks the exact body on the tool-turn and text paths (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:78, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:93), and on the wire through the real egress client with a MockTransport injected through its public transport kwarg (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:135). It checks that no object is sent with nothing set or for openai-compatible (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:102, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:114), and that bad values are refused (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:174, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:180). It covers the rows, the deny default, write validation and the CLI entry point (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:192, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:206, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:223), and router wiring with a stubbed read (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:274, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:287, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:300). Three router tests run on a real temporary settings DB: rows -\> detect -\> body (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:346); a change after detect reaching the next request, tightening and loosening (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:358); and a tampered row after detect refusing the request, then healing (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:384). Two more cover the policy-404 hint and its absence without an object or on a 401 (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:406, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:424). Nerva has no Nous Portal provider, so the knobs apply to OpenRouter only. | <a id="h586"></a>H586 | Paste or attach a screenshot into the conversation (vision & image paste) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Both halves of the accepted adaptation are delivered: the HUD composer and the terminal. useComposerImages (frontend/src/composer-images.tsx) takes image paste and drag-drop (frontend/src/composer-images.tsx:74-75) plus a file chooser. It accepts at most eight PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP images of up to 4 MiB each, and the shared InputBar wires it in (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:246). App.submit sends an image draft to runVision (frontend/src/app.tsx:410, frontend/src/app.tsx:385). runVision posts to the user-guarded POST /api/vlm/composer/describe (agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:236, registered at agents/web.py:1529). The route checks data-URI signatures and bounds raster decoding (validate_raster, agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:53). It binds the request to the reviewed destination/model revision and refuses a non-local VLM unless the owner has acknowledged it (agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:267). It then calls VLMBackend.generate_vision_checked (agents/core/llm/vlm.py:375). Since H513 the binding also covers the credential and the data policy, the H378 selection guards refuse a flagged model before any client is built (409), the configuration and the wire are checked again at the request and after cleanup (a change is never an answer: it is a 409 vlm_destination_changed, or a 502 vlm_generation_failed when the changed configuration is no longer a valid vision destination, such as a non-http(s) URL), and the status and the answer carry the data-policy warning, which the composer and the vision reply show (nerva chat --image does not print it). Images reach only the configured VLM, never the text chat model. The taint governance holds through isolation rather than a taint flag. The vision answer is a client-only transcript entry, and ordinary turns post only {message, agent} to /chat/stream (frontend/src/app.tsx:336), so no tool plan can be derived from image content. tests/test_composer_vision.py (33 passed) covers validation, auth, binding, the remote acknowledgement and error shapes, and, since H513, the warning on a pure status, a key rotation that revises the binding, a policy rotated during client construction, a key rotated during cleanup, the H378 selection refusal and a cancelled generation that closes the client. Intake and the vision turn are covered by frontend/src/test/composer-images.test.tsx, frontend/src/test/composer-vision-turn.test.tsx and frontend/e2e/composer-images.spec.ts, (the two vitest files, 12 cases, pass; the Playwright spec was not run here). The terminal: \`nerva chat --image PATH\` (repeatable, up to eight) and \`--clipboard-image\` (agents/cli/nerva.py:356) turn the chat verb into the same vision turn (_vision_turn, agents/cli/nerva.py:2395), dispatched before any /chat body is built (agents/cli/nerva.py:2043). Each file is opened once without blocking, must be a regular file, is read up to 4 MiB and sent by the type its signature says, never its name (_image_mime, agents/cli/nerva.py:2138; _read_image, agents/cli/nerva.py:2148). The clipboard is read by the platform's own reader with a fixed argv, no shell, its output read no further than the bound against a 10 s deadline, in a session of its own whose whole process group is killed at the deadline, past the bound or on an interrupt, so a descendant holding the pipe cannot stretch it (_run_reader, agents/cli/nerva.py:2248): wl-paste on Wayland, xclip on X11, pngpaste or else osascript on macOS, Windows PowerShell from the system directory, else the first powershell.exe or pwsh.exe in an absolute PATH entry that is not the current directory (never shutil.which, which puts the current directory back on Windows), on Windows, and through interop under WSL (the /mnt/c default, else the PATH's powershell.exe) (_clipboard_command, agents/cli/nerva.py:2223; _clipboard_image, agents/cli/nerva.py:2333). The verb reads GET /api/vlm/composer/status, sends that destination and binding with the images, so a model changed in between is refused (409), and a destination off the hub's machine is used only when --remote-vision names it, the terminal's form of the HUD's per-destination acknowledgement (agents/cli/nerva.py:2484). The describe call waits up to 240 s (the hub gives the model 180 s), and a timeout after the status answered is a failed turn, not a missing hub; the reply is checked as the HUD checks it (ok, a string answer of at most 128 KiB); a status without its destination, binding or local sends nothing. The --remote-vision address is compared as the hub names it (scheme and host in any case, the default port, a trailing slash); one with a user name, query or fragment is refused as a look-alike, and --remote-vision without an image is refused. A body the route refuses is answered with its first reason as {error, reason}, never FastAPI's default 422 that echoed the images back (_BoundedValidationRoute, agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:22), and the CLI prints it, with the field a validation reason names; hub text is filtered before it reaches the terminal. --agent, --session and --reasoning are refused with images: the turn goes to the vision model only, never to /chat, an agent or a session. -z, --json and --usage-file keep their meaning. tests/test_nerva_chat_image.py (30 cases) covers the route taken, the real route answering the CLI, a model changed in between, each raster kind, the remote acknowledgement, the refusals before any request, the hub's answers and the clipboard readers; its 20 mutants are all caught. The first adversarial review (0 major, 6 minor, 9 nits) found the 30 s timeout reported as no hub, the clipboard buffered whole, the route's refusal without its reason, PowerShell resolved from the current directory, 16 surviving mutants of its own, and macOS/WSL gaps; all are fixed, and tests/test_h586b_image_review.py (50 cases) pins them, its 35 mutants all caught. Test manual: CHT-115, CHT-116. The second review (0 major, 4 minor, 8 nits) found an oversized clipboard image reported as empty, the deadline stretched by a descendant, the Windows fallback still reaching the current directory through shutil.which, and 8 real survivors; all are fixed, tests/test_h586c_image_review.py (23 cases, real processes for the deadline, the group kill, the exit code and the bound) pins them, and its 16 mutants are all caught but one equivalent. Known limits: the receipt's model stays null for an image turn, and the route's own 403 (no acknowledgement) is told apart from an auth 403 by its message, a case the binding already makes unreachable. Composer image recovery review (2026-10-02): enabled recovery is exposed by paired validated empty_retries/retry_notice metadata. HUD renders the notice before remote consent and resets acknowledgment on binding changes; CLI validates and prints the notice before acknowledgment/send. Default shapes and stdout/JSON stay unchanged. Actual CLI and web composer tests, full1847 frontend tests, typecheck, E2E TypeScript compilation and build pass; browser E2E execution was not run. Native composer normalization review (2026-10-02): VLM validates and normalizes compatible replies on both budget-disabled and active governed paths. Useful typed reasoning supplies an answer without an extra call; malformed envelopes remain failures. CLI/HUD entrypoints, acknowledgment and public shapes are unchanged. Parent696 integration includes composer/policy tests; frontend/schema/assets match the prior1847-test verified snapshot. Existing equivalent status preserved. Publication review correction (2026-10-02): agents/core/llm/native_response.py:72 now admits only legacy untyped, reasoning.text and reasoning.summary details. Encrypted, opaque, unknown and malformed typed details are skipped even if they contain string summary/content/text. Seven actual governed image/video regressions failed before the fix; the corrected normalization and empty-recovery union passed all163 cases. Opaque-only video refuses in one call without granting retry authority. All base evidence hashes were current before this two-file correction; unchanged authority, transport, route and UI contracts retain their existing assessment. Explicit OpenRouter composer review (2026-10-02): agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py constructs the named native adapter and retains remote consent, destination binding, response validation and optional bounded same-body empty recovery. Status exposes existing model, destination and data-policy fields; public shapes and frontend/CLI sources remain unchanged. The final51-case adapter suite covers actual composer requests, policy disclosure and revocation. Composer still has no separate training/cost acknowledgment controls, so guarded choices remain unavailable through this UI; no new controls or automatic discovery are claimed. Image-turn UI/CLI review (2026-10-02): the prior absence of training/cost controls is resolved by independent unchecked HUD controls and explicit CLI flags. Missing, duplicate, unknown or malformed requirement metadata refuses; only required checked literal flags enter the HUD request. Draft changes/clear/refresh reset consent, and changed server guard details invalidate the binding. CLI-to-real-composer tests verify audit and simulated provider dispatch. 1858 frontend tests and6 Chrome desktop/mobile browser cases pass, with generated schema/assets, typechecks and build refreshed. Existing image isolation and unchanged text dispatch remain covered. DeepInfra consumer review (2026-10-02): new explicit catalog/image selection preserves this row's existing contract. Actual composer sends require the reviewed credential/model/destination and existing independent remote/selection confirmations; key rotation, failed refresh and late duplicate/model mutations refuse. Local-only describe/screen/Telegram consumers remain unavailable for remote DeepInfra. Image intake and CLI transport are unchanged; HUD refresh now asks the server to refresh catalog data and clears confirmation.61 new backend cases,455 focused regressions,1859 frontend cases and6 Chrome desktop/emulated-mobile cases pass; no live-provider claim. Nous account collateral review (2026-10-02): compared the complete diff from 7a73bf03. Existing CLI command implementations are unchanged; only auth parser/dispatch registration is additive. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. Existing OAuth handler bodies are unchanged; four separate owner-only Nous routes and documentation are added. All pre-edit evidence hashes matched the prior checkpoint. This preserves the existing verdict without claiming new capability completion; the 266-pass/3-skip integration suite includes existing OAuth, secrets and route gates. Nous vision integration review (2026-10-02,43c669b0): the explicit adapter retains existing selection findings, per-turn acknowledgements, final request authorization and local-only consumer constraints. Wire mode is bound to the preview; role/config/logout changes revoke stale selections. Non-Nous constructor behavior is preserved. The 1705-case vision/H277/composer union passes; no frontend runtime or public response schema changes. Existing capability verdict retained, without a live-provider claim. Auto-composer review (2026-10-02, 876c3e2b): the attachment and paste/drop handlers remain on the same responsive composer. Optional auto selection displays the chosen provider/model/destination, rejects malformed source metadata and retains independent confirmation. Full 1861-case frontend suite, typecheck and regenerated build pass; native mobile intake and live provider proof remain outside this verdict. Mutation collateral review (2026-10-02, 3ea63405): the extra backend HTTP regression prepares the model shown by composer status and confirms one subsequent image send. Frontend attachment/paste/drop code and its earlier browser validation are unchanged; native mobile intake remains outside this verdict. Vechiul verdict de excludere nu dovedește implementarea.  |
| <a id="h658"></a>H658 | A member of a shared session that lives on a different machine, running under a capability the target machine issued and can revoke | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h659"></a>H659 | A retried external request returns the original run instead of starting a second one | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The three external intakes now honour an Idempotency-Key, so a retried request returns the original run instead of starting a second one. agents/core/idempotency.py holds the rules. parse_key (agents/core/idempotency.py:79) accepts 1-255 visible ASCII characters and refuses anything else with HTTP 400 invalid_idempotency_key. check_header (agents/core/idempotency.py:322) runs before the body is read. On POST /api/webhooks/{id} (after the hub's 503 not-initialized check) and on POST /api/a2a/task it also runs before the token, signature or peer is checked; POST /api/actions/request authenticates the caller first (user_guard), then checks the key. IdempotencyStore (agents/core/idempotency.py:105) keeps a SQLite row whose primary key is (scope, key), in the data folder (idempotency.db). reserve (agents/core/idempotency.py:147) runs inside BEGIN IMMEDIATE, so two workers or processes on the same file cannot both win (8 racing threads get exactly one reservation), and a reservation survives a restart. A row holds only the fingerprint (SHA-256 of method, path and body, each length-prefixed; agents/core/idempotency.py:88) and the public reference of what the request started, capped at 2 KB. It never holds the body, a reply or a credential. A retry with the same fingerprint returns that reference with Idempotent-Replayed: true, and nothing runs again. The same key with a different request gets 409 idempotency_key_reused. A retry while the first attempt is still running gets 409 idempotency_in_progress with Retry-After. A first attempt that raised or answered 5xx releases the key, so its retry runs. So does a webhook delivery whose agent turn produced no answer: an error or timeout marker from the hook's own target agent, a backend failure reply, the no-model line, a data-handling refusal or a refused turn, which the orchestrator answers as text instead of raising (is_failed_turn_reply, agents/core/orchestrator.py:110; _attempt_failed, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:583); and a deliver-only delivery whose push failed. The marker must name the hook's target exactly, and the target is not validated. A hook whose target is not a loaded agent id (a typo, "Jarvis", a removed agent) runs a routed turn, where the routed agent's "\[jarvis timeout\]" or "\[jarvis error: …\]" is not recognised. That delivery keeps its key, and a retry replays the failed turn. A turn that answered keeps its key even when its push failed. Each reservation carries a random attempt id, and complete, release and touch require it. A webhook delivery refreshes its row every 60 s while it runs (Claim.kept_alive, agents/core/idempotency.py:288); a row nothing refreshed for 600 s (its process died) is taken over under a new attempt id, so the first attempt can no longer finish or release it. A wall-clock jump larger than the window can still cause a take-over between heartbeats. Rows expire after 24 h, swept one bounded batch of 500 per reservation on a created_at index; a key's own expired row is dropped when that key is reserved. There is no per-scope row cap. The routes call the store through asyncio.to_thread, never on the event loop (a release on the exception path excepted). When the store cannot be written, a keyed request is refused with 503 idempotency_unavailable rather than run without its guarantee. That covers the reservation: an outcome that cannot be recorded afterwards is tried three times, then logged, and the row stays pending until it goes stale, after which a retry runs the request again. durable (agents/core/idempotency.py:129) says whether the store is a file rather than :memory:. The scope is the authenticated caller, and the key is reserved only after authentication and before anything is queued or run, so a duplicate never becomes a second approval item, kernel task or audit row. POST /api/actions/request (agents/core/routers/actions.py:76) reserves in the owner's action scope after the body is validated. A replay returns the queued action, or its id if the queue no longer holds it. POST /api/a2a/task (agents/core/routers/a2a.py:73-74) reserves in the scope a2a:\<peer\> only after the peer's signature verifies. A forged retry is refused with 401 and never reads the peer's receipt, and a refused task releases its key. The stored receipt is id, status and accepted. POST /api/webhooks/{id} (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:552) reserves in the scope webhook:\<id\> after the token or signature, the receiver switch and the enabled check, and before the delivery is counted, run or sent. _run_delivery (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:609) is the previous delivery body, unchanged. _public_outcome (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:603) keeps only ok, target, skipped and error; a replay answers those with the original status code, never the reply, steps or delivery. tests/test_h659_idempotency.py (90 cases) covers the key grammar, the fingerprint, and every store state: TTL, stale take-over, restart, two workers, a thread race, an in-memory store, the reference cap, an unreadable reference, and rollback on a file and in memory. It also covers the begin/claim mapping and all three routes: replay, conflict, no key, a malformed key checked first, an invalid body or unauthenticated caller not burning a key, a failed attempt released, scopes per hook and per peer, a skipped delivery replayed, and a forged A2A retry. The review round added fencing, the heartbeat, the bounded sweep and its index, per-key expiry, the migration of a file from before attempts, the outcome's retries, begin and done off the loop in all three routes, and the failed-turn and failed-push release rules. There are 57 mutants, all killed; the review round's 20 mutants: 19 killed (3 by tests added after a first pass) and 1 equivalent (PURGE_BATCH 500 to 5: the sweep test is written against the constant). Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. [d1](../agents/core/idempotency.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/actions.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/a2a.py) |

Docker selection currently supports local Unix endpoints. Correlation tokens/nonces reject stale frames but cannot attest output against Python introspection inside the same worker; authorization is enforced by the host broker. Spills cover redirected Python stdout/stderr up to 5,000,000 bytes per stream; deliberate native-fd bypasses and the part of a runaway stream past that ceiling are not kept on disk. Production deployment evidence is separate from these real-container tests. | <a id="h665"></a>H665 | Editing several skills in one atomic, rollback-on-failure batch | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h666"></a>H666 | The agent's own task list nests subtasks under a parent | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 and fixed after one adversarial review (review-H666: no major; five minors and eight nits, all fixed). Hermes nests the agent's subtasks with one optional parent on each todo item (the id of another item) instead of a nested array, so merge-by-id keeps working, and draws the list through one defensive tree builder. Nerva now does both, for the todo tool's items and for mission plan steps. The tree builder: - agents/core/todo_tree.py tree() (agents/core/todo_tree.py:48) returns (item, depth) pairs in depth-first order, children in list order under their parent, with every item exactly once. It is stdlib only, because the CLI imports it. - Depth is capped at MAX_DEPTH = 4 (agents/core/todo_tree.py:30); a deeper item is drawn at depth 4, under its parent. - An item whose parent is missing, itself, or not an id at all is drawn at depth 0 in its place (agents/core/todo_tree.py:65). ref() accepts only text or whole numbers as ids; a bool, a fractional float, a list or a dict is no id, and a whole-valued float is its number, as JSON's 1.0 is 1 to the HUD (agents/core/todo_tree.py:33). When two items share an id, the first holds it. Anything that is not a list is an empty tree. - Cycle members, and anything under them, are reached from no root, so they are appended flat at depth 0 in list order (agents/core/todo_tree.py:78). A getter that raises counts as no parent. The walk uses an explicit stack, so a 5,000-item chain costs no recursion. - The HUD's twin is frontend/src/todo-tree.ts todoTree() (frontend/src/todo-tree.ts:28), with the same rules. Both suites read one shared file of 47 cases, frontend/src/test/todo-tree-cases.json (hand-written shapes plus seeded random ones over text, number, whole and fractional float, bool, null, list and dict ids and parents), and assert the same drawn order and depths. The review's fuzz had found the twins differing on whole-valued floats; that is fixed and pinned. The todo tool: - An item may carry parent: the id of the item it sits under. It is an allowed item field (agents/core/todo_tool.py:84) and is in the schema as a string of at most MAX_ID (agents/core/todo_tool.py:102); the description tells the model how to nest and un-nest. - _clean_parent (agents/core/todo_tool.py:197) cleans it like an id. Empty or whitespace-only text means no parent, as Hermes clears it. Anything else that is not an id is refused as todo_bad_parent, with a sentence the model can act on, and changes nothing. - A parent need not name an item in the list: a dangling, self or cyclic parent is stored and drawn at the top, never refused and never lost. - On a merge, parent null means not sent (as for content and status), and "" clears it. A changed parent counts as a write, so the plan cap applies. A parent moved by an untrusted turn taints the item, as a status does. Re-sending the same parent changes nothing: no taint, and the plan's labels stay (agents/core/todo_tool.py:400). - model_items answers the parent only when it is set (agents/core/todo_tool.py:227), so a flat list answers exactly as before and plan_bytes counts a parent's bytes. Mission plan steps: - MissionStore.create (agents/core/autonomy/missions.py:203) gives every step a nullable parent. _plan_step (agents/core/autonomy/missions.py:390) reads each entry. - A title, or any other value, is a top-level step with its own text, as before; a mapping without title or parent is still stringified. - {title, parent?} may sit under an earlier step: parent is that step's index, and only an earlier one. A plan is therefore a forest by construction. - Anything else is refused as bad_plan_step: a self, later, out-of-range, negative, bool, text or float parent, a missing or blank title, or an unknown key. - POST /api/missions (agents/core/routers/missions.py:50) accepts such a plan. A bad step answers 400 with one fixed sentence and code bad_plan_step (agents/core/routers/missions.py:73); no request data reaches the body. - The build-queue plan kept out-of-range and self parents on mission steps and let the tree degrade them. The store refuses them instead: a mission plan is written once, by its creator, so a bad parent there is a caller's mistake worth a 400. The tree still degrades any step the store did not check, such as a row written before this change, which has no parent key. A legacy entry {title, \<other key\>} is now refused too; nothing in the repo sends one. - finish_step budget charging and the completion gates are unchanged. A child step is charged like any step, closing a child does not close its parent, and exhausting the budget still auto-fails the mission. Rendering: - nerva todo draws the tree through todo_tree, indented two spaces a level (agents/cli/nerva.py:1724-1727). - The Decision Inbox's plans in flight (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx) orders items through todoTree and indents each row 14 px a level, with data-depth (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx:78-93). ITEMS_SHOWN counts rows in tree order. - MissionCanvasPanel draws plan steps through todoTree, the tree keyed by each step's idx (frontend/src/panels/mission-canvas.tsx:248). The finish controls name the step's own idx, not its drawn position: the test clicks done in a nested row drawn third and asserts it posts that step's idx. Tests: - tests/test_h666_subtasks.py (39 cases): - the tree: DFS order, a child listed before its parent, the depth cap, dangling, self and cyclic parents, non-id parents, duplicate ids, a getter that raises, and a long chain; - todo parents: storage, merge, clearing, cleaning, refusal, the cap, labels, taint, the schema and the tool answer; - mission step parents in the store and through POST /api/missions, with the refusal cases, budget charging on a child step, and nerva todo indentation. - Vitest: frontend/src/test/todo-tree.test.ts (9, including the shared cases), plus one case each in the plans-in-flight and mission-canvas suites (indentation, dangling or cyclic parents kept in view, and the finish control's idx). - 43 mutants of the new guards were all killed (four survivors of the first run were killed by added cases). One unreachable guard, a placed-twice check in the walk, was removed. The review's 51 mutants left three survivors: two (a boolean read as an id in the TS twin, a finish control addressing its drawn position) are now killed, and the third (the tree keyed by position) is equivalent, since a step's idx is its position. Six mutants of the fix round's guards were all killed. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Image-turn confirmation review (2026-10-02): the agents/cli/nerva.py diff adds selection flags only to chat image turns, rejects them on text chat, validates optional bounded requirements and forwards explicit flags. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. Nous account collateral review (2026-10-02): compared the complete diff from 7a73bf03. Existing CLI command implementations are unchanged; only auth parser/dispatch registration is additive. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. Existing OAuth handler bodies are unchanged; four separate owner-only Nous routes and documentation are added. All pre-edit evidence hashes matched the prior checkpoint. | <a id="h667"></a>H667 | Never assume /tmp is real storage; prune only the cache you own | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Code execution no longer keeps its files on the system temp root. Where a Sandbox built without an explicit work_dir makes its run directory, first match wins: JARVIS_EXEC_TEMP_DIR in the process environment, then the owner's security.sandbox_temp_dir setting, then the managed cache \<data root\>/cache/exec (resolve_root, agents/core/exec_cache.py:149). A value that is not an absolute path, or cannot be read as one (a relative path, ~nosuchuser, a NUL byte), is skipped with a warning (_as_root, agents/core/exec_cache.py:117), and the setting refuses such a value on write, as it refuses an age outside 1-8760 hours (agents/core/settings_db.py:795); a choice that names the managed cache, however spelled (through a link or a '..'), is the managed cache, compared by identity (_same_dir, agents/core/exec_cache.py:138). Each run directory is an absolute private mkdtemp (mode 0700, named nerva-sandbox-\*) under a root made 0700 when this user owns it; a root that cannot be used gives the system temp directory for that run, with a warning, unmanaged and never pruned (new_work_dir, agents/core/exec_cache.py:177). The Sandbox takes it at construction (agents/core/sandbox.py:85); the orchestrator's one production sandbox (agents/core/orchestrator.py:644) passes no work_dir, so it gets the managed cache. A run directory removed under a live sandbox, or there without its lock or wider than 0700, is made again 0700 and its lock taken again before the next run, and the tool-RPC runtime asks for that before it makes its mailbox inside the directory (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:381) (ensure_work_dir, agents/core/sandbox.py:100). Docker bind-mounts it with --mount, CSV-quoted, so a ':', ',', '"' or line break in the data root is data (_bind_mount, agents/core/sandbox.py:29). A managed run directory's lock lives outside it, in \<root\>/.locks/\<name\>.lock, where code run in the directory cannot remove, replace or forge it: an flock held for the sandbox's lifetime, recording host, pid, whether the flock was taken, the kernel's boot id and the pid's start time, written under a private temporary name and renamed into place so it is never seen empty and a link planted at its name is never written through, in a .locks directory that is private and not a link, and released (closed, then removed, as Windows needs) when the sandbox is gone (release, agents/core/exec_cache.py:312; hold, agents/core/exec_cache.py:257). An hourly job (schedule_exec_cache_prune, agents/core/scheduler_service.py:205; run_exec_cache_prune, agents/core/scheduler_service.py:583) prunes the managed cache whatever root is chosen now, off the event loop, never raising into the scheduler (prune, agents/core/exec_cache.py:499). Each run directory is aged as one group: the newest date anywhere inside it, links never followed, dates the whole directory (_newest, agents/core/exec_cache.py:327), a file being dated by the older of its mtime and ctime so a date set in the future never keeps it forever (_date, agents/core/exec_cache.py:323). A directory is removed only when that is older than security.sandbox_temp_max_age_hours (72 by default, never under an hour; max_age_hours, agents/core/exec_cache.py:596), it is not the hub's own live sandbox, and its lock is not held: on the kernel that wrote it (same boot id), a record whose writer took the flock is held exactly while the flock is, so a crashed sandbox whose pid came back (the hub is pid 1 in a container) is free; a record written without a flock is held while its pid is alive with the same start time, asked of the process table on Windows (OpenProcess and GetExitCodeProcess, never os.kill, whose signal 0 is a console Ctrl+C there) (_pid_alive, agents/core/exec_cache.py:390); only a busy flock reads as held, and any other flock error (a filesystem that refuses flock) reads the record; a record from before a reboot of this host, or from before the flag existed, is free when the probe gets the flock; a pid is read only in the pid namespace that numbered it; another host's record, another namespace's, a record whose flock the probe could not test and one with a pid no hold writes are kept until the directory is ten times the age limit old; a lock that cannot be judged is kept and logged and the sweep goes on (_held_safely); a lock that cannot be opened reads as held, and anything but a regular file is no lock (_held, agents/core/exec_cache.py:407). It is claimed by a rename before it is deleted, so a directory that cannot be claimed is kept whole; one swapped for a link after the checks is only unlinked and not counted; an unfinished claim is finished by the next sweep, and a lock whose directory is gone is removed (another host's once the lock is ten times the limit old). Only nerva-sandbox-\* entries are considered, in a managed root that is a real directory (not a link or a Windows junction) owned by this user and not writable by others; otherwise the sweep is reported failed to the scheduler, as is an unreadable root (_root_problem, agents/core/exec_cache.py:477). A root the owner chose is never bulk-deleted. Backups leave out the cache and, directly under a root the owner chose inside the data root, the run directories exactly as mkdtemp names them; an owner folder that merely starts with the prefix is kept (agents/core/backup.py:405), and \`nerva config set security.sandbox_temp_dir\` says it needs a restart. tests/test_exec_cache.py (60 cases) covers the resolution order and the refused values, the relative data root, the sandbox's own directory, its mode and its lock outside it, the owner record without flock, another host, locks that are not files, the probe's descriptors, a sandbox that is gone, a directory removed under a live sandbox (host and Docker paths), group aging by the newest date and by the directory itself, a future date, the age setting and its floor, the live list, the owner-root exclusion, foreign entries and links, a root that is a link or writable by others, a directory swapped for a link, a claim that cannot be made or was left unfinished, orphan locks, an unreadable root, the hourly job (the owner's age, off the loop, never raising, the managed cache while a root is chosen), its registration, the backup and the restart note; tests/test_h667d_exec_cache_review.py (30 cases) covers the third review: refused flocks (ENOLCK, EOPNOTSUPP, EINVAL) and a busy one, owner folders named like the prefix and nested run files in backups, another kernel's record under a free flock, a reboot, a failed exit-code query, a cache that does not exist yet, an owner-root directory made again, a failed replace, a root we do not own, a record from before the flag, another pid namespace, odd pids and a lock that cannot be judged, staged records, an interrupt during hold, a first lock taken later, reparse tags and the label; tests/test_h667c_exec_cache_review.py (55 cases) covers the second review: Windows liveness through a faked process table (no such process, access denied, exited, a reused pid) and a dead sandbox's directory pruned there, the release order, a free flock against a live pid (this process, pid 1, the parent), a renamed host on the same kernel, a flock still held, a record without a flock (alive, reused), another host's record and orphan lock ageing out, the record's fields, the tool-RPC path's re-creation, a directory there without its lock, the managed cache through a link or a '..', control characters, the mount's quoting, the planted link, the private .locks, the backup, a real tree aged with the production date and the record written before the lock has its name. Test manual: PNB-167, PNB-168. The first adversarial review (0 major, 8 minor, 9 nits: a bad setting that stopped the hub's start, a relative data root, backups including the cache, an unreadable cache reported ok, a later choice stranding the cache, lock and mtime games from inside a run directory, no flock on network filesystems, test gaps) is answered in full; its 31 follow-up mutants are all caught but three equivalent ones. The second review (one major: on Windows os.kill(pid, 0) is a console Ctrl+C that never says dead, and an open lock cannot be removed, so the cache was never pruned there; four minors: the record overrode a working flock, the tool-RPC path made the run directory again without its mode and lock, the managed cache spelled another way took no lock, twelve mutant survivors; seven nits) is answered in full; its 36 mutants, all caught but two equivalent ones (a .locks mkdir mode the chmod repairs, samefile behind resolve), five after their cases were added (a probe that took no flock, the start tick's field, a wide .locks, the mailbox made after the repair, a file named like a run directory). The third review (no major; three minors: a filesystem that refuses flock read as busy, backups dropping any folder with the prefix, eight real survivors; eight nits) is answered in full; this round's 18 mutants were all caught but three equivalent ones, three after their cases were added (an odd pid ageing out, another host's pid never read, an owner folder under the chosen root). Not taken: the collision-safe task-id directory naming, which the rationale made conditional on persistent per-task sandboxes, which do not exist (mkdtemp names are unique). TMPDIR/TMP/TEMP still pass through to child processes (agents/core/environments/__init__.py:24-26); the sandbox's own files no longer depend on them. Known limits: Windows liveness is exercised here through a faked process table (the Windows lane runs after merge); on a filesystem whose flock is local to each host (SMB, NFS with local_lock), another host's record is kept by age alone; the settings label and PNB-167 say that JARVIS_EXEC_TEMP_DIR makes a root the owner keeps (never pruned) and that a pruned cache moves with the data root; a live sandbox's own directory is exempt as a whole, not aged file by file; each Sandbox keeps its directory until the prune (there is no close()). | <a id="h670"></a>H670 | The default identity prompt is a behavior contract, and it lives in one file | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The shared identity band exists. agents/_identity/IDENTITY.md is one behaviour contract: the reply-sizing rule (match length to the weight of the ask), earned depth, the named prohibitions (no filler, no restating the request, no re-summarizing, no narrating visible tool calls), the anti-sycophancy line ('agree because it is right, not because the owner said it') and 'say when you do not know'. Its reply rules govern the agent's own replies to the owner: text drafted in someone else's voice (an email, a post, a message written as the owner) keeps that voice, greetings and sign-offs included, and a persona's warmth or curiosity lives inside the answer (review-H670 m-5); its honesty rules hold for everything an agent writes, drafted text included (review-H670b m-1). Its leading maintainer note (stripped before the model sees it, _strip_maintainer_note, agents/core/agent.py:453) says never to add 'be targeted and efficient in your exploration', and why. The repo-root SOUL.md mirrors the text, so agents working on the repo read the contract the product ships; a test keeps the two equal. identity_path (agents/core/agent.py:438) resolves it as soul_path_for does a persona: the data home's souls/IDENTITY.local.md, then a gitignored agents/_identity/IDENTITY.local.md, then the shipped file. read_identity (agents/core/agent.py:481) runs it through the same H387 helpers as a SOUL (the injection scan on the uncapped body, then the cap), announces a flagged or truncated contract (its blocked and quarantined stubs name the contract, not a persona), caps it at its own 4,000 characters (IDENTITY_MAX_CHARS, never above the persona's cap, so the two bands stay within the persona cap plus 4,000), and reads it once per settled file signature for the whole process. An override that cannot be read (not UTF-8, a directory, no permission) is reported at ERROR and the shipped contract used instead, cached against the shipped file's signature so an edit of it is seen; a contract that cannot be located or read at all leaves the agent without it; none of these stops an agent from being built. Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) is the contract, then the agent's persona (either may be empty; an agent without a SOUL still gets the contract). Every place a system prompt is built uses it: Agent.process (agents/core/agent.py:1026) and the synthesis path in agent.py, and the orchestrator's two sites (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2342, agents/core/orchestrator.py:4066) through _system_prompt_of (agents/core/orchestrator.py:485). At a compaction boundary refresh_soul (agents/core/agent.py:730) re-reads the contract as it re-reads the persona (_refresh_identity, agents/core/agent.py:599): a few stats when unchanged; when the file that resolves is absent, vanishes before the read or cannot be read, the contract in force is kept, reported at the boundary as failed-open and warned once per episode (an override absent for an instant resolves to the shipped file for that boundary, as a persona's does); and a moved contract reported as rebuilt, so an edit reaches running agents without a restart. The contract in force at start is committed to the prompt VC (SoulVersionStore) as _identity (_record_identity_version, agents/core/orchestrator.py:467), a no-op when unchanged or empty, so its history is recorded and diffable through /api/admin/prompts/_identity/... beside the personas; what is served is the file (a rollback in the VC does not change it), and an edit picked up at a boundary is recorded at the next start. The prompt-size report counts it as a shared component every call pays (identity_component, agents/core/prompt_size.py:136), and the packaged build ships it through its agents/\*/\*.md glob (overrides, \*.local.md, never ship). IDENTITY.md and its .local overlay are in the H506 instruction-file class (INSTRUCTION_BASE_NAMES, agents/core/file_tools.py:259), so a file write to them asks as an instruction file; a test derives the class from every name the contract's and a persona's loaders can resolve. The data home's README lists souls/IDENTITY.local.md. Persona SOULs and Persona.prompt_block (agents/core/cognition/persona.py:142) stay the character layer on top. tests/test_identity_contract.py (18 cases) covers every rule's presence, the banned line's absence and the kept warning, the mirror, the scan verdict, every active agent's system prompt starting with the contract (and exactly contract then persona), an agent without a persona, the orchestrator's helper and all four sites, both overrides and their order, the scan and the cap on an override, an edit reaching a running agent at the boundary, the one-stat unchanged path, a failed re-read keeping the last-good contract, the version record (once, and a failing store never stopping a start) and one read shared across agents; tests/test_h670b_identity_review.py (21 cases) covers the first review: the instruction-file class, a contract absent or vanishing at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 or unreadable override, the contract's own cap, no empty version, the start-time record, the contract's scope, a mid-file comment, the prompt-size component, the ERROR on a flagged contract, the stubs' wording and CHT-117; tests/test_h670c_identity_review.py (14 cases) covers the second review: honesty unscoped, a shipped contract that stops being UTF-8, a vanish before the real read, the hub's start recording the contract, one warning per episode and a second one, a reader that raises, the fallback following a shipped edit, a directory override, a permission error at load and at a boundary, the fallback rules once when both bands are blocked, and the docs. The first review (one major: the contract was outside the instruction-file class; seven minors: a missing contract emptied at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 override stopped every agent, the two bands exceeded the cap, the VC's rollback and A/B claim, the contract's unscoped rules against drafting personas and trait directives, six untested survivors, CHT-117 naming a retired agent; six nits) is answered; its six real survivors are pinned. The feature's 17 mutants were caught but one equivalent; the first review round's 15 were caught, one only against a stand-in (the vanish case, now driven through the real read). The second review (no major; three minors: the scope lifted the honesty rules from drafted text, a shipped contract that stopped being UTF-8 emptied the contract at a boundary, three real survivors; eight nits) is answered; its 31 mutants' three real survivors are pinned, and this round's 10 were all caught, two after their cases were added (a reader that raises, a second warning episode). Known limits: the prompt-size report measures the shipped contract, not an override in force (as it measures shipped personas); PromptRefresh.text is still the persona alone; the contract's scan verdict is reported only in the log (a persona's also reaches GET /api/agents/{id}/soul); the data home's README is written once, so an existing install does not get its new row. Test manual: CHT-117; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md names the band. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Architecture citation review (2026-10-02): only the LLM video paragraph was corrected from declaration-only to current approved ToolRPC dispatch. Shared identity text, loader, prompt composition and cited identity behavior are unchanged; no status promotion. | <a id="h671"></a>H671 | A long-lived conversation knows what day it is now | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Durable session birth is resolved through an immutable, generation-checked continuation root. Explicit owner-created child sessions carry bounded retained context and root birth through multiple generations, text/stream/process entrypoints and restart without switching the shared default. SQLite atomically commits child identity, seed, lineage and initial clock; idempotent request receipts resolve retries. Corrupt newer snapshots, reused identities and failed recovery stop dispatch or writes instead of silently replacing context. Accepted history/tool compaction alone advances the durable rebuild clock through compare-and-swap; request snapshots preserve same-day prefix bytes, reject late inherited commits, and add the trusted current-date line only when rebuild and root-birth days differ. Production rendering resolves explicit TZ or existing local IANA discovery and uses historical/current DST rules with an explicit UTC offset. Unknown birth is omitted; invalid or unavailable zone discovery preserves truthful offset-only rendering rather than inventing an IANA name. Independent actual-route, Agent, restart, concurrency, cancellation, failed-resume and observe-only regressions support the full frozen clock contract. This is code-equivalence, not a live-provider claim; ordinary subsequent JSON persistence remains best effort and turn leases are process-local. H672 hangs the persona re-read on the same commit: \`_history_for_prompt\`'s \`publish()\` calls \`_refresh_souls_at_boundary\` after the clock CAS and only when the compressor reported a compression, so a refused \`commit_clock\` migrates nothing and an accepted one is followed by the refresh in that order (\`test_a_refused_clock_commit_migrates_nothing\`, \`test_an_accepted_clock_commit_is_followed_by_the_refresh_in_that_order\`); the re-read neither reads nor writes the clock, so the same-day prefix bytes and the current-date line are untouched by it. #1188's edit to \`orchestrator.py\` is the pairing store's registry path, nothing on this row. Local auxiliary diff review (2026-10-02,aa4865b5): the claimed behavior is outside the four changed producer methods. Parent AST comparison and re-read source/citations confirm unchanged mechanisms; the cited backend test modules pass in the frozen full run. Existing frontend evidence remains tied to unchanged source. No new capability or mutation claim is added. Session birth/clock behavior and its cited sources are unchanged; this adds no session semantics and preserves the existing status. Image-recovery collateral review (2026-10-02): changes to shared CLI/image-turn or HUD local-panel disclosure are outside this row's claimed symbols. Complete base evidence pins and unchanged text anchors verified; existing capability/status preserved. Claim-specific source inspection is recorded in the bounded image source review. Image-turn confirmation review (2026-10-02): the agents/cli/nerva.py diff adds selection flags only to chat image turns, rejects them on text chat, validates optional bounded requirements and forwards explicit flags. Other CLI command handlers, settings/job flag semantics, session/approval dispatch and command-tree structure are unchanged. This row had current hashes before the edit; source diff review and229 focused image/API/CLI tests preserve its existing verdict. Full milestone results are recorded separately. DeepInfra collateral review (2026-10-02): the providers registry diff adds only a vision/model-catalog/cloud profile with unknown data policy and no main-chat capability. Existing context-window, session-clock, compaction and reasoning-vocabulary implementations and declarations are unchanged. Every pre-edit evidence hash matched0e617d63; profile/role regressions pass. This preserves the existing verdict without expanding its claimed scope. The exact command-tree expectation includes auth, and existing CLI/fish regressions pass. | <a id="h674"></a>H674 | Compression cannot stall the user: bounded holds, inactivity deadlines and derived thresholds | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A compaction summary no longer holds the owner's turn past a short bound, and a silent summarizer is cut. Hermes keeps two clocks: how long the summary may make the user wait, and how long the summarizer may go quiet. Nerva awaited the strict-local summarizer inline with neither. Its only limit was the httpx read timeout (120 s for Ollama), a total-duration wait on a non-streaming call, and nothing told the owner. (1) Bounded hold. ContextCompressor takes a gate (agents/core/context_compressor.py:325, used at :596). The orchestrator builds it per turn (_summary_gate, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3776, passed at :3975) around SummaryHold (agents/core/compaction_hold.py:136). SummaryHold.wait (:146) waits at most memory.compression_max_turn_hold_seconds for the LLM summary (settings_db.py:276; default 10 s, 0 = never wait, clamped 0..120 by hold_seconds, compaction_hold.py:63). One summary per session is written at a time: a turn that finds one still in flight neither waits on it again nor starts a second. Past the hold the turn goes on without the summary, and compress marks the result summary_deferred (context_compressor.py:621). compact (:524) then sends the turns verbatim, their old images dropped, when they still fit the window under the owner's memory.compression_max_tokens; otherwise it sends the deterministic digest. The summary keeps being written. When it lands it seeds this session's iterative-merge prior (_ctx_summary_cache), but only if no newer turn has replaced the prior this turn started from, and never through the compaction-clock CAS: publish commits only what the prompt was built from, and never writes the digest over the pending summary (orchestrator.py:3999). A turn cancelled while it waits (Stop, a client gone) still leaves its summary for the next turn. A summary that fails later seeds nothing and is logged by name. aclose stops what is still being written within the task budget (orchestrator.py:4697; SummaryHold.aclose, compaction_hold.py:185). (2) The owner is told. record_turn_notice (agents/core/turn_notices.py:35; a per-turn sink collected like turn_approvals, one notice per code) carries DEFERRED_NOTICE (compaction_hold.py:38) out as notices on /chat (ChatResponse.notices, agents/web.py:1102, TurnNotice :1085) and on every /chat/stream end event, the failed one included (:1363, :1369). The HUD shows each notice as a system line under the reply (frontend/src/turn-notices.ts, app.tsx:352). The hub log says the summary was deferred and, later, that it landed. (3) Inactivity deadline. The summarizer now streams (stream_summary, compaction_hold.py:88, called at agents/core/llm/auxiliary_text.py:64). It is cut after memory.compression_summary_idle_seconds with nothing received (settings_db.py:277; default 60 s, up to 600, no "off": idle_seconds, compaction_hold.py:71). Any chunk counts as life: a visible token, and a reasoning chunk that shows no text, through the new on_activity callback on the Ollama and LM Studio streams (agents/core/llm/base.py:665, :907). A slow stream that keeps talking is never cut. A cut stream is cancelled and given CLOSE_GRACE (1 s) to close its HTTP response. A backend with no stream is awaited whole. A degraded reply ("⚠️ …", "\[backend error …\]") or an empty one is now a failure, and so the digest is used. Before, the non-streaming generate could hand an error text over as the summary. Tests: tests/test_h674_compaction_hold.py (52) covers both settings, the idle deadline (silent, slow-but-talking, token-only, thinking, degraded, no stream), the hold (in time, at the bound, single flight, zero, cancelled wait, late failure, failure within the hold, aclose), the compressor's deferral (digest, verbatim when it fits, owner's budget, covered with a prior), and the orchestrator: the turn going on at the hold, the late summary seeding the next turn, a newer prior never overwritten, no clock commit for the deferred summary, the idle setting reaching the summarizer, aclose. It also covers the notice on /chat and every stream end event, the collector unbound after /chat, and on_activity on both local streams. frontend src/test/turn-notices.test.ts covers the HUD. 72 mutants: 70 caught after nine survivors got cases. Two were equivalent. m23 (busy ignoring done()) is equivalent because the done callback always pops the flight first. The other was a redundant typeof check in the HUD helper, which was removed. The phase-2, H20.3 and compaction-policy suites pass unchanged. The AUD-7 and H677 end-event and reply pins gain the notices key. Local auxiliary integration review (2026-10-02): _compression_summarizer selects JARVIS_AUX_COMPRESSION_MODEL through generate_local_auxiliary, which still awaits compaction_hold.stream_summary inside the existing H513 scope. Idle normalization,activity,cancellation,hold,late seeding,unusable-answer rejection and deterministic fallback remain unchanged. A new cancelled-child physical-send regression supplements the existing H674 suite.
